- **Status:** DONE — 2026-10-31 (started 2026-10-26); owner Claude; baseline `27e2a6a`; contract: [SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md](SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md), section "Step interfaces v2 (TASK-088)". ### TASK-089 — Autopilot boundary v2 and sensor-error ownership

- **Status:** DONE — the Phase 1 part, closed 2026-11-05 on baseline 6d0c3c8 (started 2026-10-26); owner Claude; baseline `27e2a6a`; contract: [SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md](SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md), section "Sensor-error ownership (TASK-089)". Failure criterion: an error applied twice, or by nobody without a declaration, or ownership that rests on an unverified parameter, key or delivery path. Increment 1 is implemented: the pinned-source catalog, generated by `tests/fixtures/sitl/generate.py` from 13 files at `8ab6afde…` (323 SIM parameters, 36 JSON keys, 30 asserted source facts; `json-protocol.json` regenerates byte-identically); the 25-source ownership taxonomy with neutral values; matrix validation; and the neutralizing parameter profile. `tests/fixtures/sitl/ardupilot-catalog.json` joined Touched Files because the catalog is its own generated artifact. Findings from the pinned source: the IMU backend's accelerometer (0.01 m/s²) and gyro (radians(0.04)) noise floors are unconditional, so changing their owner needs a native firmware patch (BLOCKED_DECISION until the 2026-10-26 decision below); the browser HAL bypasses the aircraft model's magnetic anomaly and motor terms. Evidence on Node 24.22.1: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/error-ownership.test.ts` exit 0 with 11 tests. The TASK-059 profile holds exactly the native driver's experiment parameters, and the parity profiles are empty. With `tests/fixtures.test.ts` and `tests/ardupilot-json.test.ts`: 27 tests, exit 0. `npm run typecheck` and `npm run check:docs` exit 0; the changed files lint clean. Remaining: HAL payload v2 with byte fixtures and the TASK-046 browser check; oracle tests for optional JSON keys; airspeed and rangefinder sources; profile wiring in `external-sitl.ts`, `wasm-sitl.ts` and the native driver; and a TASK-047 parity rerun after any delivery change. Increment 2 follows the user's decision of 2026-10-26: ArduPilot defaults stay the default, and a setting switches off ArduPilot's fixed IMU noise floors. Increment 2 contents:
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Required (S03, S04, S08, S15). TASK-135 landed increment 1 of each; the remaining increments continue here.
  - `native/ardupilot/patches/imu-noise-floor.patch` (`7a43d2ed…`) adds `SIM_ACC_FLOOR` (0.01 m/s/s) and `SIM_GYR_FLOOR` (0.04 deg/s), whose defaults are the upstream constants.
  - Catalog schema 2 records the patch applied strictly to the pinned sources, plus 31 facts.
  - Ownership matrix schema 2 declares `firmwarePatches`, admitted on `native-json` only.
  - New code: `imuNoiseFloorMatrix`, `scripts/ownership-profile.mjs --imu-noise-floor ardupilot|off`, `scripts/build-ardupilot-sitl.mjs` and `scripts/check-imu-noise-floor.mjs`.

  These files, `native/ardupilot/patches/`, `tests/imu-noise-floor.test.ts` and the `test-results/validation/task-089-imu-floor-*.json` reports joined Touched Files to build, select and verify the patch. `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md` also joined, because they record the decision, as did `docs/operations/runbook.md` and `docs/ru/runbook.md` for the operator procedure.

  Evidence: the pinned ArduCopter SITL was built here (upstream ELF `b54bef09…`; patched ELF `06846b05…` built by the script with GCC 13.3.0) and flown with the unchanged driver on Node 24.22.1 and pymavlink 2.4.50.
  - With default parameters, the patched firmware matched upstream bit for bit over all 27,362 `HIGHRES_IMU` samples in that pair.
  - With the floors off, the disarmed residual standard deviation falls from 6.05e-4 m/s² and 4.78e-5 rad/s to at most 1.2e-6 m/s² and 8.1e-8 rad/s. The limits were set before measurement.
  - Upstream firmware given the `off` lines keeps its floors, because it skips unknown defaults silently. This negative control fails as it should.
  - Both patched flights pass the TASK-042 raw and flight audits.
  - `npx --no-install vitest run tests/error-ownership.test.ts tests/imu-noise-floor.test.ts tests/fixtures.test.ts tests/ardupilot-json.test.ts` exits 0 with 37 tests in 4 files, and the regenerated fixtures match their registered hashes.

  Increment 3 follows the user's decision of 2026-10-27: the native harness reads the setting, and the user accepts the resulting TASK-059 re-freeze.
  - `scripts/run-sitl.mjs` accepts `MIRAGE_SITL_IMU_NOISE_FLOOR` unset (`ardupilot`) or `off` and records it in its report.
  - With `off`, `scripts/sitl/driver.mjs` appends the validated profile to `quad.parm` and reads both parameters back through `scripts/sitl/telemetry.py`. It holds arming until PARAM_VALUE reports 0 for both and otherwise fails 20 s into the simulation. Default runs send nothing new.
  - `scripts/sitl/check-raw.py` includes the read-back PARAM_VALUE frames that `run.json` declares. The analyzer's `--reference` compares samples until the first driver command.

  These four scripts joined Touched Files to apply and audit the setting.

  Evidence (2026-10-27, `node scripts/run-sitl.mjs`):
  - Upstream with the default setting and patched with `off` both PASS.
  - The `off` flight reports `SIM_ACC_FLOOR 0` and `SIM_GYR_FLOOR 0`, arms at 42.25 s, and passes the floor analysis and the TASK-042 raw and flight audits.
  - Upstream with `off` fails closed at 20 s without arming.
  - Patched with defaults matches upstream on all 13,082 samples before the first command. MAVLink command timing differs by up to 52 ms between runs, so the earlier whole-flight identity is not guaranteed.
  - `npx --no-install vitest run tests/imu-noise-floor.test.ts tests/error-ownership.test.ts tests/vehicle-manifest.test.ts tests/evidence-tools.test.ts tests/sitl-flight-trace.test.ts tests/integration/ew-sitl.test.ts tests/sitl-ew.test.ts` exits 0 with 52 tests in 7 files.

  Remaining for the switch:
  - Apply the patch in the browser HAL build, with the TASK-046 checks.
  - Re-freeze TASK-059 with the current driver on the operator's machine; until then `report-ekf.mjs` rejects new EW validation captures.
  - Re-run `check-raw.py` on earlier TASK-042/043 captures to refresh their audits.
  Increment 4 closes the Phase 1 part on 2026-11-05. An audit of the remaining lists against the code found most of them done by later tasks or outside Phase 1, and one real hole: nothing stopped a live write from applying an owned error twice.
  - A live write is checked against the run's ownership matrix. `parameterWriteConflict` finds the error a SITL parameter sets, and `ParameterService` refuses before sending when MirageSITL applies that error or this run switches it off, so the Parameters tab's write, its sending of a set and its send-back from the history are all covered. An ArduPilot-owned error stays free to change. Against native ArduCopter 8ab6afde with Ideal sensors, `SIM_BARO_RND` is refused with its reason and ArduPilot keeps 0, and `RTL_ALT_M` is confirmed. Batches were already covered by their clash check against the generated parameters.
  - Every SIM_ parameter of the catalog is declared: 96 belong to an error source and 227 to one of eight declared classes, each with its reason (`SIM_PARAMETERS_WITHOUT_A_SOURCE`). A new name after a pin change fails a test, and so does an error active by default outside a source; none is.
  - The switch runs on macOS. `scripts/build-ardupilot-sitl.mjs` assumed Linux (GNU `cp --reflink`, an ELF check) and now builds natively, in 2 min 25 s with Apple clang 21. With `node scripts/run-sitl.mjs` on the M5, disarmed residuals are 6.05e-4 m/s² and 4.78e-5 rad/s on stock firmware, and 5.7e-11 m/s² and 8.07e-8 rad/s with `off`, inside the limits of 1e-5 and 1e-6 set earlier; `off` on stock firmware fails closed at 20 s without arming. Patched firmware on defaults matches stock on every sample before the first command in 5 of 5 further flights, 13,104 to 13,110 samples each. Of 12 flights compared with one stock flight, 2 took a different noise sequence with the same statistics, one of them the unchanged stock bytes at another path, so that is ArduPilot's startup and not the patch; its cause was not traced, and a comparison showing no identical samples should be flown again.

  Left out of Phase 1 on purpose, each with its reason:
  - HAL payload v2, the TASK-046 browser check, the patch in the browser HAL build and the TASK-047 parity rerun: browser-hosted ArduPilot is preserved and not expanded (scope section 2), and no HAL field changed here. They reopen with that path.
  - Airspeed and rangefinder sources: S08 does not include those sensors, and the S19 aircraft do not carry them. They are declared `not-fitted`.
  - Optional JSON keys: the line writes only `velocity_wind` and the battery section, and both have oracle tests (TASK-093, TASK-147).
  - Profile wiring: TASK-147 wired the Run tab and the native driver. Connect writes nothing to a SITL it does not own and checks by read-back, and `wasm-sitl.ts` is the browser path.
  - The TASK-059 re-freeze moves to TASK-171. The earlier TASK-042/043 audits are Linux and WSL captures, which are not Phase 1 evidence, and were not re-run.
  - Decision D-10 stands: Phase 1 runs stock firmware and leaves this switch unused.

  Regression: `tsc`, lint and `npm run build` exit 0; `npm test` gives 1736 passed, 1 skipped and 4 failed, the 4 being the known Mac difference in `tests/drive-chain.test.ts` and three native flights that overlapped others ("Arm: RC not found"), which pass alone; 26 browser suites in Chrome 156 give 49 passed, run together with TASK-179.
- **Title & Description:** Decide, per sensor and error source, whether MirageSITL or ArduPilot's SITL sensor backends add each error, and deliver the required fields through the JSON line, the browser HAL mailbox and MAVLink injection ([V2_PLAN.md](V2_PLAN.md) B1). Upstream firmware is the default. The one recorded firmware patch (decision 2026-10-26) only switches off ArduPilot's fixed IMU noise floors on request.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-041, TASK-046, TASK-047, TASK-059, TASK-087.

Only delivery paths with repository evidence are admissible: JSON keys covered by the TASK-041 oracle, HAL payload fields in `browser_hal.h`, and `GPS1_TYPE 14` MAVLink injection from TASK-059. ### TASK-090 — Fidelity evidence pipeline

- **Status:** IN_PROGRESS — 2026-10-26; owner Claude; baseline `27e2a6a`; contract: [SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md](SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md), section "Fidelity evidence pipeline (TASK-090)". Failure criterion: any path that turns synthetic or unhashed data into PASS, or a metric that disagrees with its analytic oracle. Increment 1 is implemented: RMS, bias and Theil metrics; a radix-2 FFT and Welch H1 response with coherence; the CIFER-style cost with W_γ = [1.58(1 − e^(−γ²))]², W_p = 0.01745 and the J ≤ 100 guideline as a ceiling; bench CSV v1 import to CT, CQ, CP and FM with ISA density correction and consistency flags; capture schema v1; and `scripts/check-fidelity.mjs` on the shared evidence helper. Evidence on Node 24.22.1: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/fidelity-metrics.test.ts tests/bench-import.test.ts` exit 0 with 20 tests. The oracles are a direct DFT (1e-12), a time-domain IIR filter against its analytic response (1e-12 relative), a pure delay (1e-9°), the coherence of a known signal-to-noise ratio (0.5 ± 0.03, a 5σ bound), analytic J for gain, phase and coherence-weighted mismatches (1e-9) and an analytic Theil coefficient. `node scripts/check-fidelity.mjs` exit 2 (BLOCKED_EXTERNAL). The analytic capture `validation/fidelity-synthetic-capture.json` (SHA-256 `74e2c16d…91b2a7`, with `validation/bench-synthetic.csv`, `191082c4…dbfbb0`) exits 2 with J = 19.950 = 20·W_γ(1). Malformed, unhashed or over-permissive variants exit 1. `tests/fixtures.test.ts`, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run check:docs` exit 0; the changed files lint clean. Remaining, and required for DONE (G4): one real ArduCopter SYSTEMID flight capture and one real thrust-stand file. Also remaining: DataFlash conversion to capture v1 and the simulation replay that produces the simulated series.
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Required (S03, S04, S08, S15). TASK-135 landed increment 1 of each; the remaining increments continue here.
- **Title & Description:** Score model changes against measured data: time-domain log-replay metrics, frequency-response identification from ArduCopter SYSTEMID chirps, and component bench data import ([V2_PLAN.md](V2_PLAN.md) B3), using the repository's evidence and exit-code conventions.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-033, TASK-087.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/physics/src/fidelity-metrics.ts`, `packages/physics/src/bench-import.ts`, `packages/physics/package.json`, `scripts/check-fidelity.mjs`, `tests/fidelity-metrics.test.ts`, `tests/bench-import.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/validation/fidelity-synthetic-capture.json`, `tests/fixtures/validation/bench-synthetic.csv`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/ru/architecture.md`, `docs/V2_PLAN.md`, `docs/ru/V2_PLAN.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** `scripts/check-fidelity.mjs --input PATH --sha256 HEX` (exit 0 PASS, 1 FAIL or invalid evidence, 2 BLOCKED_EXTERNAL); capture schema v1 with provenance; metrics: RMS, bias, Theil inequality coefficient, Welch H1 frequency response with coherence, cost `J = (20/n)·Σ W_γ·[(Δ|H|_dB)² + 0.01745·(Δ∠H_deg)²]` against a declared limit (guideline 100); bench CSV v1 to CT/CP versus RPM with density correction and consistency flags.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Synthetic oracles recover known transfer functions within declared tolerances and known mismatches give the analytic J; malformed or unhashed input fails; missing measured data reports BLOCKED_EXTERNAL, never PASS; at least one real flight capture and one real bench file are scored before the task is DONE (G4).

### TASK-091 — BET kernel performance headroom

- **Status:** IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline 58116ba; 2026-10-30. Registered 2026-10-26 from the physics fidelity review (baseline 4593971). Goal: cut the production bound-rotor step cost so that 16 rotors fit 800 and 1200 Hz and later fidelity fits the budget, with results unchanged within the tolerance below. Profile before any change (native release build, callgrind, one TASK-144 rotor: 16 strips, 8 azimuth samples, NACA6409 polar of 121 x 10 x 2 cells; 45 us per step at p50): 30% of the instructions re-validate the whole configuration on every step (`bound::config`, including `PolarTable::new`, which scans every coefficient), 45% are polar lookups (binary searches, natural logarithms of the Reynolds axis, the confidence corners and the trilinear sums), 9% `atan2` and 5% the `fmod` behind `rem_euclid`. Tolerances, declared before implementation. T1 (default path): against the kept reference kernel, every output of the production step (force, air moment, transmitted moment, shaft speed, torque, power, inflow and angular momentum) agrees within 1e-12 relative (vector components relative to the vector's norm, an absolute floor of 1e-15) with identical flags, over a declared state sweep (axial, edgewise up to an advance ratio of 0.3, body rates, cyclic pitch, descent, clamped and low-confidence polar queries, both drive modes), and shaft speed and inflow stay within 1e-9 relative over 1e5 closed-loop steps. T2: every TASK-033 suite passes unchanged, including the 18 `scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs` comparisons (0.1 m and 0.5 degrees against 4800 Hz), the native, scalar and SIMD128 tests and the G1 probes. An optional mode that changes the numerics (single-evaluation predictor, regime-dependent azimuth sampling) is off by default, is judged by T2 and sets a validity flag. Design of the default path: the configuration is validated once at initialization (the spec already makes it immutable) and each step rechecks only the shape that memory safety needs; polar brackets come from an O(1) guess on uniform axes, verified against the neighbours with the binary search as fallback, so the bracket and the weights stay bit-identical; the logarithms of the Reynolds axis are taken once per step; the azimuth wrap uses exact conditional subtraction instead of `fmod`; the element speed is computed once. The baseline is recorded before the kernel changes: `test-results/bench/bet-baseline.json` from the new `scripts/bench-bet.mjs` (WASM flight steps for 4, 8 and 16 rotors at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz) and `native/bet/examples/bench_bound.rs` (native rotor steps); the reference kernel stays callable so the native comparison runs both in one process. Failure criterion: an output outside T1 or T2, a new allocation, or a speed-up claimed from a different machine than its baseline. Touched Files additions, made before implementation: `native/bet/examples/bench_bound.rs`, `scripts/bench-bet.mjs` (a separate benchmark, so the acceptance checks in `scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs` keep their meaning) and `test-results/bench/bet-baseline.json`. Increment 1 (exact path), 2026-10-30: `step_config` replaces the per-step full validation, `PreparedPolar` and `Rotor::evaluate_fast` implement the design above, and `bound::step_reference` and `bound_v2::step_reference` keep the reference kernel callable; `native/bet/src/bound_v2.rs` and `native/bet/tests/support/neuralfoil.rs` (the NACA6409 fixture as native tables) joined Touched Files during implementation. Evidence: the production results are bitwise identical to the reference, stricter than T1. `cargo test --workspace --locked` exit 0, including `prepared_lookup_returns_the_reference_bits` (over 2,000,000 queries on NACA6409 with one and two Mach nodes, the synthetic table and an irregular alpha axis), `production_kernel_matches_the_reference_within_t1` (3,000 states, every one bitwise), `the_wrap_reproduces_rem_euclid_bits`, and the closed-loop comparisons (1e5 steps at 400 Hz and 2e4 at 1200 Hz in torque mode, 1e5 ESC-drive steps at 800 Hz with a power cut; every record bitwise). The wasm32 check exits 0; 52 focused Vitest tests pass; `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs` passes all 18 comparisons and `--allocation` passes G1 (0 GC events, at most 0.0049 B/step, stable memory). Speed against the baseline on the same machine (`node scripts/bench-bet.mjs --baseline test-results/bench/bet-baseline.json`, report `test-results/bench/bet.json`, Node v24.22.2, Intel Xeon at 2.80 GHz): WASM flight steps 1.2x to 1.7x faster in mean, typically 1.3x; native rotor step p50 34 us against 43 us. Octocopter at 1200 Hz: p99 0.88 ms (scalar) and 0.84 ms (SIMD128) against the 0.4167 ms G3 limit, so G3 and the 5x target are not met. The rest of the cost is the arithmetic (about 1,000 instructions per blade element, `atan2` and the interpolation chief among them); the next increment needs the stakeholder's choice between the opt-in numerics modes, modes on by default, or stopping here (asked 2026-10-30). Stakeholder decision of 2026-10-30: opt-in modes, off by default, so default flights keep today's results; then a rewrite within T1 if the target still needs it. Design of increment 2, recorded before implementation. Bound configuration version 2 (version 1 is unchanged and stays the default; the encoder writes version 2 only when a mode is on): header word 36 holds the kernel options, bit 1 the reduced predictor and bit 2 hover sampling, and any other bit is rejected; arena header bytes 56..63 hold the hover-sampling state (f64 0 or 1) only when hover sampling is on, and stay zero otherwise. Reduced predictor: the half-step prediction uses the azimuth samples 0 and N/2 (N even), whose first harmonics cancel; only its axial force and torque are used. Hover sampling: the midpoint evaluation uses every other azimuth sample (N even, at least 4) while the regime measure m = max(transverse air speed / tip speed, in-plane body rate / shaft speed, |cyclic pitch|) stays below 0.05, entered below 0.05 and left above 0.10 (hysteresis), with full sampling at a stopped shaft. Output flag 64 marks a step whose midpoint used hover sampling and flag 128 a reduced prediction; `BetFlightDynamics` excludes both from ModelOutsideValidity and reports them separately. The reference kernel ignores the options. Acceptance (T2 for the modes): the 18 TASK-033 comparisons pass with both modes on, and 6 added forward-flight trajectories (configurations 0 to 2, pilot pitch command, 400 and 1200 Hz, scalar) stay within 0.1 m and 0.5 degrees of the default kernel at the same rate over 10 s; G1 is unchanged; speed is reported with the modes off and on. Touched Files additions for increment 2: `packages/wasm-bet/src/rotor.ts`, `packages/wasm-bet/src/rotor-v2.ts`, `packages/physics/src/bet-dynamics.ts`, `packages/physics/src/profile-dynamics.ts`, `tests/bet-runtime-fixture.ts`, `tests/bet-runtime.test.ts`, `native/bet/include/bet_bound.h`. Increment 2, 2026-10-30. The modes are implemented as designed. The T1 rewrite (`Rotor::evaluate_sampled`: element velocities resolved on the tangential direction and the axis, where the cross products reduce to scalar products, with per-strip and per-azimuth factors and four sums per azimuth) runs only in configurations with options: as the default kernel it moved the TASK-092 torque-drive golden hash (`tests/drive-chain.test.ts`), so the default stays the bit-identical `evaluate_fast`, which also gained two bit-identical polar changes (the confidence value is tested before the corner weight, and one window holds a cell's corners). Evidence: `cargo test --workspace --locked` exit 0, with every default-path closed-loop record bitwise (120,000 torque-mode and 100,000 ESC-drive records), the opt-in kernel within T1 on all 3,000 blade states (84 of them bitwise), `configuration_version_two_carries_only_the_known_kernel_options`, `hover_sampling_follows_the_regime_with_hysteresis`, `opt_in_modes_stay_close_to_the_reference_in_hover_and_forward_flight` (thrust within 2.8e-15 in hover and 4.8e-4 at an advance ratio near 0.17; limits 5e-3, set before the run) and `opt_in_kernel_modes_allocate_zero_over_one_million_steps`. The wasm32 check, `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` exit 0; 53 focused Vitest tests pass, including the TASK-092 hash and the new kernel-option test in `tests/bet-runtime.test.ts`. `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs` passes 18/18; with `--kernel-options 3` the 18 comparisons pass and the six forward-flight trajectories stay within 0.080 m and 2.1e-3 rad of the full kernel (worst: configuration 0 at 400 Hz, 80% of the 0.1 m limit); `--allocation` passes G1 (0 GC events, at most 0.0049 B/step). Speed against the baseline on the same machine (Node v24.22.2, Intel Xeon at 2.80 GHz): without options (`test-results/bench/bet.json`) WASM flight steps are 1.1x to 1.5x faster in mean, and the native rotor step 0.9x to 1.3x at p50 (noisy on this shared host); with both modes (`test-results/bench/bet-modes.json`) WASM flight steps are 2.9x to 3.9x faster in mean and the native rotor step 3.2x to 3.3x at p50. Octocopter at 1200 Hz with both modes: p99 0.389 ms (scalar) and 0.336 ms (SIMD128), inside the 0.4167 ms G3 limit; without options 0.89 and 0.97 ms. Sixteen rotors at 1200 Hz with both modes: mean 0.37 ms against the 0.833 ms period. Remaining: the 5x target (about 3.5x with the modes, 1.3x without), SIMD128 across strips, and G3 on target hardware. `test-results/bench/bet-modes.json` joined Touched Files. Increment 3, recorded before implementation (2026-11-01, Claude, baseline e7d09a9). Machine: the stakeholder's Apple M5 MacBook Air (macOS 26.6, 10 cores, Node v24.22.1, Rust 1.97.0), shared during the work with another session's Chrome GPU load (load average 4 to 11), so speed is compared within one run or between runs taken back to back. Baseline on this machine: the 58116ba sources with the e7d09a9 `scripts/bench-bet.mjs` (its native example timing the production step only, as at 58116ba) give a native rotor-step p50 of 28.2 us and octocopter flight steps at 1200 Hz of p50 0.289 ms and p99 0.341 ms; the cloud baseline `test-results/bench/bet-baseline.json` stays as recorded. Profile (native release build, component timing, because no sampling profiler is installed): per blade element the reference kernel costs about 90 ns, `evaluate_fast` 55 ns and `evaluate_sampled` 46 ns; one prepared polar lookup alone costs 28 to 57 ns, `atan2` 7.5 ns, `ln` 3.8 ns and `sqrt` 1.1 ns, so the lookup is about two thirds of an element. A flight step also carries about 0.1 ms of work outside the rotor kernel (record transfer, body, contact and bus in `BetFlightDynamics`, outside this task's scope), so the 5x target is measured on the bound-rotor tick: `scripts/bench-bet.mjs` adds WASM rotor ticks (`BetRotorEngine.stepInto` of 8 and 16 rotors at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz, scalar and SIMD128) next to the flight steps, and the same script runs on the baseline sources. Design: the T1 kernel `Rotor::evaluate_sampled` is rewritten in three passes per azimuth sample: a branch-free pass over the strips that the compiler can vectorize (relative velocity, speed, angle of attack with the exact conditional wrap, Reynolds and Mach numbers and the Reynolds logarithm), a pass of scalar polar lookups (`PreparedPolar::sample`: the same brackets, the Reynolds bracket starting from the strip's bracket at the previous azimuth sample, weights by the same divisions, then the confidence corners and the trilinear sums), and a pass of force sums. `atan2` and `ln` become kernel functions with exact Taylor coefficients, so they vectorize: atan on |t| <= tan(pi/16) after reduction by multiples of pi/8 (11 terms), and ln through atanh((m - 1)/(m + 1)) with m in [sqrt(1/2), sqrt(2)) (11 terms); the Reynolds-axis logarithms use the same function. A non-finite intermediate or an angle outside the conditional wrap falls back to the previous T1 kernel, kept as `evaluate_resolved`. The bound configuration gains kernel option bit 4 (exact kernel, not combinable with bits 1 and 2), which runs `evaluate_fast`, bitwise equal to the reference. Version 1 configurations switch to the rewritten kernel only if the bound-rotor tick needs it for the 5x target (the stakeholder decision of 2026-10-30 above); the TASK-092 golden hash in `tests/drive-chain.test.ts` then stays unchanged and runs with bit 4. Tolerances, declared before implementation: T1 as declared above, unchanged, for every state of the sweep at stride 1, and also with 1, 3, 5 and 63 strips (the SIMD tails) in the native build and, through a feature-gated test export, in the scalar and SIMD128 WASM builds; T2; component accuracy against the platform functions over at least 1e6 arguments each: kernel atan2 within 2e-15 rad, kernel ln within 2e-15 x max(1, |ln x|); and, if version 1 changes kernel, the closed-loop T1 limits against the exact kernel (shaft speed and inflow within 1e-9 relative over 1e5 steps, outputs within T1 on every step) and the 4000-step torque-drive flight of `tests/drive-chain.test.ts` with each of its 16 recorded values within 1e-9 relative (absolute floor 1e-12) of the exact kernel's and the flags equal. The failure criterion above is unchanged. Touched Files additions for increment 3, made before implementation: `native/bet/src/test_exports.rs` and `tests/rotor-kernels.test.ts` (the WASM SIMD tails), `tests/drive-chain.test.ts` (bit 4 for the golden hash, only if version 1 changes kernel) and `test-results/bench/bet-baseline-m5.json` (the baseline on this machine). Increment 3, 2026-11-01. The rewritten kernel did not reach 5x with the modes off, so version 1 configurations now run it (the stakeholder decision of 2026-10-30) and option 4 keeps the exact kernel. The lookup is `KernelPolar::sample` (`polar.rs`), not a method of `PreparedPolar`, which the exact kernel keeps unchanged. Changes made during implementation, each kept within the declared tolerances: the Reynolds and two-node Mach weights multiply by interval reciprocals formed once per evaluation (a query clamped onto the last node keeps the weight 1); the corner scan is skipped once the evaluation has flagged a low-confidence corner, since it could only set that flag again (it helps only evaluations that touch such a corner, as the benchmark rotor does); the trilinear sums take Re and Mach first on both alpha nodes; clamps use comparisons instead of `f64::max` and `f64::min`, which lower to library calls with data-dependent branches on wasm32 (this alone took the 8-rotor SIMD128 tick in crossflow from 0.174 to 0.084 ms in a scratch A/B run); the regime measure of option 2 (three `hypot` library calls) is formed only with option 2; the series stop at t^19 and s^17, which the declared accuracy allows; and the strip pass is two loops. Correction to the declaration: the 0.1 ms of non-rotor work per flight step came from the cloud reports; on this machine the octocopter flight step is close to its 8-rotor tick (baseline p50 0.290 against 0.288 ms SIMD128), so both move together. Evidence on the M5 (Rust 1.97.0 in `native/`, Node v24.22.1). `cargo test --workspace --locked` in `native/`: exit 0, 103 tests; the same command from the repository root with rustup stable 1.99.1: exit 0, 103 tests; `cargo check -p mirage-bet --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --locked`: exit 0. T1: `production_kernel_matches_the_reference_within_t1` holds every state for 16, 1, 3, 5 and 63 strips (3,000 each, 58 to 125 of them bitwise per count) with the exact kernel bitwise on all; `kernel_atan2_and_ln_meet_their_declared_accuracy` finds worst errors of 4.4e-16 rad and 4.3e-16 (limits 2e-15) over 1,000,000 arguments each plus bit neighbours of the reduction edges; the closed loops keep shaft speed and inflow within 1e-9 and every output within T1 (100,000 steps at 400 Hz and 20,000 at 1200 Hz in torque mode, 100,000 ESC-drive steps with a power cut), and with option 4 every record is bitwise (100,000 and 20,000) and the ESC loop ends bitwise; `configuration_version_two_carries_only_the_known_kernel_options` accepts 4 alone and rejects 5, 6, 7, 8, 12 and bit 31. SIMD tails in WASM: `bet_test_kernel_t1` in `tests/rotor-kernels.test.ts` (scalar and SIMD128 test builds) holds T1 and equal flags for 1, 3, 5, 16 and 63 strips at 1, 3 and 8 azimuth samples, the largest ratio to the T1 bound being 8.1e-3 in both builds. `tests/drive-chain.test.ts`: the default kernel stays within the declared 1e-9 of the exact kernel over the 4000-step torque flight with equal flags. Its golden SHA-256 `0149633376…` does not hold on this machine at all: the untouched 58116ba tree gives `0319387af2d4…` here as well, so it is a platform difference of the Linux x86 recording, not of this change; the exact kernel reproduces the 58116ba value exactly. T2: `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs` exit 0, 18/18 comparisons, worst 3.5e-3 m (limit 0.1 m); with `--kernel-options 3` exit 0, 18/18 and the six forward-flight trajectories within 0.0803 m and 2.1e-3 rad of the full kernel (configuration 0 at 400 Hz again the worst case). G1: `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs --allocation` exit 0, status `pass`, native allocator 0 calls and 0 bytes over 1,000,000 steps with the positive control detected, and every WASM graph with 0 GC events, stable memory and at most 0.00444 B/step (the fixed window cost). `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` and `npm run check:docs` exit 0. `npm test`: 1053 passed, 6 failed and 6 skipped in 151 files; four `tests/documentation-policy.test.ts` failures came from editing the mirrors during the run and pass on rerun (6/6), `tests/model-validation.test.ts` read T2 and G1 reports made stale by later comment edits and passes (4/4) after both gates were rerun on the final sources (exit 0 each), and the remaining failure is the golden hash above. Speed (`node scripts/bench-bet.mjs` on the 58116ba sources, report `test-results/bench/bet-baseline-m5.json`, then `node scripts/bench-bet.mjs --baseline test-results/bench/bet-baseline-m5.json`, report `test-results/bench/bet.json`, run back to back with the same script; 20,000 timed steps after 20,000 warm-up steps per row; load average 1.2 to 4 at the end). Bound-rotor ticks, default kernel: 8 rotors p50 0.0746 ms and p99 0.0805 ms at 1200 Hz in SIMD128 against 0.288 and 0.301 ms (3.86x mean at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz), 0.0902 and 0.0965 ms in scalar against 0.286 and 0.298 ms (3.17x); 16 rotors p50 0.149 ms and p99 0.159 ms in SIMD128 (3.85x to 3.86x) and 0.180 and 0.192 ms in scalar (3.16x to 3.17x). Native rotor step p50 7.8 us against 28.1 us (3.6x). Flight steps 3.6x to 3.7x (SIMD128) and 3.1x to 3.2x (scalar) in mean; octocopter at 1200 Hz p50 0.078 ms and p99 0.084 ms (SIMD128), 0.094 and 0.100 ms (scalar); sixteen rotors 0.155 and 0.165 ms (SIMD128), 0.187 and 0.197 ms (scalar). With both modes (`--kernel-options 3`, report `test-results/bench/bet-modes.json`): rotor ticks 8.2x (SIMD128) and 6.9x to 7.0x (scalar), native 8.0x, flight steps 7.2x to 7.6x and 6.3x to 6.8x; octocopter at 1200 Hz p99 0.042 ms, sixteen rotors 0.083 ms (SIMD128). The rotor tick in crossflow and body rates (a scratch A/B run, not in the report) gains 3.4x in SIMD128 against 3.7x in still air. Verdicts: the default kernel is 3.2x to 3.9x faster, below the 5x target; with the stakeholder's opt-in modes the target is exceeded. Octocopter compute p99 in Node on this M5 is far inside 0.4167 ms, but G3 is judged in Chrome on the M5 with the TASK-073 tooling, not by this Node benchmark: BLOCKED_EXTERNAL here. `docs/benchmarks/schema.json` stays the `bench-physics` schema (`tests/step-metrics.test.ts` validates against it); the BET report is described in `scripts/bench-bet.mjs`. Remaining: 5x on the default kernel. The per-element cost is now about 29 ns natively, roughly half in the scalar polar-lookup pass and half in the vectorized strip pass; the next route is SIMD128 across strips in the lookup pass (brackets, weights and the trilinear sums on lanes, with the table reads as scalar gathers) or a configuration version that stores interleaved coefficient cells, which needs an ABI change. G3 in Chrome on the M5. Stakeholder decision of 2026-11-01: the modes stay opt-in, so 5x on the default kernel remains the target of the next increment.
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Required for real-time 16-motor runs (S10, S19): the review measured an octocopter tick at about 0.70 ms against the 0.4167 ms G3 compute limit at 1200 Hz.
- **Title & Description:** Cut the production rotor tick cost at least 5× with results unchanged within a pre-declared tolerance, so later fidelity fits the G3 budget.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-014, TASK-033.

## Phase 8: Core Flight Dynamics

Wire the kernels that already exist into the flight path and make the body, contact and wind consistent.

### TASK-092 — Electromechanical drive in the production step

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline d1aeaca; 2026-10-29 (browser WASM hover BLOCKED_EXTERNAL: no Emscripten toolchain and no local TASK-045 firmware artifact; thrust-stand curvature comparison reported for the user under SA-01, no data in the repository). Goal: replace the ideal torque-source ESC with the voltage-driven motor, ESC and shared battery bus, so that throttle-to-thrust curvature and battery sag come from the physics; TASK-144 builds on it (stakeholder decision of 2026-10-29: TASK-092 first). Design. The motor law is `i = (d·V_bus − k_e·Ω)/R` clamped to `[−I_brake, I_max]`, with k_e = k_t in SI. The electromagnetic torque is `k_t·i`, and a Coulomb no-load torque τ₀ = k_t·I₀ opposes rotation; at rest it holds the shaft up to τ₀, so it never drives it backwards. Inside the current limits the back-EMF term −(k_t²/R)·Ω enters the shaft's exact viscous solution as extra damping, so the electrical time constant does not limit the step; at a limit the torque is constant over the step. The shaft accepts a signed motor torque and still stops at zero speed. The ESC holds its target duty for its update period (one tick when the period is shorter), filters it with a first-order lag of `commandTauS`, and a power cut sets the current to zero (freewheel) and bypasses the lag. Bound-rotor input v2 drive mode 1 carries `esc_duty` and `bus_voltage` at their reserved offsets and the drive parameters in bytes 192–255, which drive mode 0 still requires to be zero. Mode 1 was rejected with −8 until now, so no valid record changes meaning. The duty, held target and hold timer live in arena header bytes 32–55, which v1 already requires to be zero, so an arena driven in mode 1 refuses torque steps until it is reset. Mode 1 fills the electrical outputs (motor and bus current, terminal power `i·(i·R + k_e·Ω)`, copper loss) and sets validity bit 1. The battery accepts a signed demand: regeneration charges it within the same current limit, and the charge stays at most 1. A new export `bet_bus_step` steps the shared bus from a 128-byte record. The bus couples with a one-tick lag: motors use the voltage the bus produced on the previous tick, and the bus then delivers the sum of their terminal powers, so the source, internal loss and delivered powers close exactly on every tick the bus is not power-limited. `BetFlightDynamics` takes an optional ESC drive (per-motor parameters and the battery); without it the torque path stays bitwise unchanged. It publishes bus voltage and current to the session, the HAL (sensor value 21) and the JSON battery section, and maps motor power-cut faults onto the physical path. The vehicle manifest v2 gains optional `motors[].noLoadCurrentA`, `escs[].updateRateHz` and `escs[].brakeCurrentLimitA`, which the cards supply (TASK-139). The ESC drive requires them, and existing manifests stay valid with the same bytes. `ardupilotParameterProfile` adds `BATT_MONITOR`, `MOT_BAT_VOLT_MAX`, `MOT_BAT_VOLT_MIN` from the battery and `MOT_THST_EXPO` fitted to a static thrust-versus-duty sweep; `BATT_CAPACITY` was dropped during implementation because no repository source evidences it. Failure criterion: energy that does not close on an unlimited tick, a torque path that is no longer bitwise identical, a power cut that keeps driving, an allocation in the step, or a drive input that is silently ignored. Thrust-stand data for the curvature comparison are not in the repository; under SA-01 MirageSITL sets no tolerance, so the curvature is reported for the user to judge (F-02). Evidence (2026-10-29, Linux container, Node v24.22.2, Rust 1.97.0). `cargo test --workspace --locked` in `native/`: exit 0, 84 tests, including `native/bet/tests/drive.rs` (6) and `native/bet/tests/bound_v2.rs` with the ESC and bus zero-allocation probes over 1,000,000 steps each; the v1 bound-rotor trajectory digest over 4000 steps (`1e5ec25d4196dd06`) is unchanged. `npx --no-install vitest run` of `tests/drive-chain.test.ts` (8), `tests/wasm-abi-v2.test.ts` (17), `tests/vehicle-manifest.test.ts` (24), `tests/wasm-hal.test.ts` (6) and the BET, session and motor-fault suites pass; the full `npm test` passes 933 tests (1 skipped) in 134 files, and `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build` and `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` (PASS) exit 0. `CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium npx --no-install playwright test`: 82 passed, 28 skipped (hardware GPU and firmware cases), after the WASM export list gained the two bus exports; a first run had also failed the two timing-sensitive `simulation.spec.ts` cases, which passed on rerun and in the full rerun. Oracles: native and WASM ESC and bus records agree within 1e-12; every tick of a 2 s climb at 0.8 and 1 s of braking descent closes the charge integral bit for bit, the pack voltage within 1e-12 and the bus power within 1e-9 relative, with up to 0.29 V of sag and 42 regenerating ticks; a cut motor carries 0 A and coasts down; the torque drive reproduces the SHA-256 `0149633376a2365bec389ffbb28dc261a8f93a128d8a0d7766aeabffe84ab26d` of 4000 state records computed with the d1aeaca code and its WASM build. Curvature (F-02, reported for the user under SA-01, no tolerance): the parity rotor on the synthetic 300 KV, 0.1 Ω drive at 16.8 V gives `MOT_THST_EXPO` e = 0.853 (RMS residual 0.006, nine samples over MOT_SPIN_MIN..MAX), and the same rotor on duty·0.2 N·m gives e = −0.252 (RMS 0.047). `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs --allocation`: exit 0, `test-results/validation/bet-runtime-allocation.json` status `pass`; it now also runs the native ESC and bus allocator tests and an ESC-drive graph (four spinning rotors on the synthetic pad, one partial power cut, shared bus) for 1,000,000 warmed steps at 0.0049 B/step (the fixed window cost), with no GC and stable memory, at about 0.47 ms per 400 Hz step. Its first run failed with 30 GCs (0.56 B/step): with other dynamics instances in the process, V8 boxed the doubles stored in the tagged class fields of the bus state, so they now live in `BetFlightDynamics.electrical`, which hot readers index. `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs`: exit 0, 18/18 convergence cases. `scripts/validate-models.mjs` now requires exactly the five measured allocation graphs; it had required three since TASK-033, although TASK-047 added a fourth, so any fresh allocation report read as a runtime failure. `node scripts/validate-models.mjs --out <scratch>` then gives numerical and runtime `pass`, propeller `failed` and NASA deferred, as before. Native ArduCopter: `node scripts/prepare-autopilot-parity.mjs --defaults <pinned copter.parm> --output <dir>`, then `MIRAGE_PARITY_DRIVE=esc node <dir>/native-driver.mjs` against the pinned stock ELF (SHA-256 `34d82699d815e75d4f39bdf065dd5ca1067c31c058a6bc8eaa028ac12efe75b9`, ArduPilot 8ab6afde): exit 0, status `captured`, capture SHA-256 `68cfd1df2262b904df8fed93bd9464d369221660e5ae0dd0e794e1f92f112f07` (earlier runs before comment and storage-only source changes gave the same flight). The second defaults file set `BATT_MONITOR 4`, `MOT_BAT_VOLT_MAX 16.8`, `MOT_BAT_VOLT_MIN 13.2` and `MOT_THST_EXPO 0.8525049284203131`. GUIDED takeoff to 5 m, hover between ticks 64000 and 72000 within −5.006 to −4.979 m down at 7.46–7.51 A and 16.26–16.27 V, landing and disarm. The autopilot's SYS_STATUS (118 messages, pymavlink 2.4.50) reported 16.27 V and 7.48 A in hover and never a negative current, although the bus current reached −0.30 A while braking. The rotor validity flags 4 and 8 (low-confidence polar corner, outside axial-inflow validity) of the synthetic rotor stay recorded, as in TASK-047. The TASK-047 parity reports become historical snapshots because their identity hashes `bet-dynamics.ts`, `wasm-sitl.ts` and the native rotor sources. Browser WASM hover: BLOCKED_EXTERNAL, no Emscripten toolchain and no TASK-045 firmware artifact in this environment; the HAL mapping is covered by `tests/wasm-hal.test.ts` only. Documentation: `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md` and its mirror, this roadmap, `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` with mirrors; `npm run check:docs` passes. Correction of 2026-11-04: TASK-197 replaces this task's one-tick lag with the iteration its contract allowed, because the lag flipped the voltage at high demand.

### TASK-197 — Battery bus and motors solved together in the flight step

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 9d49058; 2026-11-04 (registered the same day by the user's decision to fix the finding of TASK-177). Goal: the motors and the bus agree within each tick, and a pack at its limit holds the motors back, in a flight and in `thrustCapacity` alike. Failure criterion: a bus that turns round tick after tick, motors that draw more than the pack delivers, more thrust from a lower limit, a moved voltage at a point that was already stable, or an allocation in the step. Design: `bet_bound_bus_solve_v2` solves the loop before the rotors step, from each motor's filtered duty and starting speed, by a safeguarded Newton search; the contract is in the engine specification, "Solving the bus with the motors". Oracles set before the first run: the voltage's closed form, and the pack's own law at its limit. Measured on the example cards at 800 Hz: the quad at full duty swung 1.44 V and now 0.000001 V, at 15.2942 V against 15.2942 V by hand; 16 motors at full duty drew 4458.5 W from a pack that delivers 1818.8 W and gave 285.7 N, and now draw 1818.7 W and give 153.55 N; holding the quad's pack to 40 A raised its thrust to 86.11 N, and now lowers it to 48.97 N against 78.45 N; a flight at full command gives what `thrustCapacity` says within 0.01 N on all five S19 configurations; hard steps from rest to full to nothing turned the quad's voltage round 2907 times, and now 3; the motors' voltage and the pack's differ by at most 0.042 V on those steps, against 2.1 V. Points that were stable did not move: 16.549 V and 15.928 V on the quad at duty 0.5 and 0.8, before and after. The solver took at most 9 steps over 20 000 random mixes. G1: one million native solves of 16 motors with zero allocator calls, and `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs --allocation` measures 0.003 B/step and 0 GC events over one million ESC steps. 16 rotors at 800 Hz take a mean 0.193 ms and p99 0.223 ms of the 1.25 ms period (M5, Node 24.22.1). Real flights with native ArduCopter 8ab6afde: `node scripts/phase1/nominal-flight.mjs` passes every step on all five configurations at real time with hover unchanged, 16.64 V and 7.7 A on the quad and 15.72 V and 50.6 A on 16 motors, so the TASK-158 reports and matrix cells are refreshed; its stock-settings negative control still fails as it should, at 59.26 deg/s of roll against 60.44 before, so that oscillation is not the bus. A climb from 10 to 16 m on the quad peaks at 13.06 A on the example pack, and at 9.00 A on one limited to 9 A, whose rise is 2.86 s against 2.67 s, overshoot 10.4 % against 0 and settling 5.92 s against 4.21 s; shown and not judged (SA-01). A known limit: the half tick between the solve and the motors' power leaves a pack whose motors are spinning up slightly under its limit, 19.98 of 20 A in one test, and that goes once the speeds settle. Regression: `cargo test --workspace` gives 113 passed and 0 failed; `tsc`, lint and `npm run build` exit 0; `npm test` gives 1719 passed, 1 skipped and 3 failed, the 3 being the known Mac difference in `tests/drive-chain.test.ts` and two native flights that overlapped others ("Arm: RC not found"), and those flight files pass alone, 25 flights in all; the 22 browser suites that touch these tabs give 41 passed and 1 failed, the power panel test, whose exact 2 A now reads 1.99998 A and whose 10 W rating only the old draw past the limit could exceed, since that pack delivers 8.4 W per motor; on the contract above, with a 5 W rating, all 42 pass.
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Required (S06): a voltage sag and a pack limit that do not reach the motors make every high-demand result wrong, and on 16 motors the swing began just above hover.
- **Title & Description:** Solve the shared bus and the ESC motors together within each flight step, so the one-tick lag of TASK-092 no longer flips the voltage at high demand, and feed a pack's limit back to the motors as TASK-026 left open.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-092, TASK-146, TASK-177.
- **Touched Files:** `native/bet/src/bus_solve.rs`, `native/bet/src/battery.rs`, `native/bet/src/motor.rs`, `native/bet/src/bound_v2.rs`, `native/bet/src/bus_abi.rs`, `native/bet/src/lib.rs`, `native/bet/include/bet_bound.h`, `native/bet/tests/bus_solve.rs`, `native/bet/tests/allocation.rs`, `packages/wasm-bet/src/rotor-v2.ts`, `packages/physics/src/bet-dynamics.ts`, `packages/physics/src/thrust-capacity.ts`, `scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs`, `tests/bus-coupling.test.ts`, `tests/thrust-capacity.test.ts`, `tests/limit-monitor.test.ts`, `tests/wasm-abi-v2.test.ts`, `tests/browser/native-bootstrap.spec.ts`, `tests/browser/power-panel.spec.ts`, `tests/integration/flight-tasks-sitl.test.ts`, `tests/support/quad-profile.ts`, `tests/fixtures/wasm-abi/generate.py`, `tests/fixtures/wasm-abi/interface-v2.json`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `docs/validation/phase1-matrix.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/quad.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/quad-stock-settings.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/hexa.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/octo.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/x8.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/octo-paired-16.json`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PHASE1_PLAN.md`, `docs/ru/PHASE1_PLAN.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** `bet_bound_bus_solve_v2`, `bet_bus_solve_version` and the shared bus solution record, additive; drive mode 1 records, bus records and `bet_bus_step` keep their bytes. `BetRotorEngineV2.solveBusInto` is allocation-free. A pack at its limit holds its voltage and current, and the voltage the ESCs multiply falls by one shared factor.
