### TASK-093 — Weather and atmosphere plumbing

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 7ea5fb5; 2026-11-03 (registered 2026-10-26 from the physics fidelity review, baseline 4593971). Goal: wind and atmospheric state as real inputs of the BET flight path through `weather.set`. Failure criterion: a weather that applies on another tick, air that differs from the ICAO troposphere, rotors that see the ground velocity instead of the velocity relative to the air, a gust that depends on wall pacing, or an allocation in the tick. Design: `weather.set` keeps its protocol v2 fields; `temperatureK` and `pressurePa` are the air at the session origin, and `airAtOrigin` maps the contract's sea-level pressure, temperature offset and the origin's elevation onto it, so that every tick holds the contract formula (TASK-148 supplies the elevation and a measured QNH). The session copies a due command into preallocated slots, and `BetFlightDynamics.setWeather` applies it at the tick boundary: a uniform mean wind with the seeded TASK-032 gust at 1 Hz at the CG and every rotor hub, and the ISA air at the CG's height, which the rotors read every step and the session publishes as `ambientPressurePa`; `trueAirspeedMps` is relative to the air, and the SITL link writes `velocity_wind` (no `airspeed` until the TASK-089 oracle covers that key). Weather outside the model envelope is refused with OUT_OF_RANGE at its tick and faults the session, a vehicle that leaves the air envelope faults the dynamics, and every new epoch starts without weather, bit for bit as before. The registered `packages/protocol/src/control-v2.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/external-sitl.ts` and `tests/session-v2.test.ts` were not needed: the v2 fields carry the weather (TASK-166 aligns the admission ranges with the model envelope), the TASK-041 encoder already writes `velocity_wind`, and the session tests are in `tests/weather-plumbing.test.ts`. The gust's gain moved to a typed slot and `SeededRandom.resetFrom` reads a seed from a slot, because the first measurement found 16 to 32 bytes per weather application from V8 boxing numbers (a double object field, and a seed above 2^31 passed as an argument). Evidence (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1): `tests/atmosphere.test.ts` 8 passed (the ISA within 1e-9 relative of its defining equations written independently over 200 combinations of reference air and height, the standard tables at their printed precision, `airAtOrigin` against the contract formula, `Wind.configure` against a new gust); `tests/weather-plumbing.test.ts` 7 passed (the exact tick in four epochs at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz, the refusal and fault, bit-for-bit replay under fine, 10 ms and irregular wall pacing, Galilean invariance within 1e-9 over 400 steps, the downwind drift, the reset); `tests/external-sitl-session.test.ts` 5 passed (`velocity_wind` from the weather's tick); with `tests/fixtures.test.ts`, `tests/bet-runtime-graphs.test.ts`, `tests/session-v2.test.ts`, `tests/bet-runtime.test.ts`, `tests/control-v2.test.ts` and `tests/scenario-runner.test.ts`, 9 files and 144 tests passed. `node scripts/bench-wind.mjs` reproduces its tracked report (0.000128 B/step, no GC, the same last wind after 9,000,000 steps). `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs --allocation` passed: weather on the synthetic pad 0.0031 B/step over 1,000,000 steps with 1000 weather applications after 20,000 warm-up applications; the real session -0.0005 B/tick (net of 516,544 B in the empty windows) over 1,000,000 ticks in 250 windows with 750 scheduled `weather.set` applied after 10,000 warm-up applications; no GC event in either, every other graph as before, the positive control detected, the native allocator at 0 calls. A first session measurement that forced a full GC before every window averaged 44 B per tick: in the check's process, after its other graphs, the GC collected applied commands' shapes and V8 discarded the optimized tick (`--trace-deopt`: embedded weak objects cleared), which boxed numbers until recompiled; without that GC, and in a fresh process even with it, every window measured 0. A weather applied only a few times runs in V8's first tiers, which box numbers (about 280 B per application over the first hundred). Gates: `npx tsc --noEmit` 0; `npm run lint` 0; `npm run build` 0 (only the existing chunk-size warning); `npm run check:docs` passed; `npm test` on a quiet machine 1364 passed and 6 failed, all known: the TASK-092 golden hash of `tests/drive-chain.test.ts` (`0319387a…` here, a platform difference that also fails on the untouched tree), two files that read a WASM module while another test file was rebuilding it (`tests/flight-contact.test.ts`, `tests/model-validation.test.ts`, the build race; both pass alone), and `tests/documentation-policy.test.ts`, run before this record's hash refresh, which passes after it.
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Required (S07): weather and atmosphere in sessions.
- **Title & Description:** Make wind and atmospheric state real inputs of every flight path: implement `weather.set`, recompute ISA with temperature offset and QNH every tick, and evaluate wind at the CG and each rotor hub.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-010, TASK-032, TASK-087.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/physics/src/atmosphere.ts`, `packages/physics/src/wind.ts`, `packages/physics/src/bet-dynamics.ts`, `packages/physics/src/session-v2.ts`, `packages/test-support/src/prng.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/sitl-link.ts`, `apps/web/src/physics.worker.ts`, `scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs`, `scripts/bet-runtime-graphs.ts`, `scripts/validate-models.mjs`, `tests/atmosphere.test.ts`, `tests/weather-plumbing.test.ts`, `tests/external-sitl-session.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures.test.ts`, `tests/bet-runtime-graphs.test.ts`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/SPECS_SHARED_MEMORY.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SHARED_MEMORY.md`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/ru/architecture.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** `weather.set` applied at tick boundaries with acknowledgements instead of `unsupportedReason`; `T = T₀ + ΔT − L·h`, `p = p₀·(1 − L·h/T₀′)^(g/(R·L))`, `ρ = p/(R·T)`; `BetFlightDynamics` reads density, viscosity and speed of sound per tick; true airspeed relative to the air; `velocity_wind` in the JSON line. At the pinned commit `SIM_JSON` derives airspeed from ground velocity unless the JSON line carries `airspeed`, and applies `velocity_wind` only after that, so the native path also sends `airspeed` once the TASK-089 oracle covers that key.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Scheduled weather changes apply on the exact tick in every epoch; ISA references within 1e-9 relative; constant-wind flight shows the expected ground-speed offset and airspeed; replay is seed- and FPS-independent; no allocation in the tick.

### TASK-094 — Rigid-body core v2

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 2e0960e; 2026-10-31. Registered 2026-10-26 from the physics fidelity review (baseline 4593971). Goal: the Phase 1 part of this task (S03, S18): CG offsets in the body step and the full inertia tensor in Rapier-owned sessions. Scope by the Phase 1 dispositions: the gravity model and Earth-rotation terms stay with TASK-108 (delivered by TASK-148), and mass-property rates with TASK-119 (deferred), so interface v2 keeps those fields fail-closed (−8). The rotor-coupling error budget against the 4800 Hz reference is not part of the DoD and is not done here. Design. The body state stays the CG state, as in v1 and as `inertia_cg` names it. `cg_offset_body` r_c points from the body reference point O, about which the wrench moment is given, to the CG. The step uses M_cg = M_O − r_c × F; gravity acts at the CG. This is the CG form of the contract's equations about O: with I_O = I_cg + m(|r_c|²·1 − r_c·r_cᵀ), both give the same motion, which a test checks by solving the coupled equations about O. A zero offset, −0 included, skips the transfer, so output bytes 0..127 stay equal to v1 bit for bit. Output bit 2 (angular acceleration, I⁻¹(M_cg − ω × I·ω) at the end state) and bit 4 (reference position p_O = p_cg − R·r_c) become valid on every step. Rapier: contact configuration version 2 puts the products of inertia I_xy, I_xz, I_yz (tensor entries, the convention of `inertia_cg`) in slots 28..30, which version 1 requires to be zero. The airframe then gets `MassProperties::with_inertia_matrix`, which diagonalizes the tensor into a principal-axis frame. Version 1 configurations keep the old code path bit for bit. `GroundContact` writes version 2 only for a non-diagonal tensor. Tolerances, set before implementation. (1) A CG offset under a body-fixed force through O at rest, without gravity: exact single-axis rotation θ = αt²/2, with angle and rate within 1e-9 after 1 s at 400 Hz, and the reference position within 1e-12 of p_cg − R·r_c. (2) Torque-free precession of an axisymmetric body, and the same body with its principal frame rotated so that the body tensor has products of inertia: body rates against the closed form within 1e-9 relative after 2 s at 400 Hz, and world angular momentum conserved within 1e-10 relative. (3) The angular-acceleration output against I⁻¹(M_cg − ω × I·ω) computed in the test, within 1e-12 relative. (4) The step matches the coupled equations about O solved as a 6×6 system, within 1e-12 relative. (5) Zero offset bitwise equal to v1 over 2,000 steps. (6) Rapier against RK4, for a free body with products of inertia and ω = (3, 7, 2) rad/s: rates within 2 % and attitude within 0.02 rad after 1 s at 400 Hz; the error at 1200 Hz at most 0.6 of the 400 Hz error (first order); and the same run without the products at least five times further from RK4, so the products are shown to act. (7) G1 zero allocation at 10^6 body v2 steps with an offset, natively and in WASM, and for contact sessions with a full tensor. Failure criterion: any tolerance missed, a v1 byte change, or an allocation. Touched Files additions, made before implementation: `native/bet/tests/body_abi_v2.rs` and the interface-v2 fixture generator, fixture, registry entry, parity test and reference example, because the CG offset leaves the fail-closed set. Also fixed while running the contact tests: `native_abi_rejects_invalid_frames_and_preserves_single_integration` failed whenever it ran after a per-tick-plane test, because the process-global input still held plane mode 1; it now zeroes the input first. Evidence. Native (`cargo test --workspace --locked` in `native/`): 100 tests in 24 binaries pass. `body_abi_v2` has 7 tests: the exact single-axis rotation, the 6×6 check about O (worst relative difference below 1e-12 over 400 steps), −0 neutrality, the neutral-record v1 equality over 2,000 steps with the new outputs, the rejection table without the CG-offset cases, and zero allocator calls and bytes over 10^6 steps with and without an offset. `rigid_body` has 6 tests: precession of the axisymmetric body and of the same body with its principal frame turned 0.7 rad about (1, 2, 3), with rates and world angular momentum inside 1e-9 and 1e-10; a Python RK4 run before the tests gave 7.2e-13 and 3.5e-13 for these parameters. The angular acceleration solves Euler's equations by substitution. A first run of the rotated case failed because the kernel accepts only an exactly symmetric tensor, by design, and the test built it entry by entry; the test now mirrors the upper triangle. Contact (`node scripts/build-contact.mjs --native --test-native`): 6 tests pass. Against the 4800 Hz RK4 body after 1 s, the rate error is 1.154e-3 and the attitude error 1.137e-3 rad at 400 Hz, and the rate error is 3.847e-4 at 1200 Hz, a ratio of 0.33. Without the products the rate error is 1.153, a thousand times larger. Version 2 rejects an indefinite tensor and a non-zero slot 31, and version 1 rejects products. TS (`npx --no-install vitest run tests/rigid-body-v2.test.ts tests/wasm-abi-v2.test.ts tests/fixtures.test.ts tests/contact-binding.test.ts tests/terrain-contact.test.ts`): all pass. The WASM adapter repeats the exact rotation and the v1 bit equality, runs 10^6 offset steps under WASM's trapping allocator, and steps a full-tensor contact session 200,000 times with zero counted allocations. The WASM body matches the native reference within 1e-12 relative for the new CG-offset golden record. `tests/fixtures/wasm-abi/generate.py` regenerated `interface-v2.json`: only the CG-offset rejection was removed and the offset record added, `bet-abi-v1.json` is unchanged, and the registry hash and derivation are updated. `native/bet/examples/interface_v2_reference.rs` needed no change. Planted faults, each reverted, are caught: the moment transfer's sign, an unrotated reference offset, the angular acceleration taken at the start state, and Rapier given only the diagonal. Found while testing, for TASK-095: a contact session that travels tens of metres, such as a tumbling body under body-fixed thrust, allocates in Rapier's broad phase after about 80 m and faults with −6, with or without products of inertia; a session held in place runs 200,000 steps without an allocation. Full gates: `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` 1005 passed and 1 skipped, the four documentation-policy failures being the stale mirror hashes before the refresh; `npm run build` exits 0; the browser suite gives 86 passed and 28 skipped with no failure. Not done here, owned by other tasks: Earth-rotation terms and gravity models (TASK-108, delivered in Phase 1 by TASK-148), mass-property rates (TASK-119, deferred), and the rotor-coupling error budget against 4800 Hz, which the DoD does not require.
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Required (S03, S18): CG offsets and the full inertia tensor, and the base of TASK-095.
- **Title & Description:** Generalize the 6-DOF core for CG offsets, time-varying mass properties and optional Earth-rotation terms, and make Rapier-owned sessions keep the full inertia tensor.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-028, TASK-029, TASK-088.
- **Touched Files:** `native/bet/src/rigid_body.rs`, `native/bet/src/body_abi_v2.rs`, `native/bet/tests/rigid_body.rs`, `packages/wasm-bet/src/body-v2.ts`, `native/contact/src/contact.rs`, `native/contact/tests/contact.rs`, `packages/physics/src/ground-contact.ts`, `tests/rigid-body-v2.test.ts`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/ru/architecture.md`, `native/bet/tests/body_abi_v2.rs`, `tests/fixtures/wasm-abi/generate.py`, `tests/fixtures/wasm-abi/interface-v2.json`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `tests/wasm-abi-v2.test.ts`, `native/bet/examples/interface_v2_reference.rs`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Equations of motion about a body reference point with CG offset: `m·(a_O + ω̇×r_c + ω×(ω×r_c)) = F` and `I_O·ω̇ + İ_O·ω + ω×(I_O·ω) + m·r_c×a_O = M_O`; optional `v̇ = R·f + g(φ,h) − (2·ω_ie + ω_en)×v`; documented rotor-coupling error budget against the 4800 Hz reference with an optional two-evaluation variant; Rapier mass properties in a principal-axis frame.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Analytic CG-offset, torque-free precession and inertia-coupling fixtures pass within declared tolerances; zero-offset v2 matches v1 bitwise; Rapier sessions with products of inertia match RK4 within the declared budget; G1 gates pass.

### TASK-095 — Compliant ground contact in flight sessions

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 42a3db1; 2026-11-03 (browser-hosted ArduCopter not run: TASK-045's disposition keeps it out of Phase 1, and the reference Mac has no Emscripten toolchain or local TASK-045 firmware artifact; terrain planes in sessions are TASK-187). Registered 2026-10-26 from the physics fidelity review (baseline 4593971). Goal: compliant Rapier contact as the integration owner of flight sessions, replacing the attitude-resetting synthetic pad and the inelastic floor. Plan, in increments: (1) contact sessions that travel keep the allocation gate; (2) implicit strut and tire spring-dampers with effective stiffness k + c/dt, static and Coulomb friction, and the brush tire model; (3) `GroundContact` as the body integrator of `BetFlightDynamics`, with a synchronous `GroundContact.reset` that re-initializes the same instance; (4) a compound hull (capsules, boxes, skids) and propeller-strike events; (5) ArduCopter takeoff and landing on the native path (`scripts/sitl/quad.mjs`), and on the browser path once its WASM firmware artifact is available. Requirement for (2), declared before measuring: in the drop fixture the peak airframe acceleration at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz stays within 5 % of the three rates' mean. Increment 1, done. A native test that lets a tumbling body under body-fixed thrust travel more than 1 km failed: Rapier's continuous collision detection runs on its own for any fast dynamic body, and `CCDSolver::solve_continuous` builds temporary vectors, so the session faulted with −6 after about 80 m, with or without products of inertia. A native trace build (`MIRAGE_CONTACT_TRACE=1`) now prints the stack of the first allocation inside `contact_step` and aborts. It and the WASM trace now stop only inside a step, not at the initialization probe. Fix: `max_ccd_substeps = 0`. Every ground is a halfspace, which yields contact at any penetration depth, so nothing can tunnel through it and CCD adds nothing. Baseline measured for increment 2: the existing explicit strut and tire forces give rate-dependent drop peaks, 38.7, 34.1 and 32.6 g at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz from 0.5 m and 74.7, 66.2 and 62.5 g from 2 m (synthetic four-wheel gear of `tests/contact-support.ts`, 1.5 kg), so the requirement fails today. Evidence for increment 1: `node scripts/build-contact.mjs --native --test-native`: 7 tests pass, including `a_session_that_travels_kilometres_never_allocates` (60,000 steps at 1200 Hz, more than 1 km of travel, zero allocator calls); the TS contact suites (`tests/contact.test.ts`, `contact-allocation`, `contact-binding`, `landing-gear`, `jsbsim-contact`, `terrain-contact`, `rigid-body-v2`) all pass on the rebuilt WASM. Full gates: lint 0, typecheck 0, build 0; `npm test` 1005 passed and 1 skipped, its only failures the 4 stale mirror hashes of the documentation policy test, refreshed after this review; `npm run test:browser` 86 passed and 28 skipped. Increment 2a, done (implicit strut and tire loads; friction and the slope-hold and sideslip fixtures remain for 2b). Each wheel stroke takes a θ-method step before Rapier integrates, from the new `tangent` of `LinearStrut`, `GasStrut` and `TireCurve`, with backward Euler on touchdown and lift-off steps (design and the rejected variants in the engines spec). The requirement holds: drop peaks 27.72, 28.72 and 28.84 g at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz from 0.5 m and 55.00, 57.04 and 57.16 g from 2 m, within 2.5 % of their mean and 4.3 % of a 100 kHz RK4 reference (28.96 and 57.43 g); tires up to 10⁷ N/m settle at their static deflection within 1 % at every rate. Evidence: `node scripts/build-contact.mjs --native --test-native` 9 passed (from a clean binding target; a target left by unwind trace builds fails on the panic strategy); `cargo test -p mirage-bet --test landing_gear` 11 passed; the 9 TS contact suites 47 passed on the rebuilt WASM; `node scripts/check-contact-binding.mjs` native and WASM agree over 9 × 1,000,000 steps with zero allocator calls (`test-results/validation/task-029-binding.json`); `node scripts/bench-contact.mjs` and `bench-contact.mjs 3 1200` pass the JavaScript and native allocation gates (`test-results/bench/contact.json`, `contact-3-1200.json`). Full gates for 2a: lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm test` 1009 passed and 1 skipped; `npm run test:browser` 86 passed and 28 skipped; `cargo test --workspace` 102 passed. Increment 2b, done (friction): static friction below breakaway and Coulomb friction above, per ground-plane axis against a stick anchor, and the brush side force `C_α·tan α` while rolling, capped at the lateral `μ·N`; contact configuration version 3 adds the cornering stiffness (`corneringStiffnessNpRad`), design in the engines spec. The slope-hold, slope-slide, sideslip and brush fixtures pass their declared tolerances at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz (creep at most 5.4·10⁻⁷ m in 5 s against 1 mm; roll and braked slide within 0.13 % of Coulomb; sideslip stopping distance within 1.4 % of v²/(2μg); brush decay within 2 % of the closed form). `scripts/build-contact.mjs --test-native` now builds in its own target directory: after `check-contact-binding.mjs` had built the abort-only library in the shared one, the test build failed on the panic strategy. Evidence: `node scripts/build-contact.mjs --native --test-native` 14 passed, also directly after `check-contact-binding.mjs`; the 8 TS contact suites 48 passed, with a binding test that the cornering stiffness reaches the native brush law at all three rates; `check-contact-binding.mjs` 9 of 9 parity cases with zero allocator calls; `bench-contact.mjs` and `bench-contact.mjs 3 1200` pass the allocation gates. Full gates for 2b: lint 0 (after removing one unnecessary assertion that the first run flagged), typecheck and build 0; `npm test` 1013 passed and 1 skipped; `npm run test:browser` 86 passed and 28 skipped; `cargo test --workspace` 102 passed. Increment 3, done (flight-model wiring): `BetFlightConfiguration.contact` makes `GroundContact` the body integrator in place of the BET rigid body and the synthetic pad, checked against the model's mass, inertia and gravity; `GroundContact.forVehicle` builds it from the vehicle's mass properties and the gear, and the synchronous `GroundContact.reset` lets the dynamics' `reset` restart it. A synthetic 2 kg four-rotor fixture on four legs rests, climbs 2.5 m and lands at 0.29 m/s at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz, its attitude changing at most 6·10⁻⁵ rad per step (no reset), and after `reset` repeats the same steps. Evidence: `tests/flight-contact.test.ts` 5 passed; `node scripts/bench-flight-contact.mjs` (358 touchdowns in 357 cycles over 10⁶ steps at 400 Hz) 0.00296 B/step, no GC event, no native allocator call, mean step 0.24–0.25 ms (`test-results/bench/flight-contact.json`). Aircraft profiles do not describe gear yet, so `buildProfileDynamics` still uses the synthetic pad; terrain planes and the SITL takeoff and landing remain. Full gates for 3: lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm test` 1018 passed and 1 skipped; `npm run test:browser` 86 passed and 28 skipped; `cargo test --workspace` 102 passed. Increment 3b, done 2026-11-01 (profile vehicles on their supports, and ArduCopter takeoff and landing on the native path; baseline c1026fe). The user decided on 2026-11-01 for a standard gear now and a gear editor later (TASK-194, registered here). The standard gear is the basic support approximation that decision D-07 had already fixed (F-07, F-08 in [phase1-fidelity.md](decisions/phase1-fidelity.md)): one support under each arm at the arm's motor radius, the reference point at the profile's ground clearance at rest, k = m·g/(N·δ_s) and c = 2·ζ·√(k·m/N) with δ_s = 5 mm and ζ = 0.7, and μ_s = 0.6 with μ_k = 0.5. A first draft of the rules (four feet at the diagonals, 10 mm, one μ) contradicted D-07 and was replaced before any commit. Manifest derivation 3 adds the supports and their synthetic dataset `standard-landing-gear` (none for a profile without a clearance, which F-07 places on the body hull of increment 4; of the S19 fixtures only octa-x, which stays on the synthetic pad and lists the limitation). `buildProfileDynamics` hands the gear to `GroundContact` through `withoutLandingGear` and `profileFlightDynamics`, which builds a new contact with the new synchronous `GroundContact.forVehicleNow` at every rate change; the physics Worker takes a `contact.module` message, the Run tab's SITL load fetches it from the development server's loopback-only `/__contact/` route, and `profileModelPort` rejects a geared vehicle before the module is loaded. Contact configuration version 4 adds each wheel's kinetic friction ratio μ_k/μ_s; a ratio of 1 is version 3 bit for bit. The vehicle manifest v2 gains the optional `landingGear[].kineticFrictionRatio`, and existing manifests keep their bytes. Supports under the motors exposed a defect of increment 2b: the stick friction used each wheel's share of the translational airframe mass alone, so a stance wide against the yaw inertia (M·r² > 2·I) over-corrected the yaw every step, and the quad rested with its yaw rate flipping at 0.09 rad/s. The stick now uses the effective mass at the contact point, 1/(1/M + (r×t)·I⁻¹·(r×t)), weighted by load share; at rest the body rates fall to 10⁻¹⁴ rad/s. The S19 power-panel browser spec held 80 % open-loop throttle, which on the gear pitches over and falls where the pad had levelled the quad, so it now holds 30 % (below the hover) against a 2 A pack limit and a 10 W motor rating. That showed a gap of TASK-092 and TASK-146: a pack at its current limit caps the battery's delivered power, but the motors still draw their full bus current, so the limit does not reach the thrust (reported to the user, not changed here). Evidence: `node scripts/build-contact.mjs --native --test-native` 19 passed (from a clean test target; a target left by `check-contact-binding.mjs` still fails on the panic strategy), among them the version 4 kinetic slide (0.5039–0.5075 m against v²/(2μ_k·g) = 0.5099 m), the hold on tan 0.45 at μ_k = 0.525 (creep at most 3.3·10⁻⁶ m) with the slide on tan 0.6 at 0.6307 m/s², version 3 bit for bit at a ratio of 1, and the wide-stance rest (body rates at most 7.8·10⁻¹² rad/s); the existing fixtures keep their tolerances, most unchanged to four digits; `tests/contact-binding.test.ts` passes the ratio through the WASM contact at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz; `node scripts/check-contact-binding.mjs` 9 of 9 parity cases with zero allocator calls (`test-results/validation/task-029-binding.json`); `tests/aircraft-profile.test.ts` 39 passed, the D-07 oracle restated from the decision record and the derivation 1 recordings reproduced by taking the gear out; `tests/profile-dynamics.test.ts` with the four geared S19 configurations at rest within 0.1 mm of the rigid-plate statics at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz with body rates below 10⁻⁶ rad/s; `tests/external-sitl-flight.test.ts` (native ArduCopter 8ab6afd, 800 Hz, speedup 4): READY at 41.1 s, takeoff to 5 m, hold within 5.011–5.071 m, LAND, touchdown at 0.44 m/s and at rest 0.1 µm from the predicted height, disarmed, no step turning the body more than 1.05 times its rate (largest excess 1.1·10⁻¹⁰ rad in 58,432 steps), no lockstep gap; `tests/motor-check-sitl.test.ts` 7 passed: all five S19 configurations pass their motor check against native ArduCopter, and the swapped and reversed faults are named; `node scripts/check-sitl-link-allocation.mjs` exit 0, 10⁶ lockstep steps at 400 Hz with 0.00404 B/step, no GC event, the quad on its supports throughout (`test-results/bench/sitl-link-allocation.json`); `node scripts/bench-flight-contact.mjs` 0.00296 B/step, no GC event, no native allocator call, 358 touchdowns, mean step 0.21–0.22 ms (`test-results/bench/flight-contact.json`); `node scripts/bench-contact.mjs` and `bench-contact.mjs 3 1200` pass the JavaScript and native allocation gates (`test-results/bench/contact.json`, `contact-3-1200.json`); in Chrome, `tests/browser/profile-dynamics.spec.ts` (a load before the contact module is rejected), `power-panel.spec.ts`, `external-sitl.spec.ts` (Run tab: READY, takeoff, hold, landing on the supports) and `motor-test.spec.ts` pass. Remaining: the compound hull and propeller-strike events (increment 4, with the supports of profiles without a clearance), terrain planes in sessions (TASK-187), and ArduCopter on the browser path, which waits for the WASM firmware artifact (TASK-045). Full gates for 3b (Apple M5, Node 24.22.1, Chrome): lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm test` 1217 passed and 1 skipped, its failures the 4 stale mirror hashes of the documentation policy test, refreshed after this review, and `tests/drive-chain.test.ts`, which fails on main too; `npm run test:browser` 112 passed and 16 skipped, its two failures two landed-height thresholds of the first gear draft (`external-sitl.spec.ts`, `power-panel.spec.ts`), which pass against the D-07 supports' heights on rerun; `cargo test --workspace` not rerun (no `native/bet` change), the contact crate's tests as above. Increment 4, in progress 2026-11-01 (the frame and the propellers as contact shapes; baseline 0e260ac). The user decided on 2026-11-01 for a hull worked out from the arms now, marked not measured, with body size fields later (TASK-196), and for a propeller clearance check that blocks a run and offers a previewed fix (TASK-195); both registered here. Design, before implementation. Standard hull 1 (`packages/aircraft/src/standard-hull.ts`, synthetic dataset `standard-hull` of manifest derivation 4, optional manifest `hull`): a body cylinder about the body z axis through the reference point, radius 0.25 times the shortest arm and half-height 0.4 times that radius; and one capsule per arm from the body axis to its motor mount at the arm's height, radius 0.05 times the largest propeller radius on the arm. A profile without a ground clearance takes, for its supports, the clearance that leaves the hull's lowest point one static deflection δ_s above the ground at rest; this reading of F-07 keeps the resting hull off the ground, and a hard landing that doubles the load reaches it. Each rotor adds a propeller disc: a cylinder at its hub about its thrust axis, its radius the rotor radius and half its thickness 0.025 times that radius. Contact configuration version 5 (contact C ABI v3, since the buffers grow): `c[1]` holds up to 32 shapes (capsules and cylinders, body FRD from the CG) after the wheels, which replace the airframe sphere and take its friction and restitution against the airframe ground plane; each step reports per shape the deepest penetration, the normal impulse and force, the deepest point in body axes and whether it touches. These shapes never carry the supports' load at rest. TASK-149 turns the reports into accident events with the F-09 thresholds; the TASK-095 interface's propeller-strike events are these reports. Failure criterion: a resting S19 configuration with any shape in contact, a strike reported on a shape that did not touch, a shape report missing a contact the geometry implies, a configuration of versions 1 to 4 that changes bit for bit, or an allocation in the step. Increment 4, done 2026-11-01. Touched Files additions during implementation: `native/contact/src/probe.rs` (a hull case of the parity probe) and `native/rapier/patches/parry-halfspace-reuse.patch` with `native/rapier/source.lock.json`, `native/contact/Cargo.lock`, `scripts/build-contact.mjs`, `config/offline-inventory.json`, `docs/operations/offline-inventory.md` and `docs/ru/offline-inventory.md`. The first tumbling-hull test faulted the C ABI with −6 at step 2,367: parry3d-f64 0.31.1 took a capsule's or cylinder's contact points from their storage at every halfspace contact (`core::mem::take` in `contact_manifold_halfspace_pfm`), so every touching shape allocated every step. The build now patches the pinned parry crate archive (SHA-256 in the source lock, from Cargo's cache or crates.io) to keep that storage and copy the previous points, at most four, to the stack; the artifact manifest records the patch hash. Evidence: `node scripts/build-contact.mjs --native --test-native` 25 passed (from a clean test target), among them a capsule and a cylinder resting at their geometric depth (CG 0.0798–0.0799 m against 0.08 m, 0.03997 m against 0.04 m) and carrying m·g within 0.01 %, a tilted disc held 1 mm low reporting 1 mm and its analytic rim point within 10⁻⁶ m, only the disc below a quad's feet reporting contact, version 5 without shapes equal to version 4 bit for bit, eight invalid shape records rejected, and a quad hull with eight discs tumbling across the ground for 120,000 steps through the C ABI without an allocation (arm capsules and discs both touching); `node scripts/check-contact-binding.mjs` 12 of 12 parity cases, among them a hull without wheels at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz, each 10⁶ native steps with zero allocator calls and the WASM run within the predeclared budget (`test-results/validation/task-029-binding.json`); `tests/contact-binding.test.ts` passes the shapes through the WASM contact at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz (CG 0.08 m over the ground within 2 mm, m·g within 2 %, a clear cylinder reporting nothing) and rejects invalid shapes; `tests/aircraft-profile.test.ts` 39 passed, the standard hull restated from the user's decision and octa-x on supports at the clearance that leaves its body 5 mm off the ground, the derivation 1 recordings reproduced by taking the gear and the hull out and the derivation 4 hashes recorded; `tests/vehicle-manifest.test.ts` 24 passed (the optional `hull` and its dataset, `withoutHull` restoring the bytes, 11 rejections); `tests/profile-dynamics.test.ts` 16 passed: all five S19 configurations rest within 0.1 mm of the rigid-plate statics at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz with no hull or propeller shape touching on the way, and a quad X rolled 60° and pitched 10° nose down, dropped 5 mm above its lowest shape, strikes first and alone with the propeller disc that geometry puts lowest; `node scripts/bench-contact.mjs` and `bench-contact.mjs 3 1200` pass the JavaScript and native allocation gates; `node scripts/bench-flight-contact.mjs` 0.00296 B/step, no GC event, no native allocator call; `node scripts/check-sitl-link-allocation.mjs` exit 0, 10⁶ lockstep steps at 400 Hz with 0.00404 B/step and no GC event, the quad X with its hull and discs on its supports throughout; `tests/motor-check-sitl.test.ts` and `tests/external-sitl-session.test.ts` pass against native ArduCopter, and `tests/external-sitl-flight.test.ts` (READY, takeoff to 5 m, hold, LAND on the supports) passes. Remaining: accident events and pause (TASK-149), terrain planes in sessions (TASK-187), the propeller clearance check (TASK-195), and ArduCopter on the browser path (TASK-045). Full gates for 4 (Apple M5, Node 24.22.1, Chrome): lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm run check:docs` passes; `npm test` 1269 passed and 1 skipped, its failures `tests/drive-chain.test.ts`, which fails on main too, and the two native ArduCopter runs of `tests/external-sitl-flight.test.ts` and `tests/dataflash-capture.test.ts`, which disturb each other when they run at once (the flight test's ArduCopter disarms during takeoff, the capture reads a damaged log); run together on main 0e260ac they fail the same way, and each passes alone here; `npm run test:browser` 115 passed and 16 skipped; `cargo test --workspace` not rerun (no `native/bet` change), the contact crate's tests as above. Closed 2026-11-03 on d5d1f2c. Of the items left after increment 4, TASK-149 and TASK-195 are DONE, and terrain planes in sessions are TASK-187's own DoD. In Phase 1 the DoD's two paths are the native harness and the Run tab, both flying native ArduCopter as an external process (PROJECT_SCOPE.md section 2); browser-hosted ArduCopter is not a Phase 1 flight mode by TASK-045's disposition, and it could not run here anyway (no `emcc`, `MIRAGE_EMSDK_DIR` unset). Manifests without gear, which no aircraft profile derives any more, still stand on the synthetic pad and list it as a limitation. Evidence rerun on d5d1f2c (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1): `node scripts/build-contact.mjs --native --test-native` 25 passed, the drop, slope-hold, slide and sideslip fixtures at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz among them; `tests/contact.test.ts`, `contact-binding`, `landing-gear`, `terrain-contact`, `flight-contact`, `profile-dynamics`, `accident-session` and `external-sitl-flight`, run one file at a time, 8 files and 57 tests passed, the last with native ArduCopter 8ab6afd taking off, holding and landing on the supports without an attitude reset; `node scripts/bench-flight-contact.mjs` 10⁶ steps at 400 Hz, 0.00296 B/step, no GC event, no native allocator call, 358 touchdowns; `node scripts/check-sitl-link-allocation.mjs` 10⁶ lockstep steps at 400 Hz, 0.00382 B/step, no GC event, on the gear throughout; `node scripts/bench-contact.mjs` 0 native allocator calls and the JavaScript gate passed (3,664, 3,576 and 2,928 B per 10⁶ steps, no GC event, against 743,240 B and 30 GC events for the positive control), and `bench-contact.mjs 3 1200` the same with three wheels at 1200 Hz (0 native allocator calls, the JavaScript gate passed); in Playwright's headless Chromium, `tests/browser/external-sitl.spec.ts` passed: the Run tab took the quad X profile to READY with native ArduCopter, took off to 5 m, held and landed on its supports. The NASA drop comparison stays deferred.

### TASK-096 — Multirotor airframe aerodynamics

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 8237f98; 2026-11-03. Registered 2026-10-26 from the physics fidelity review (baseline 4593971). Goal: body drag in production flight (S05). Failure criterion: drag that differs from `F = −½·ρ·|v_a|·(C_D·A)·v_a`, that uses the ground velocity instead of the velocity relative to the air, that counts the rotors' momentum drag twice, that is not flagged outside its data, or that allocates in the tick. Plan, in increments: (1) the drag model in `BetFlightDynamics` from a manifest `airframe.drag` block, which `buildProfileDynamics` consumes (done); (2) drag data from aircraft profiles: a preliminary estimate from the standard hull's geometry, labeled preliminary, in the derived manifest (done), and drag areas the user states with their source in the Aircraft tab (done); (3) the trimmed forward-flight lean angle against its reference (shown, not judged, F-02) and EKF3 drag fusion (`EK3_DRAG_BCOEF_X/Y`, `EK3_DRAG_MCOEF`) converging in SITL (done). User decision of 2026-11-03: the cross-wind result below, which EKF3's per-axis drag model was then taken to explain (corrected at the end of this status), meets the DoD's convergence, since the along-wind component converges; MirageSITL keeps its drag along the relative wind rather than EKF3's per-axis form. Increment 1: the configuration's `airframeDrag` holds a symmetric positive-definite drag-area tensor, the center of pressure from the CG and the data's airspeed range; every step adds the force and its moment to the rotors' wrench, from the body-axis velocity relative to the air at the step's midpoint that the rotors' inflow uses (TASK-093) and the density they read, and sets `VALIDITY_FLAG_DRAG_OUTSIDE_RANGE` (bit 17) beyond the range. The tensor holds the airframe without its rotors, whose momentum drag the BET produces. The manifest block gains an optional `maxAirspeedMps`; `buildProfileDynamics` moves the center of pressure to the CG, lists a block without a range as a limitation, and drops a dataset that only consumed sections name (the motor data of a manifest with its own propulsion dataset failed the same way before). The registered Rust kernel (`native/bet/src/airframe.rs`, `native/bet/src/lib.rs`, `native/bet/tests/airframe.rs`) was not needed: the drag is a few products in the TypeScript step. Increment 2: manifest derivation 6 adds `standardHullDrag` of the standard hull in its own synthetic dataset `standard-hull-drag`, labeled preliminary and not measured: each body cylinder or arm capsule adds C_D times its area across its axis (1.2, a subcritical circular cylinder of infinite span, an upper estimate) and along it (1.17, a flat disk or blunt end, Hoerner), arms counted from the body radius outward; the center of pressure weights the shapes' centers by their drag areas, and the range ends where the body reaches Reynolds number 2e5 in ISA sea-level air (26 m/s for the S19 quad X, whose 0.012 m² frontal drag area alone needs a 2.9° lean at 10 m/s). Every profile flight now has body drag. Profile schema 5 adds `airframeDrag`: drag areas the user states (C_D·A along x, y and z, the center of pressure, the airspeed range and a source that is not blank), written exactly while stated and returned byte for byte to schema 4 or lower when removed; the derivation writes them as the diagonal tensor in the profile's own dataset, without the `standard-hull-drag` dataset, and the Aircraft tab's Body drag section shows the estimate and lets the user state values, which start from it and need a source. Increment 3 (`tests/airframe-drag-sitl.test.ts`, native ArduCopter 8ab6afde through the TASK-152 installation): the quad X holds position in GUIDED in a steady north wind; its trimmed lean is 2.03° at 4 m/s and 4.58° at 8 m/s against 0.44° and 1.76° for the body drag alone (1.65° and 2.83° without any airframe drag), shown and not judged; the rotors' share calibrates `EK3_DRAG_MCOEF` = 0.062 1/s, and `EK3_DRAG_BCOEF_X/Y` = m/(C_D·A) = 130 kg/m²; set in flight, they let EKF3 learn the along-wind component within 10 s (−8.14 m/s against −8 m/s; zero without them). The cross-wind component carries an error that EKF3's drag model explains: EKF3 predicts each body axis's drag from that axis's airspeed alone, as ArduPilot's built-in SITL multicopter computes its drag (`SIM_Frame.cpp`), while drag along the relative wind (the contract's) and the BET rotors' side forces leave a persistent sideways innovation (XKF2 IDY +0.003 to +0.03 m/s² against about +0.001 on the built-in quad) that EKF3 absorbs into heading and cross-wind. With ArduPilot's default simulated gyro drift (SIM_DRIFT_SPEED 0.05°/s per minute) the heading turns about 0.045°/s and the cross-wind estimate reaches −2.07 m/s after 175 s, while the built-in quad holds its heading in the same test; with the drift off the heading settles about 2° off and the cross-wind estimate about 0.5 m/s off. The test asserts the along-wind component and records the cross-wind one. Evidence (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1): `tests/airframe-drag.test.ts` 5 passed (force and moment within 1e-12 of the formula in four constant winds, the extra acceleration F/m within 1e-10, the turn rate within 1e-5, the flag on either side of the range, the refusals, Galilean invariance with drag within 1e-9 over 400 steps); the consumed block in `tests/profile-dynamics.test.ts`; with `tests/vehicle-manifest.test.ts`, `tests/bet-runtime.test.ts`, `tests/bet-runtime-graphs.test.ts`, `tests/weather-plumbing.test.ts`, `tests/session-v2.test.ts` and `tests/aircraft-profile.test.ts`, 8 files and 124 tests passed; `tests/standard-hull-drag.test.ts` 4 passed (the estimate against drag areas worked out by hand for a body with an arm along x and at 45°, and a symmetric positive-definite drag centered on the body axis for every S19 fixture); `tests/aircraft-profile.test.ts` 39 passed, taking derivation 6 back to the recorded derivation 5 bytes for every fixture, so only the drag changed; `tests/browser/aircraft-derivation.spec.ts` 2 passed in headless Chrome, which derives the same derivation 6 bytes; `tests/airframe-drag-sitl.test.ts` 1 passed in 46 s of wall time; `tests/standard-hull-drag.test.ts` 7 passed with profile schema 5 (the round trip, the refusals, the derived stated drag); `tests/browser/airframe-drag.spec.ts` 1 passed in headless Chrome (the estimate, the prefill, the refusal without a source, version 2 in schema 5 and version 3 back in schema 4), and the Aircraft tab's browser tests with their accessibility checks 10 passed. `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs --allocation` passed with the new graph: airframe drag in the pad's gusty wind 0.0020 B/step over 1,000,000 steps, no GC event, the drag flag set on 620,592 of them; every other graph as before, the positive control detected, the native allocator at 0 calls. Gates: `npx tsc --noEmit` 0; `npm run lint` 0; `npm run build` 0 (only the existing chunk-size warning); `npm run check:docs` passed; `npm test` 1380 passed and 4 failed: `tests/calculation-mode.test.ts` and `tests/polar-catalog.test.ts`, which expected the earlier refusal wording and schema 5 as an unknown version (updated, 19 passed), `tests/airframe-drag-sitl.test.ts`, which hit the WASM build race and passes alone, and the known M5 golden hash of `tests/drive-chain.test.ts` (`0319387a…`). Correction of 2026-11-04, found by TASK-158: the quad flew this test in a sustained 10 Hz attitude oscillation that ArduCopter's stock rate gains and 20 Hz gyro filter cause on this airframe (`docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, section "Attitude oscillation of the quad X at stock gains"), and that oscillation, not EKF3's drag form, is the main cause of the cross-wind and heading error above. The same fusion over 175 s with the 40 Hz gyro filter of the user's decision of 2026-11-04: cross-wind estimate −0.11 m/s against −1.90 m/s at stock settings, heading +0.17° against −7.8°, XKF2 IDY +0.0028 m/s² on average against +0.0185 m/s², growing to +0.041 m/s²; along-wind −8.33 m/s against −8.23 m/s; rate gains at 0.8 of stock gave the same result (−0.10 m/s, +0.18°). The earlier lean, `EK3_DRAG_MCOEF` and drift figures above were measured in that oscillation. `tests/airframe-drag-sitl.test.ts` now flies with the 40 Hz filter and also asserts the cross-wind estimate within the declared 1.0 m/s. Evidence (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 27.2, Node 24.22.1): 1 passed; lean 1.81° at 4 m/s and 4.58° at 8 m/s against 0.44° and 1.76° for the body drag alone, `EK3_DRAG_MCOEF` 0.060 1/s, and over the last 10 s of the 60 s fusion −8.33 m/s along the true −8 m/s wind and −0.13 m/s across it, with the heading 0.11° from where it was. The user's decision stands for the drag form, and the DoD's convergence now holds on both components.
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Required (S05): body drag. Its measured-data comparisons follow decision D-11.

## Phase 9: Advanced Rotor Aerodynamics

Rotor fidelity in hover, edgewise flight, descent and near the ground.

- **Dependencies:** TASK-019, TASK-033, TASK-091.

### TASK-098 — Inflow v2: annular, tip loss, edgewise and dynamic inflow

- **Status:** TODO — registered 2026-10-26 from the physics fidelity review; baseline 4593971.

- **Dependencies:** TASK-021, TASK-088, TASK-091, TASK-097.
- **Touched Files:** `native/bet/src/inflow.rs`, `native/bet/src/blade.rs`, `native/bet/src/bound_v2.rs`, `native/bet/tests/inflow.rs`, `native/bet/tests/dynamic_inflow.rs`, `packages/wasm-bet/src/rotor-v2.ts`, `tests/fixtures/rotors/dynamic-inflow.json`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `scripts/validate-models.mjs`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/ru/architecture.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Annular momentum with the Prandtl factor F (at most four warm-started Newton iterations); Glauert `λ = μ·tan α_d + C_T/(2·√(μ² + λ²))`; Pitt–Peters three-state dynamic inflow `[M]·λ̇ + [L(χ)]⁻¹·λ = [C_T, C_L, C_M]ᵀ` with `M = diag(128/75π, 16/45π, 16/45π)` and `λ(r,ψ) = λ₀ + (r/R)·(λs·sinψ + λc·cosψ)`; derived time scale `τ ≈ M·R/(4·v_i)`; manual `inflowTauS` overrides flagged synthetic.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Hover momentum residual ≤1%; edgewise and climb fixtures match annular BEM references within declared tolerances; the inflow step-response time constant is within 10% of the analytic value; the UIUC rerun is recorded; iteration-cap failures are flagged, never silent; G1 gates pass.

## Phase 10: Propulsion & Environment

Dynamic battery and motor models, standard turbulence, wind shear and Earth fields.

### TASK-104 — Battery v2: dynamic, thermal and aging

- **Status:** TODO — registered 2026-10-26 from the physics fidelity review; baseline 4593971.
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Deferred: heating, cooling and aging are excluded (scope section 2); load-dependent sag and electrical limits come from TASK-092 and TASK-146. It keeps its ID, contract and evidence; work on it needs separate stakeholder approval.
- **Title & Description:** Model realistic voltage sag, recovery and temperature behavior so ArduPilot battery failsafes and sag compensation behave as in the field.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-026, TASK-092, TASK-093.
- **Touched Files:** `native/bet/src/battery.rs`, `native/bet/tests/battery.rs`, `tests/fixtures/power/battery-2rc.json`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `tests/battery-dynamics.test.ts`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/ru/architecture.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Thevenin 2-RC model `V = OCV(SOC,T) − I·R₀(T,SOC) − V₁ − V₂` with `V̇_k = −V_k/(R_k·C_k) + I/C_k` discretized exactly; tabulated LiPo open-circuit voltage; Arrhenius `R₀(T)`; pack heat balance `m·c_p·Ṫ = I²·R₀ + Σ V_k²/R_k + I·T·∂U/∂T − h(V_air)·A·(T − T_amb)`; capacity fade and resistance growth; parameters fitted from pulse tests.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Pulse-response fixtures match analytic RC solutions; a measured pulse or flight discharge is reproduced within the declared voltage RMS tolerance; the `BATT_FS_VOLTSRC 1` sag-compensated failsafe behaves as expected in SITL.

### TASK-106 — Turbulence v2: Dryden, von Kármán and Kaimal

- **Status:** DONE — the Phase 1 part, Claude; baseline feb91c9 with TASK-166; 2026-11-05 (registered 2026-10-26 from the physics fidelity review, baseline 4593971). MIL-F-8785C low-altitude Dryden turbulence in `packages/physics/src/turbulence.ts`, exactly discretized, with fixed RNG streams and coefficients refreshed on tick indices, on the flight path as the TASK-166 effect `wind.turbulence`. Evidence, tolerances declared before the first run: `tests/turbulence.test.ts`, 7 passed, over 1e7 samples at 400 Hz with h = 10 ft and V = 50 m/s: variance within 2 %, autocorrelation at one and two time constants within 0.02 of the closed forms, Welch estimates in log-spaced bands from 0.1 Hz to 150 Hz within 5 % of the folded specification spectra, and seed replay, exact linearity in intensity, the axes and the tick-indexed refresh; a hand-worked 100 ft check of the MIL-F-8785C scale lengths. `tests/effect-controls.test.ts` runs it through the real session: off is bitwise absent, a new mean wind leaves it running, and three wall pacings replay it. `node scripts/bench-wind.mjs`: zero allocation with coefficient refreshes included, no collections in the probe's windows; `node scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs --allocation`, exit 0, with `wind.turbulence` among its session graph's commands: −328 bytes over 1,000,000 steps, no collections. The first bench run found 66 bytes a tick from uint32 results boxed across non-inlined PRNG calls, so `SeededRandom.nextGaussianInto` writes the two draws out in one method. Not done, and not required for Phase 1: rotational gusts, inter-hub coherence, von Kármán and Kaimal spectra, the medium- and high-altitude forms. V reads as at least W20, a choice made for hover that the spec states as a choice.
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Required (S07) for turbulence; Dryden spectra are the minimum Phase 1 increment.
- **Title & Description:** Replace the uncorrelated per-axis AR(1) gust with standard turbulence spectra scaled by altitude, airspeed and condition, including rotational gusts and spatial coherence.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-032, TASK-093, TASK-166.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/physics/src/wind.ts`, `packages/physics/src/turbulence.ts`, `packages/physics/src/atmosphere.ts`, `packages/physics/src/bet-dynamics.ts`, `packages/physics/src/session-v2.ts`, `packages/physics/package.json`, `packages/test-support/src/prng.ts`, `packages/protocol/src/control-v3.ts`, `packages/protocol/src/control-v3.schema.json`, `tests/turbulence.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures.test.ts`, `tests/control-v3.test.ts`, `tests/effect-controls.test.ts`, `tests/browser/instructor.spec.ts`, `scripts/bench-wind.mjs`, `scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/SPECS_SHARED_MEMORY.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SHARED_MEMORY.md`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/ru/architecture.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** MIL-F-8785C Dryden shaping filters `H_u = σ_u·√(2·L_u/(π·V))/(1 + (L_u/V)·s)` and `H_v,w = σ·√(L/(π·V))·(1 + √3·(L/V)·s)/(1 + (L/V)·s)²` with low-altitude scale lengths and intensities; rotational gusts; von Kármán rational fits; Kaimal spectra advected by the mean wind for hover; inter-hub coherence; exact discretization with tick-indexed coefficient refresh; fixed RNG stream IDs.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Estimated PSDs match the specification spectra within declared tolerances over 1e7 samples; variance and scale lengths match; replay is seed-deterministic and FPS-independent; G1 gates pass. The effect exposes its on/off and intensity controls under the TASK-166 contract, and off is bitwise identical to the effect being absent.

## Phase 13: Structures & Extreme Environment

Flexible structure, mass changes, convective and hazardous flows, and unsteady aerodynamics.

## Product Phase 1 program (PROJECT_SCOPE.md)

Registered on 2026-10-28 from the approved [PROJECT_SCOPE.md](../PROJECT_SCOPE.md) (audit baseline `5b4a2fc`) and an inventory of baseline `384e974` and the unmerged branches. [PHASE1_PLAN.md](PHASE1_PLAN.md) maps every requirement group to existing and new work and gives the disposition of earlier open tasks, the stage order, the decisions and the risks. Registration implements nothing and authorizes no development: each task starts only under an explicit development instruction and stays within that instruction's scope.

Phase 1 targets macOS on Apple Silicon in Chrome. ArduPilot runs as an external local SITL process, and the browser provides the UI, visualization and simulation integration. Each task names the scope requirement groups it serves (S01–S19); these IDs are unrelated to the sprint-batch labels in the table above. Historical Windows and WSL evidence is preserved but is not Phase 1 acceptance. Deferred tasks keep their IDs and contracts. The stages below follow the scope's execution plan; no dates or effort estimates are given.

## Stage P1.1: Feasibility and Decisions

Scope step 1. Each task produces evidence on the target platform or an exact BLOCKED status; decisions record their owner and date.

### TASK-127 — Native ArduPilot SITL on macOS Apple Silicon

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline bdec1c0; 2026-10-29, re-verified on baseline 78b4ee0 on 2026-10-30. On Apple M5, macOS 26.6 (Darwin 25.6.0), Apple Clang 21.0.0, Node 24.22.1, Python 3.9.6 and pymavlink 2.4.50, stock ArduCopter `8ab6afde107a91df1b5f60ade8f333cb8a61b190` built from a clean pinned checkout without a patch; the arm64 Mach-O binary SHA-256 is `d47c1f64934f1911e4d6fb88da8aa18c21c9e438f3ae687b0e56eb13807e08e4`. The re-verification capture `.cache/task-127/capture-004` passed `scripts/sitl/check-raw.py` (31,953 observations) and `scripts/check-sitl-capture.mjs`: 67,257 physics frames at 800 Hz, 4,161 scored samples, 10.19 s hold at truth altitude 9.786–9.987 m, landed and disarmed, zero gaps, duplicates, drops, restarts or stale samples; only owned PIDs were terminated. Evidence: [macos-sitl.md](decisions/macos-sitl.md). `scripts/sitl/native-binary.ts`, `tests/native-sitl-binary.test.ts` and `scripts/check-sitl-capture.mjs` were added to Touched Files for Mach-O admission and the host record.
- **Title & Description:** (S01, S02) Build the pinned ArduCopter SITL natively on an Apple Silicon Mac and fly it in a closed loop against MirageSITL physics through the JSON backend. The owned-process harness refuses non-Linux hosts and checks for ELF binaries today. Extend it to accept a macOS host and Mach-O binaries, keep its Linux path, and add no Windows work. Record the toolchain and every blocker. A virtual machine or container is not substituted without a recorded stakeholder decision.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-041, TASK-042.
- **Touched Files:** `scripts/run-sitl.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/driver.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/telemetry.py`, `scripts/sitl/native-binary.ts`, `scripts/check-sitl-capture.mjs`, `tests/support/parity-native.ts`, `tests/native-sitl-binary.test.ts`, `tests/integration/external-sitl.test.ts`, `docs/decisions/macos-sitl.md`, `docs/ru/macos-sitl.md`, `docs/documentation-index.json`, `docs/operations/runbook.md`, `docs/ru/runbook.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Host record: macOS version, chip, Xcode command-line tools, Python and pymavlink 2.4.50, ArduPilot commit and binary SHA-256. The harness accepts ELF on Linux and Mach-O on macOS, keeps process-group ownership and cleanup, and still refuses Windows. Missing prerequisites exit 2 (BLOCKED_EXTERNAL).
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** On an Apple Silicon Mac the pinned ArduCopter builds from a clean pinned checkout, or the exact failure is recorded. The TASK-042 arm, takeoff, hold and land scenario passes `scripts/check-sitl-capture.mjs` on that Mac. Cleanup terminates only owned processes. Fake and non-executable binaries are still rejected. Cloud Linux runs and historical WSL captures do not count (G4).

### TASK-128 — ArduPilot version and the 16-motor output path

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline bdec1c0; 2026-10-29, re-verified on baseline 78b4ee0 on 2026-10-30. The pinned ArduCopter V4.8.0-dev `8ab6afde107a91df1b5f60ade8f333cb8a61b190`, arm64 Mach-O SHA-256 `d47c1f64934f1911e4d6fb88da8aa18c21c9e438f3ae687b0e56eb13807e08e4`, expresses quad-x, hexa-x, octo-x and x8 with stock frame classes and the eight-arm, two-motor 16-motor configuration with stock frame class 15 and `MotorsMatrix:add_motor_raw`/`init(16)` Lua, without a firmware patch. Motor functions are 33–40, 82–85 and 160–179; `SERVO_32_ENABLE` selects 16- or 32-channel JSON packets; the pinned firmware names the arming parameter `ARMING_SKIPCHK`, set to 0. On Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1 and pymavlink 2.4.50 the 2026-10-30 matrix in `.cache/task-128/rerun-20261030/` passes 42/42 one-motor tests and five native hovers at 10 m ±1 m for at least 10.19 s; all 20 raw and physics audits exit 0, 406,525 frames replay through scalar WASM with zero error, and the 16-rotor G1 probe records 13,000,000 steps with zero GC and a zero N/2N slope. Evidence: [ardupilot-frames.md](decisions/ardupilot-frames.md). This is compatibility evidence for D-01; it does not approve a release firmware version. `tests/fixtures/sitl/generate_frames.py`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `scripts/run-sitl-frames.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/frames-driver.mjs`, `scripts/check-sitl-frames.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/check-raw.py` and `tests/support/frames-allocation.mjs` were added to Touched Files for the frame probe and its audits; `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md` and its mirror were not changed, because the harness contract is recorded in the decision record.
- **Title & Description:** (S03, S19) Select the compatible ArduCopter version or versions for Phase 1. Verify, in the hash-pinned source and in native SITL on macOS, that quad, hexa and octo frames, one coaxial-pair frame and the eight-arm, two-motor (16-motor) configuration can be expressed with stock frame classes or the stock scripting-matrix frame. Check the motor limit, the output count and the JSON servo packet. A firmware limitation is escalated as BLOCKED_DECISION with options; the 16-motor requirement is never reduced.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-041, TASK-127.
- **Touched Files:** `tests/fixtures/sitl/frames.json`, `tests/fixtures/sitl/generate.py`, `tests/fixtures/sitl/generate_frames.py`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `scripts/sitl/multirotor.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/driver.mjs`, `scripts/run-sitl-frames.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/frames-driver.mjs`, `scripts/check-sitl-frames.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/check-raw.py`, `tests/support/frames-allocation.mjs`, `tests/sitl-frames.test.ts`, `docs/decisions/ardupilot-frames.md`, `docs/ru/ardupilot-frames.md`, `docs/documentation-index.json`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Frame catalog generated from the pinned source: frame class and type to motor count, motor numbering, position and yaw factors and spin; motor output-function values; maximum motor and output counts; 16- and 32-channel JSON servo packets. A synthetic N-rotor reference model in the harness replaces the quad-only reference for these checks.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** For each of the five configurations native SITL arms, a one-motor-at-a-time test moves exactly the expected output in the physics packet, and the aircraft hovers. Otherwise the exact limitation and its options (for example the stock scripting-matrix frame, a build option, or a firmware patch that needs approval) are recorded as BLOCKED_DECISION. The selected versions are recorded with commit and binary hash.

### TASK-129 — External SITL clock ownership, synchronized pause and acceleration

- **Status:** DONE — Cursor; baseline bdec1c0; 2026-10-29. On Apple M5, macOS 25.6.0, Node 24.22.1, stock ArduCopter `8ab6afde107a91df1b5f60ade8f333cb8a61b190` binary SHA-256 `d47c1f64934f1911e4d6fb88da8aa18c21c9e438f3ae687b0e56eb13807e08e4`, JSON lockstep owns simulated time. Ground and hover holds of 60.052204958 s and 60.050562167 s wall resumed with zero settled firmware telemetry, autopilot `SYSTEM_TIME` advance 0.2 s, no failsafe text, no EKF reset in the resume window, and scalar-WASM replay error 0. Hover after resume lasted 10.2025 s inside 10 m ±1 m. `WP_SPD` writes during each hold were answered only after resume and restored to 10. The bridge stayed open, 60 pings per hold. Requested `--speedup` 2 achieved 1.9840398077982284 with paused wall excluded and 1.9740263931843125 continuously; requested 20 achieved 11.306202551400018, host-limited. Evidence: [sitl-clock.md](decisions/sitl-clock.md). No firmware change. `docs/PHASE1_PLAN.md` and its mirror were added to Touched Files to record D-03.
- **Title & Description:** (S02, S10) Demonstrate on macOS, with native ArduCopter in JSON lockstep, that MirageSITL physics owns simulated time. Withholding physics replies freezes ArduPilot time, resume continues from the retained state, and accelerated runs are limited only by the host. Characterize MAVLink parameter access and RC and ground-station traffic while paused. Characterize the bridge's 5 s idle disconnect, which would drop a paused session. Classify which manual-connection configurations are clock-compatible.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-041, TASK-043, TASK-127.
- **Touched Files:** `scripts/run-sitl-pause.mjs`, `scripts/check-sitl-pause.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/pause-telemetry.py`, `scripts/sitl/check-pause-raw.py`, `scripts/sitl/pause-probe.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/native-binary.ts`, `scripts/sitl/multirotor.mjs`, `tests/fixtures/sitl/frames.json`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`, `apps/bridge/src/server.ts`, `tests/integration/sitl-pause.test.ts`, `docs/decisions/sitl-clock.md`, `docs/ru/sitl-clock.md`, `docs/documentation-index.json`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/PHASE1_PLAN.md`, `docs/ru/PHASE1_PLAN.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Pause is a tick-boundary hold of the next JSON reply. Simulated time advances only on executed steps, and paused wall time is excluded from simulated duration. The bridge distinguishes a paused session from an idle one. The acceleration setting (`--speedup` or its successor) and the measured simulated/wall ratio are recorded.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** On macOS, pauses of at least 60 s wall time on the ground and in hover resume without failsafe, EKF reset or a state discontinuity beyond a tolerance declared before the test. Whether parameter reads and writes work while paused is recorded with evidence. The achieved acceleration is measured. ArduPilot is not modified.

### TASK-136 — Local project store with immutable versions

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a4227dd; 2026-10-28. `packages/project` stores projects, component cards, aircraft profiles and experiments in IndexedDB as immutable versions addressed by the SHA-256 of their RFC 8785 canonical JSON, and writes and reads project archive v1 (`.mirageproj`). The contract is in [SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md](SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md), section "Local project store and project archive v1 (TASK-136)": it defines the project-archive part of D-05 and chooses IndexedDB over the Origin Private File System, with launcher-managed files as the documented alternative. Evidence on Node 24.22.2: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/project-store.test.ts` exit 0 with 26 tests. Its oracles are the RFC 8785 examples, the FIPS 180-2 "abc" digest, a record canonicalized and hashed by Python 3.13 `json` and `hashlib` (342 bytes, SHA-256 `10c1d0ab…34866`), Unicode NFC compositions, and archives that the test parses and rebuilds from the documented byte layout with `node:crypto`. Nine deliberate defects each fail at least one test: no key sorting, no NFC, unfrozen experiment inputs, dropped missing dependencies, no archive digest check, no entry hash check, no size limit, no head check and forks allowed. `CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/project-store.spec.ts` exit 0 on Chromium 153.0.8010.12, also with `--repeat-each=3`. The test writes a project, closes Chromium and relaunches it on the same profile, and runs save, reopen, copy, export and import with the network disabled and no request made. Cyrillic and Latin names round-trip; the experiment's bytes and inputs stay identical after two later profile edits; a tampered and a truncated archive are rejected without storing anything; the archive imports into a second database with all 6 records; and the IndexedDB backend refuses a stale head and a fork atomically. Full `npm test`: 121 files, 774 tests passed and 1 skipped (the ArduCopter WASM artifact test, which needs `MIRAGE_ARDUPILOT_WASM_DIR`); an earlier run, made before the mirror hashes were refreshed, failed only `tests/documentation-policy.test.ts` on those hashes. `npm run build` and `npm run check:docs` exit 0. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` and `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` exit 0. The application does not use the store yet; TASK-137 adds the shell and its project context. During the task, `tests/support/project-store-harness.ts` and `tests/support/project-store-harness.html` joined Touched Files for the browser harness, and `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/PHASE1_PLAN.md` and their mirrors joined to record this status and the D-05 part that the task defines. Earlier status: IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline a4227dd; 2026-10-28. Goal: a local project store with immutable, content-addressed versions and project archives (S16, S01). Failure criterion: any path that changes the bytes of a stored version, accepts a tampered or oversized archive, loses or alters a Cyrillic or Latin name, or imports an archive without reporting a missing dependency.
- **Title & Description:** (S16, S01) Persist projects, aircraft profile versions, component cards and experiments locally, with no account or cloud service. Editing creates a new version and never changes the inputs of earlier experiments. Export and import report missing dependencies. User-defined names accept Russian and English text. The task chooses the storage backend (Origin Private File System or IndexedDB) and justifies the choice; files managed by the launcher remain a documented alternative.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-076, TASK-081.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/project/package.json`, `packages/project/src/schema.ts`, `packages/project/src/project.schema.json`, `packages/project/src/store.ts`, `packages/project/src/versions.ts`, `packages/project/src/archive.ts`, `tests/project-store.test.ts`, `tests/browser/project-store.spec.ts`, `tests/support/project-store-harness.ts`, `tests/support/project-store-harness.html`, `package-lock.json`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/ru/architecture.md`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/PHASE1_PLAN.md`, `docs/ru/PHASE1_PLAN.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Project schema v1 validated from `unknown`; records addressed by the SHA-256 of canonical JSON; Unicode NFC names with length limits; quota reporting; archives with a manifest, per-file hashes and size limits checked before allocation, as in TASK-081.

### TASK-137 — Phase 1 application shell and navigation

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a286ec5; 2026-10-29. The web application opens in an English shell (`apps/web/src/shell/`) with a project context and the twelve tabs of the description below, in order. The former single demo page is the Run tab's flight backend "Synthetic demo", labeled synthetic; "ArduPilot SITL (not available yet)" is listed and disabled until TASK-153 and TASK-154, and the ten other tabs state what they will hold. The selected tab and the open project are the project store's local setting `shell.navigation`. For that, `packages/project` adds local settings in IndexedDB database version 2, and opening a version 1 database keeps its records ([SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md](SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md), section "Local settings (TASK-137)"). The Project tab creates, opens, renames, exports and imports projects and shows the storage report. While another tab is open, the Run tab's 3D view stops rendering and the simulation keeps running. Evidence on Node 24.22.2 and Chromium 153.0.8010.12 with SwiftShader: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/app-shell.test.ts tests/project-store.test.ts` exit 0 with 38 tests. The tab list is parsed from this entry's description, so the shell cannot drift from it. `CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/app-shell.spec.ts tests/browser/project-store.spec.ts tests/browser/accessibility.spec.ts` exit 0 with 10 tests. Every tab is reached with the arrow keys, Home and End, and only the selected tab is in the Tab order. On all twelve tabs, with a long Russian project name, the axe scan reports no violation and nothing overflows 390 px. Navigation and the open project survive a reload. A project with a Russian name is renamed, exported as `<name> 2.mirageproj`, imported again here ("2 records already stored") and into a fresh profile ("2 records added"), and a tampered copy is refused. A version 1 database built by the test upgrades to version 2 and keeps its records. WebGL draw calls, counted outside the renderer, stay constant while another tab is open and one second of simulation passes. Eleven deliberate defects each fail at least one test: arrow keys that stop wrapping, extra keys accepted in the stored navigation, an item that is not a project opened as the project, a damaged setting that disables the store, non-canonical setting bytes accepted, any setting key accepted, no setting size limit, reserved characters kept in file names, file names not cut to 120 bytes, version 1 databases not upgraded, and the 3D view drawing while hidden (702 draw calls instead of 180). ### TASK-138 — Aircraft profile v1 for multirotors up to eight arms and 16 motors

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 1cca063; 2026-10-29. `packages/aircraft` validates aircraft profile schema 1 from `unknown`. It holds one to eight arms with length, azimuth, height, outward and side tilt and a pair separation; up to 16 motors with arm, level (single, upper or lower), spin, ArduPilot output and component-card references; and mass properties, an optional payload, the battery card, the ground clearance of decision F-07 and a sensor-configuration slot reserved for TASK-143. `deriveLayout` gives every rotor's position, thrust axis, hub orientation and spin, and one wake pair per coaxial pair. `deriveVehicleManifest` adds resolved propulsion values and returns a validated vehicle manifest v2 as canonical JSON with its SHA-256. `profileDraft` and `readAircraftProfile` keep a stored profile's dependencies equal to its component cards. The contract is in [SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md](SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md), section "Aircraft profile v1 (TASK-138)". Evidence on Node 24.22.2 and Chromium 153.0.8010.12: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/aircraft-profile.test.ts` exit 0 with 39 tests. The oracle recomputes every rotor of the five S19 fixtures with independent code to 1e-12: the engine's `Math.cos` and `Math.sin` for the mounts, `Math.atan2` to recover both tilts, and a separate quaternion-to-matrix formula. Hand-derived quad X and X8 positions agree, and the derived quad X passes the TASK-087 check of the ArduCopter quad X that TASK-047 flew, which fails once two spins are swapped. The parallel-axis combination matches a sum about the reference point to 1e-12 of the trace. The tests reject 37 invalid profiles, among them 17 motors, 9 arms, duplicate outputs, four inconsistent pairs (a pair without a separation, a single motor with one, two upper motors, a single and a lower motor), indefinite, asymmetric and triangle-violating inertia, and a payload with negative or triangle-violating inertia, and 9 inconsistent component resolutions. Twenty-three deliberate defects each fail at least one test, among them an inward tilt, a reversed pair, a missing half turn in the hub quaternion, an inverted spin, a channel equal to the output, a wrong parallel-axis sign, a removed validation check, a wrong Taylor coefficient and a two-part π/2 reduction. Node and Chromium return different last bits from `Math.sin`, `Math.cos` and `Math.tan` for 3.3–3.7% of 200,000 arguments, so the layout uses `trig.ts`: against V8 on 400,001 points its sine and cosine are within 1 ulp and its tangent within 3 ulp, and its π/2 parts match Machin's formula to 2^-120. `CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/aircraft-derivation.spec.ts` exit 0, also with `--repeat-each=3`: Chromium derives the five fixtures, and a variant on angles where the engines disagree, with the same SHA-256 as Node. In a negative control with the engines' own trigonometry, Node and Chromium agree on the five symmetric fixtures but derive different manifests for that variant (`7e9bd3de…` and `2c2e8e52…`); with `trig.ts` both give `234f0d6e…`. Full `npm test`: 123 files, 825 tests passed and 1 skipped (the ArduCopter WASM artifact test, which needs `MIRAGE_ARDUPILOT_WASM_DIR`). `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `npm run check:docs`, `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` and `node scripts/check-offline.mjs --dist apps/web/dist` exit 0. The application does not use the package yet: TASK-141 adds the editor and TASK-144 loads the manifest. Earlier status: IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline 1cca063; 2026-10-29. Goal: aircraft profile schema 1 in a new `packages/aircraft`, validated from `unknown`, with a geometry layout and a deterministic derivation to vehicle manifest v2 (S03). TASK-087 is IN_PROGRESS, but the manifest v2 schema, validator and derived values that this task builds on are implemented and tested; its remaining increments, the native `quad.parm` and application registration, do not change the manifest. Propeller blade geometry and motor, ESC and battery values come from component cards (TASK-139), so the derivation takes them as resolved inputs and never invents them. On this machine Node 24.22.2 and Chromium 153 return different last bits for `Math.sin`, `Math.cos`, `Math.tan` and `Math.atan2` (1 ulp for 3–25% of arguments), so the derivation computes sines, cosines and tangents from basic arithmetic, which every engine rounds identically. Failure criterion: a derived rotor position, thrust axis or spin that disagrees with the independent oracle; an admitted profile with more than 8 arms or 16 motors, duplicate outputs, inconsistent pair geometry or non-physical inertia; or manifest bytes that differ between Node and Chromium for the same inputs. Touched Files additions, made before implementation: `packages/aircraft/src/trig.ts` for the engine-independent trigonometry; `tests/browser/aircraft-derivation.spec.ts` and `tests/support/aircraft-derivation.ts` for the Chromium byte check; `docs/architecture.md` and its mirror for the new package; and `CLAUDE.md` and its mirror for the status.
- **Title & Description:** (S03) Define the product-level aircraft profile on top of the vehicle manifest v2 (TASK-087). It covers one to eight arms with length, azimuth, height and tilt; one or two motors per arm with pair separation; and per-motor position, thrust axis and spin. It also holds mass, CG, inertia and payload, the battery, per-motor propulsion component references, a sensor-configuration reference and the motor-to-output mapping. Derive the manifest that physics consumes. Any change to geometry or components is a new version and needs a new run.
