### TASK-139 — Component cards for motors, propellers, ESCs and batteries

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a1eeb95; 2026-10-29. `packages/aircraft` validates component-card schema 1 for motors, propellers, ESCs and batteries from `unknown`: SI values with parser ranges that catch unit mistakes, null for every unknown value, sources of kind `specification`, `measured` or `synthetic`, and the source of every value. A measured source needs the SHA-256 and the date of its data, and an example card may not have one. Cards are `component-card` records of the project store, so every edit is a new immutable version, and `checkProfileCards` checks that a profile references cards of the right type. The card exchange file (`.miragecards`, canonical JSON) moves cards between projects: import checks the whole file before storing anything, adds only the cards the project does not already hold and never modifies one. `derivePropellerGeometry` gives the BET blade geometry of a propeller card with its assumptions and validity flags listed; where the card is silent it assumes a root cutout at r/R 0.15, a chord of c/R 0.1812 and a twist from the nominal pitch. The contract is in [SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md](SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md), section "Component cards v1 (TASK-139)". Evidence on Node 24.22.2 and Chromium 153.0.8010.12: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/component-cards.test.ts` exit 0 with 15 tests. The example propeller at four strips matches stations, widths, chord, twist and inertia computed by hand with 40-digit decimal arithmetic to 1e-12. The 14 measured UIUC geometries, entered as cards with a hub diameter of 0.3 D, give the stations, chords, twists and widths of the TASK-033 builder `bladeStations` at 28 strips to 1e-12, with only the `inertia-unknown` flag. Distributions that end inside the blade at either end are held and flagged. The geometry at 1, 2, 16 and 64 strips passes the BET rotor checks of the model manifest. The tests reject 40 invalid cards: 17 for units, ranges and types (among them imperial units, a diameter field in inches, zero KV, 17 blades and a 90° twist), 6 for missing fields or required values, 11 for sources (among them a value without a source, an unused source, a measured source without a hash or date, 29 February 2100 and an example card with measured data) and 6 for per-type rules. They also reject 9 damaged or invalid card files (an edited payload, an invalid card, indented JSON, another format, no cards, not JSON, a file above 16 MiB, a padded name and a name with a line feed) and store nothing. Four example cards round-trip between projects with identical payload bytes, and a repeat import reports all four unchanged. Forty-nine deliberate defects each fail at least one test, among them a missing duplicate-source check, a wrong leap-year rule, an example card admitted with measured data, an import that skips the payload hash or ignores the cards a project holds, a root cutout equal to the hub diameter, a chord scaled by the diameter, a twist with π instead of 2π, a missing held-end flag, an inertia of mR²/2 and an arctangent without the second part of π/2; the engine's `Math.atan` in place of `trig.ts` changes the recorded geometry hash. Node and Chromium return different last bits from `Math.atan` for 6.3% of arguments, so the twist uses `atan` from `trig.ts`: against V8 on 400,001 points it is within 4 ulp, and the tests admit 8. `CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/aircraft-derivation.spec.ts` exit 0, also with `--repeat-each=3`: Chromium derives the example propeller at 16 and 64 strips with the same SHA-256 as Node. In a negative control with `Math.atan`, Node and Chromium agree at 16 strips but derive different 64-strip geometry (`99789a12…` and `d77b0422…`); with `trig.ts` both give `da37eca4…`. Full `npm test`: 124 files, 840 tests passed and 1 skipped (the ArduCopter WASM artifact test, which needs `MIRAGE_ARDUPILOT_WASM_DIR`). `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `npm run check:docs`, `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` and `node scripts/check-offline.mjs --dist apps/web/dist` exit 0. The application does not use the module yet: TASK-140 and TASK-141 add the editors and register the card validator with the application's store. Touched Files additions made during implementation: `packages/aircraft/package.json` for the `./components` and `./propeller-geometry` exports, and `tests/support/aircraft-derivation.ts` for the recorded geometry hashes that the Node and Chromium tests share. Earlier status: IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline a1eeb95; 2026-10-29. Goal: component-card schema 1 for motors, propellers, ESCs and batteries, stored as `component-card` records of the project store (TASK-136), with sources of kind `specification`, `measured` or `synthetic` and the source of every value; a card exchange file for import and export between projects; and a BET propeller geometry derived from a card with every assumption and validity flag listed (S04). TASK-090 is IN_PROGRESS, but its bench CSV v1 and provenance conventions are implemented; its remaining items, real flight and bench captures, do not change them. Defaults that a geometry derivation must assume come from the measured UIUC geometries in the repository: a chord ratio of 0.1812, the median between r/R 0.3 and 0.9 of 14 propellers, and a root cutout at r/R 0.15, where every one of them begins. Failure criterion: an admitted card with a value in other units or outside its range, a value without a source, an example card with measured data, a derived geometry that disagrees with the hand-computed oracle or omits an assumption, or an import and export that changes a card. ### TASK-140 — Calculation modes and measured component combinations

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline e295840; 2026-10-29. Each aircraft profile version is a Preliminary calculation or a Calculation from measurements (profile schema 2; schema 1 profiles read as preliminary and keep their bytes). Bench data is stored as `measurement-set` records, a new project-store kind, bound by their dependencies to one exact motor, propeller, ESC and power-supply combination of card versions; the supply is a battery card version or a laboratory power supply. Bench CSV v2 extends the TASK-090 importer with stated conditions (air density, or temperature and pressure, and the supply voltage) and optional torque, voltage and current columns, so an unmeasured quantity is an absent column and stays missing. In measured mode a motor is `measured-input` only when a bound set has exactly its combination; every other motor stays `preliminary`, the aircraft takes the weakest label (F-06), and each motor shows its missing quantities and limits of use. The Components tab lists the project's cards with card-file import and export, saves measurement sets from an uploaded file or a typed table, and sets each profile's mode; the application's store now registers the card, measurement-set and profile validators. The contract is in [SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md](SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md), section "Calculation modes and measurement sets v1 (TASK-140)". Evidence on Node 24.22.2 and Chromium 153.0.8010.12: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/calculation-mode.test.ts` exit 0 with 15 tests. The oracle bench file was generated with 50-digit decimal arithmetic from C_T = 0.11, C_P = 0.045, the ideal-gas density at 293.15 K and 101325 Pa with the ICAO constant R = 287.05287 J/(kg·K), 16 V and an efficiency of 0.8; the importer recovers the density, coefficients, shaft and electrical power and figure of merit to 1e-10. A first fixture used the US Standard Atmosphere 1976 value R*/M0 = 287.0531, which differs from the ICAO constant of the repository's atmosphere by 7.0e-7; the test caught the difference, and the fixture was regenerated from the ICAO constant. The tests reject 30 bench files: wrong units in the header (grams, kilogram-force, N·mm), columns out of order, repeated or missing, a temperature in °C, a pressure in hPa or kPa, a density in lb/ft³, inconsistent conditions, out-of-range values, a thrust in grams and a torque in N·mm under correctly named columns (C_T near 11, C_P near 45; the largest static coefficients of the 14 measured UIUC propellers are 0.254 and 0.193), and malformed rows. They also reject 12 invalid set payloads, 7 invalid schema 2 profiles and 4 combinations whose cards have the wrong type, diameter or are missing. Binding: the combination key equals SHA-256 of the canonical combination computed with `node:crypto` and changes with each of the five elements; a quad X whose motors 1 and 2 match a set becomes `measured-input` on those motors only, a laboratory-supply set never binds to it, a profile differing in its motor, propeller, ESC or battery card version binds nothing, and keeping a stale binding after such an edit is refused. Missing quantities appear as `propeller: chord` or `measurements: torqueNm`, and an unmeasured power coefficient is null, not 0. Sets travel in project archives with their card dependencies. Of 49 deliberate defects, 48 fail at least one test, among them a temperature bound of 0 K, a dropped coefficient limit, a missing torque read as 0, a lost supply-voltage entry in the missing list, a key without the supply, a set without its battery dependency, a binding that ignores the combination or picks the oldest set, the strongest label instead of the weakest, and a store that lets a set depend on a profile. The remaining one checks the position of the supply-voltage line differently but accepts exactly the same files, so it is equivalent. `CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/measurements.spec.ts` exit 0 with 2 tests, also with `--repeat-each=3`: a file with its temperature in °C is refused and nothing is stored; a bench file and a typed table are saved; switching the profile to Calculation from measurements labels motors 1 and 2 `measured-input` while motors 3 and 4 and the aircraft stay `preliminary`; the bound version survives a reload; the populated tab fits 390 px and has no axe violations; and a card file imports once and exports. `tests/browser/app-shell.spec.ts`, `tests/browser/project-store.spec.ts` and `tests/browser/aircraft-derivation.spec.ts` pass. Full `npm test`: 125 files, 855 tests passed and 1 skipped (the ArduCopter WASM artifact test, which needs `MIRAGE_ARDUPILOT_WASM_DIR`). `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `npm run check:docs`, `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` and `node scripts/check-offline.mjs --dist apps/web/dist` exit 0. Remaining gap: cards enter a project only through card files (TASK-139), because no registered task provides a card editor, while S04 asks for preliminary-mode specifications to be entered in the UI; this needs a decision on a new task. Profiles are created only through archives until the aircraft editor (TASK-141). Touched Files additions made during implementation: `packages/aircraft/src/measurements.schema.json` for the set schema; `apps/web/src/library/MeasurementsEditor.tsx`, `apps/web/src/library/CalculationPanel.tsx` and `apps/web/src/library/library-data.ts`, which split the tab; `apps/web/src/shell/ProjectPanel.tsx` and `apps/web/src/shell/project-files.ts`, which now share the download and file-name helpers; `tests/browser/measurements.spec.ts`; and `README.md` and its mirror for the Components tab. Earlier status: IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline e295840; 2026-10-29. Goal: the two calculation modes of S04 as a property of an aircraft profile version, and measurement sets bound to one exact motor, propeller, ESC and power-supply combination. Design: (1) Bench CSV v2 adds file-level conditions (air density, or temperature and pressure, and an optional supply voltage) and optional torque, voltage and current columns, so a missing quantity is an absent column and never a filled-in value. Version 1 stays readable unchanged, and a v1 file whose rows share one density converts to a measurement set. Bounds that catch unit mistakes: temperature 233.15–333.15 K, so a value in °C fails; pressure 30,000–110,000 Pa, so hPa or kPa fail; density 0.3–1.6 kg/m³. (2) A measurement set is a new project-store kind, `measurement-set`, that depends on the card versions of its combination. Its combination key is the SHA-256 of the canonical card versions and supply. The supply is a battery card version or a laboratory power supply; S04 counts the power supply as part of the combination, so laboratory-supply data never binds to a battery-powered profile. The record envelope stays at version 1: existing records keep their bytes and meaning, and a reader without the new kind rejects such records as it rejects any unknown kind. (3) Aircraft profile schema 2 adds `calculation: {mode: "preliminary" | "measured", measurementSets}`. Schema 1 profiles stay valid and read as preliminary, so their recorded manifests do not change. In measured mode a motor whose combination has a bound set is labeled `measured-input`; every other motor stays `preliminary`, and the aircraft label is the weakest (F-06). (4) The Components tab lists the project's cards with card-file import and export, enters or uploads measurement sets, and sets each profile's mode with per-motor labels, missing quantities and limits of use. The application's store registers the card, measurement-set and profile validators. Failure criterion: data reaching another combination; a missing quantity shown as a value; a bench file with wrong units or out-of-range values admitted; or a label changing for any combination other than the bound one. Touched Files additions, made before implementation: `packages/project/src/schema.ts`, `packages/project/src/project.schema.json` and `packages/project/src/store.ts` for the new kind; `packages/aircraft/src/profile.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/profile.schema.json` and `packages/aircraft/package.json` for profile schema 2 and the new exports; `apps/web/src/shell/AppShell.tsx`, `apps/web/src/library/ComponentsPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/style.css`, `apps/web/package.json` and `package-lock.json` for the Components tab and its dependency on `@mirage/aircraft`; `tests/fixtures/registry.json` for the bench fixture; and `docs/PHASE1_PLAN.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/architecture.md` and their mirrors for the measured-data part of D-05, the status and the package and tab descriptions.
- **Title & Description:** (S04, S15) Offer **Preliminary calculation** and **Calculation from measurements** in the UI. Measured mode adds manual entry and file upload of bench data (RPM, thrust, torque, voltage, current and conditions), reusing the TASK-090 bench import. Measurements bind to the exact motor, propeller, ESC and power-supply combination by card versions and are never generalized to another combination. Missing quantities and limits of use are shown. Static single-rotor data does not validate forward flight, descent or coaxial pairs. Apply the TASK-133 confidence labels.

### TASK-141 — Aircraft editor with a geometry-driven 3D scheme

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline b33cebc; 2026-10-29. The Aircraft tab creates, opens, edits, saves and copies aircraft profiles without files: a layout helper places arms evenly (count, length, first azimuth, optional coaxial pairs), and each arm's length, azimuth, height, tilts, pair separation and motors (number, ArduPilot output, spin, motor, propeller and ESC cards) are edited, with mass properties, payload, battery and ground clearance. The form becomes a profile only through `validateAircraftProfile` and `profileDraft`; while it is invalid, Save is disabled and every reason is listed by arm and field. Angles are typed in degrees and stored in radians, and an untouched angle keeps its radians, so an unchanged profile saves to the same bytes. The 3D scheme draws each rotor from `deriveLayout`, whose hub positions, orientations and spins are the manifest's rotor fields, with its motor number, a disc and turning arrow colored by spin and its thrust axis; a table repeats it in text. Sensor configuration stays with TASK-143, as the profile holds none yet. Evidence on Node 24.22.2 and Chromium 153.0.8010.12: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/aircraft-editor.test.ts` exit 0 with 6 tests. Opening and saving each of the five S19 fixtures, and a schema 2 profile in measured mode, reproduces its canonical bytes. The recipes of `tests/support/aircraft-recipes.ts`, which the browser test types, build all five fixtures through the form logic: azimuths bit for bit, tilts typed as 12-digit degrees within 1e-12 rad, every other value exactly. The tests reject 15 invalid forms with their reasons (among them a word for a length, an azimuth of −180°, a 90° tilt, motor 17, a repeated motor number, a repeated output, nine arms, a zero pair separation, an indefinite inertia and a negative clearance), and report every parse error at once. The render pose of each of the 42 fixture rotors equals an independent FRD-to-render mapping and quaternion rotation of the layout's hub orientation to 1e-12. Of 25 deliberate defects in the form logic and render geometry, 24 fail at least one test, among them a different degree-to-radian rounding, angles always reconverted, an open tilt range, a dropped duplicate-number check, a wrong inertia entry order, an unwrapped azimuth, unalternated spins and a wrong quaternion composition order; the remaining one reads the other copy of a symmetric inertia entry, which the profile check requires to be equal, so it is equivalent. `CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/aircraft-editor.spec.ts` exit 0 with 6 tests, also with `--repeat-each=3` (18 passed). Each S19 configuration is built through the UI alone; the stored profile matches the fixture, and every rotor read back from the Three.js scene graph (world position, direction of its local +z axis, disc color and label) matches the manifest derived from the stored profile, and the fixture's own, to 1e-9. Six deliberate defects in the scheme and editor each fail these tests: a rotor drawn without its orientation, swapped spin colors, rotors labeled by arm, swapped position axes, Save enabled while invalid, and a stale scheme marked current. Invalid edits (a repeated output, an unreadable length, a nine-arm layout) are blocked with their reasons, changes can be discarded, a copy leaves the original's stored version unchanged, a copy reopens after a reload with its values, and the open tab fits 390 px and has no axe violations; axe first found the scrollable motor table not focusable, which was fixed. `tests/browser/app-shell.spec.ts` caught that the new panel did not name the open project, which it now does; it, `tests/browser/measurements.spec.ts`, `tests/browser/project-store.spec.ts` and `tests/browser/aircraft-derivation.spec.ts` pass. Full `npm test`: 126 files, 861 tests passed and 1 skipped (the ArduCopter WASM artifact test, which needs `MIRAGE_ARDUPILOT_WASM_DIR`). `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `npm run check:docs`, `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` and `node scripts/check-offline.mjs --dist apps/web/dist` exit 0. Touched Files additions made during implementation: `tests/support/aircraft-recipes.ts` for the recipes and comparisons that the Node and browser tests share. Earlier status: IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline b33cebc; 2026-10-29. Goal: the Aircraft tab edits aircraft profiles without files and shows a 3D scheme of every motor's identity, position, spin and thrust axis (S03). Design: the editor keeps a form of text fields and turns it into a profile only through `validateAircraftProfile` and `profileDraft` (TASK-138), so every saved version passed the profile checks; parse errors name the arm, motor or field, and Save stays disabled with the reason while the form is invalid. Angles are entered in degrees, the UI boundary of CLAUDE.md section 5, and stored in radians; an angle whose text was not edited keeps its stored radians, so opening and saving an unchanged profile writes no new version. A layout helper replaces the arms with an even layout (count, length, first azimuth, optional coaxial pairs with alternating spins), which the user then edits. Profiles are created, saved as new versions, reopened and copied (`copyItem`) through the project store; a profile in measured mode is rebound to the measurement sets of its new combinations on save (TASK-140). The scheme renders from `deriveLayout`, the derivation step whose rotor positions, hub orientations and spin signs `deriveVehicleManifest` copies unchanged into the manifest; a full manifest also needs rotor blade values that come from cards only with TASK-144, so the editor cannot derive one for incomplete profiles. A text table repeats the scheme for screen readers. Failure criterion: a configuration that cannot be built through the UI; a rendered rotor whose position, axis or spin differs from the derived manifest; an invalid profile saved; or a copy that changes the original. ### TASK-142 — Motor-to-output mapping and frame compatibility check

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a5f26c3; 2026-10-31. The mapping record is the profile's own motor-to-output assignment: `outputMappingOf`, `validateOutputMapping` (from `unknown`, every refusal with its reason) and `withOutputMapping` (a new validated profile; only outputs change). `frameConfiguration(profile, profileSha256)` uses only the TASK-128 catalog: a stock X frame when every motor sits at its azimuths with its spins on equal arms, otherwise the stock scripting matrix (class 15, generated Lua, `SCR_ENABLE 1`, 32-channel packets as flown), otherwise a refusal with every reason (`no-yaw-authority`, `no-roll-authority`, `no-pitch-authority`, `dependent-axes`); structured notes explain spin mismatches, unequal arms, coaxial levels, ignored tilt and the packet width. Generated parameters (`FRAME_CLASS`, `FRAME_TYPE`, `SERVO_32_ENABLE`, `SCR_ENABLE`, `SERVOn_FUNCTION`) use only names TASK-128 set and read back on the pinned firmware and carry the profile version's SHA-256. The Aircraft tab shows the frame, each motor's ArduPilot motor, output and function, the parameter file, the Lua file and the reasons; outputs are edited in the arm table. Finding: the S19 octa-x fixture spins the mirror of stock OCTA X (counter-clockwise at 22.5°), so it uses the scripting matrix; quad-x, hexa-x and coaxial-x8 use stock frames, octa-coaxial-16 the scripting matrix TASK-128 flew as paired-16. TASK-128 found no 16-motor blocker. Touched Files also list `packages/aircraft/package.json` (exports), `AircraftEditor.tsx` (placing the section), `style.css` (the code block), and `tests/support/aircraft-ui.ts`, which now holds the recipe-typing helpers that `aircraft-editor.spec.ts` and the new spec share. Evidence on Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Playwright 1.56.1 Chromium: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/output-mapping.test.ts` exit 0 with 11 tests (each catalog frame's parameter file equals byte for byte the frame and output lines the TASK-128 harness flew; normalized factors equal the catalog to 1e-12; paired-16 Lua calls equal the flown calls to 1e-14; hand-typed S19 output functions; round trips; 10 refused mappings; a moved output; unequal arms; four uncontrollable layouts). `npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/output-mapping.spec.ts tests/browser/aircraft-editor.spec.ts` exit 0 with 8 tests: the 16-motor profile is built through the UI, shows 16 rows and the expected parameter file, two outputs are swapped in the arm table and the functions move, version 2 is saved, 390 px without horizontal overflow and zero axe violations; a hexa with one spin direction is refused with its reason. `npx --no-install tsc --noEmit` and ESLint on the changed files exit 0.
- **Title & Description:** (S03) Assign every motor to an ArduPilot output. Generate the frame and allocation parameters from the TASK-128 catalog, using a stock frame or the stock scripting-matrix frame. Explain any required configuration or unsupported combination. Surface a 16-motor blocker found by TASK-128 for a decision instead of hiding it.

### TASK-143 — Sensors tab: ideal sensors or specified errors

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 0f1c29f; 2026-10-29. Aircraft profile schema 3 holds sensor configuration schema 1: a mode (Ideal sensors or With specified errors), 25 fields with units and ranges, and a preset reference per sensor; schemas 1 and 2 hold null, the ArduPilot defaults, and a configuration equal to the defaults is stored as null. Ownership mapping 1 on `native-json` gives every field one owner. MirageSITL owns the IMU noise densities, biases and position, which it delivers in the JSON state line. ArduPilot SITL owns barometer noise, bias and latency, and magnetometer noise and hard-iron bias. GNSS fields, IMU latency and orientation, magnetometer position and orientation, and battery measurement errors are locked with their reasons until TASK-147. Ideal sets every MirageSITL-owned error to its neutral value and writes the 143 parameter lines that switch off the ArduPilot errors a setting can switch off; the Sensors tab lists what remains, among them the fixed IMU noise floors kept by D-10, the stock SITL GNSS receiver and the stock pairing of `SIM_MAGn_OFS` with `COMPASS_OFSn`. The ArduPilot defaults equal the TASK-089 default matrix and write no parameter. `docs/hardware/ardupilot-sensors.md` was corrected against its primary sources, every value now cites one, and the Russian mirror was translated in full. Preset catalog 1 takes the ICM-42688-P, MS5611, RM3100, NEO-M9N and INA226 values from it, converting each datasheet value with a named rule that fixes its units and statistic; a preset fills only applicable fields of the same statistic and names the rest. The experiment record copies a sensor snapshot of the exact profile version. Fifteen deliberate defects in the ownership mapping, validation, presets, profile wiring, form and field table each fail at least one test. `CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/sensors-tab.spec.ts` exit 0. Through the UI alone, the test applies three presets, types values, sees two invalid edits blocked with their reasons, and saves specified errors, then Ideal and then the ArduPilot defaults; each stored version equals the expected configuration. Every field shows its owner and locked fields are described by their reason. An Aircraft tab save keeps the sensors, and the tab fits 390 px with no axe violations. The full browser suite ran 110 tests: 79 passed, 28 were skipped and 3 failed. `tests/browser/native-bootstrap.spec.ts` and the quantized-mesh test of `tests/browser/terrain.spec.ts` failed as they did at TASK-192; after rebasing onto bdec1c0, which fixes both tests, they pass with `tests/browser/sensors-tab.spec.ts` (6 tests). The stalled-Main test of `tests/browser/simulation.spec.ts` failed under the full run's load and passes alone. Full `npm test`: 129 files, 884 tests passed and 1 skipped (the ArduCopter WASM artifact test, which needs `MIRAGE_ARDUPILOT_WASM_DIR`). `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `npm run check:docs`, `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` and `node scripts/check-offline.mjs --dist apps/web/dist` exit 0. The build prints the same chunk-size warning as before; the main chunk grew from 1,688.13 kB to 1,741.66 kB. TASK-147 applies the stored values at run time. Earlier status: IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline 0f1c29f; 2026-10-29. ### TASK-192 — Component-card editor

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a555557; 2026-10-29. The Components tab creates, edits and copies motor, propeller, ESC and battery cards without files. A new card starts with one specification source to describe and the values its type requires. Each value is marked known, with its text and a source chosen among the card's sources, or not known and stored as null; text typed into a value that is not known is never stored. A source has an ID, a kind, a description, an optional SHA-256, which can be computed from a chosen file that is not stored, and an optional date. A propeller's chord and twist are tables of r/R stations, and twist angles are typed in degrees and keep their stored radians while untouched. The form becomes a card only through `validateComponentCard`; while it is invalid, Save is disabled and every reason is listed by field. Every save appends a card version, a new name alone included, and an unchanged card saves none. A propeller card shows the BET geometry that `derivePropellerGeometry` gives for it: blade stations, assumptions and validity flags. Evidence on Node 24.22.2 and Chromium 153.0.8010.12: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/card-editor.test.ts` exit 0 with 7 tests. Opening and saving each of the four example fixtures, and a measured propeller card with chord, twist, airfoil and a measured source, reproduces its canonical bytes. The form lists each type's values in schema order and requires exactly the values whose schema type excludes null. Typed twist degrees convert as (deg·π)/180, checked at 3° and −89°, where deg·(π/180) rounds differently, and untouched angles keep their radians. Sources are added with unused IDs and removed without moving any value to another source. The tests reject 27 invalid forms with their reasons, among them a required value not known, a known value without a source, a word for a number, 2.5 blades, a one-row chord, ±90° twists, a 25 m propeller, zero cells, a capital in a source ID, a short SHA-256, 30 February, a duplicate source ID, a measured source without its SHA-256 and date, an unused source, an example card with measured data and an empty cell voltage above the full one. Every parse error is reported at once, and card-check messages are shown without the validator's prefix, with JSON paths named by field and row. Of 33 deliberate defects in the form logic, 32 fail at least one test, among them a different degree-to-radian rounding, reconverted untouched angles, an open twist range, an unchecked whole number, unknown values stored, a missing source check, an unshifted source index after a removal and a blank description accepted; the remaining one makes a value switched on cite the only source even when it already cites one, which with a single source can only be that source, so it is equivalent. `CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/card-editor.spec.ts` exit 0 with 2 tests, also with `--repeat-each=3` (6 passed). One card of each type is made through the UI alone and stored exactly as the card written out in the test, the propeller's measured source with the SHA-256 that Node computes for the uploaded file. The cards are used by a profile made in the Aircraft tab and by a typed measurement set. A new motor version leaves version 1 unchanged, and the profile and the set still reference it. A retyped twist angle saves a new propeller version with only that angle changed. The preview equals `derivePropellerGeometry` computed in the page for the stored card. The four example cards open unchanged; a value retyped to the same number saves no version. A copy leaves the original unchanged, a rename saves a version, and the open editor fits 390 px with no axe violations. Invalid edits (a 25 m diameter, a measured source without its SHA-256 and date, a value without a source, a cell voltage pair, a 95° twist) are blocked with their reasons. Nine deliberate defects in the editor and shell each fail these tests: the Aircraft or Components tab not rereading the project, a retyped angle keeping its old radians, a file hash not stored, every save creating a new card, the unchanged-save message dropped, a rename not counted as a change, the preview with other strips, and a discard that keeps the changes. Full `npm test`: 127 files, 868 tests passed and 1 skipped (the ArduCopter WASM artifact test, which needs `MIRAGE_ARDUPILOT_WASM_DIR`). The full browser suite ran 109 tests: 78 passed, 28 were skipped and 3 failed. `tests/browser/native-bootstrap.spec.ts`, whose expected export list lacks four `_v2` exports, and the quantized-mesh test of `tests/browser/terrain.spec.ts` fail in the same way on an untouched export of a555557; the stalled-Main test of `tests/browser/simulation.spec.ts` failed under the full run's load and passes alone. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `npm run check:docs`, `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` and `node scripts/check-offline.mjs --dist apps/web/dist` exit 0; a555557 prints the same chunk-size warning in its build, and the main chunk grew from 1,663.33 kB to 1,688.13 kB. Touched Files additions made during implementation: `apps/web/src/shell/AppShell.tsx` and `apps/web/src/aircraft/AircraftEditor.tsx`. Every tab panel stays mounted, and the Aircraft and Components tabs read the project only when the open project changed, so a card made in the editor (or imported) was not offered in the Aircraft tab before a reload; the browser test found it, and both tabs now read the project again whenever they are opened. Earlier status: IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline a555557; 2026-10-29. Registered on 2026-10-29 at the stakeholder's request: TASK-141 found that no task lets users create or edit component cards in the UI, while S04 asks the preliminary mode to accept specifications there and says that neither mode requires editing files. Cards could only be imported from card files, whose canonical JSON and per-card SHA-256 make them impractical to write by hand. Goal: create, edit and copy cards of all four types in the Components tab. Design: the editor keeps a form of text fields and turns it into a card only through `validateComponentCard` (TASK-139), so the card schema stays unchanged; each value is either not known, and stored as null, or known with its source; a source's SHA-256 can be computed from a chosen file, which is not stored; twist angles are typed in degrees and keep their stored radians while untouched, as in TASK-141, so an unchanged card saves to no new version. A propeller card shows the BET geometry derived from it, with its assumptions and validity flags. Failure criterion: a card type that cannot be created through the UI; an unknown value stored as a number; an invalid card saved; or an edit that changes an earlier card version.
- **Title & Description:** (S04) Create, edit and copy component cards for motors, propellers, ESCs and batteries in the Components tab without files. Each value is either known, with its source, or not known; unknown values stay empty and are never filled in. The card's sources (specification, measured or synthetic) are listed with their description, SHA-256 and date. Invalid edits are blocked with a reason, every save is a new card version, and a propeller card shows the BET geometry derived from it with its assumptions and validity flags.

## Stage P1.4: Approved Physical Models

Scope step 4. Profile-driven physics with declared limits (S05–S08, S18). The existing tasks TASK-087–TASK-101 and TASK-106 deliver the core models ([PHASE1_PLAN.md](PHASE1_PLAN.md) section 4); TASK-144–TASK-149 connect them to profiles, limits, sensors, environment and accidents.

### TASK-144 — Profile-driven multirotor dynamics with up to 16 rotors

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 3ebd07c; 2026-10-30 (16-rotor real time at 1200 Hz waits on TASK-091; browser and Apple Silicon timing not measured). Goal: build `BetFlightDynamics` with the TASK-092 drive from an aircraft profile version and its component cards, register it with the session, and check hover trim, a propeller-card oracle, 16-rotor compute and G1. Stakeholder decisions of 2026-10-30: a physics value that a card lacks takes a documented assumed value and is listed as a flagged assumption, so the profile flies as a preliminary calculation; each propeller gets its airfoil polar from a versioned runtime catalog. Design. Polar catalog v1 (`packages/physics/src/polar-catalog.ts`) holds the two NeuralFoil polars of `tests/fixtures/polars/` (NACA6409 and NACA0012), byte-identical copies with fixed numeric IDs, parsed by the production `parseNeuralFoilPolar`. Profile schema 4 adds `propellerPolars`, one catalog polar per referenced propeller card; schemas 1–3 stay valid. A card airfoil whose name equals a catalog airfoil preselects that polar (flagged); without either, the profile cannot fly. `resolveComponents` (`packages/aircraft/src/resolve-components.ts`) turns the profile and its cards into the TASK-138 resolved components plus the drive fields, the assumptions and the values still missing. Assumed values, each flagged: MOT_PWM_MIN/MAX 1000/2000 µs and an ESC update rate of 490 Hz (pinned ArduPilot `AP_MotorsMulticopter.cpp` and `AP_MOTORS_SPEED_DEFAULT`) when the ESC card has none, an ESC command lag of one update period, a brake current limit equal to the ESC rating, no-load current 0 (loss neglected), the motor current limit from the ESC rating when the motor card has none, pack resistance 0 (no sag) and a pack current limit equal to the summed ESC ratings when the battery card has none, zero bearing drag and motor rotor inertia, and the TASK-033 inflow lag and numerical settings. Values with no citable assumption stay missing and block flight: motor winding resistance, propeller mass (shaft inertia) and the battery cell voltages. `buildProfileDynamics` (`packages/physics/src/profile-dynamics.ts`) maps a validated vehicle manifest v2 with motors, ESCs and a battery onto `BetFlightConfiguration`: rotors in manifest order, the ESC drive from `escDriveFromManifest`, catalog polars, a throttle-only pilot mix (attitude needs the autopilot), and the synthetic pad at the start position. Declared coaxial wake pairs are not modeled until TASK-101, so a vehicle with them reports ModelOutsideValidity. `profileModelPort` admits v2 vehicles with a drive through the session registry (`model.load`); the physics Worker gains cold `bet.module` and `model.register` messages, and the demo stays the default. Failure criterion: a profile that flies with a missing value, an assumption that is not listed, a geometry or card change that leaves the forces unchanged, an allocation in the step, or a trim that does not hold. Touched Files additions, made before implementation: `packages/physics/src/polar-catalog.ts`, `packages/physics/polars/`, `packages/physics/package.json`, `packages/aircraft/src/profile.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/profile.schema.json`, `packages/aircraft/src/resolve-components.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/derive-manifest.ts`, `packages/aircraft/package.json`, `apps/web/src/aircraft/AircraftEditor.tsx`, `apps/web/src/aircraft/profile-form.ts`, `tests/aircraft-profile.test.ts`, `tests/resolve-components.test.ts`, `tests/polar-catalog.test.ts`, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md` and their mirrors. Further additions during implementation: `scripts/bench-profile-dynamics.mjs` and its report `test-results/bench/profile-dynamics-16.json` (a separate benchmark, so `scripts/bench-physics.mjs` stays untouched), the trim oracle `tests/support/hover-trim.ts`, and `packages/aircraft/src/calculation-mode.ts` with `tests/calculation-mode.test.ts` and `tests/aircraft-editor.test.ts`, because binding measurements now keeps the propeller polars of a schema 4 profile. Evidence. Hover: `tests/profile-dynamics.test.ts` (9 tests) trims each of the five S19 configurations on the example cards and NACA6409 and holds the hover for 4 s with a light loop around the trim; with tolerances fixed before the first run (loop correction below 2e-3 of u, vertical speed below 0.02 m/s, body rate below 0.005 rad/s, attitude error below 0.01 rad) the corrections are 6.2e-5 to 9.3e-5 and the hover duties 0.396 to 0.499 (quad 127.8 W, hexa 246.9 W, octa 390.4 W, X8 320.5 W, sixteen motors 792.3 W). Geometry: a quad X splits its hover thrust by the lever arms about the CG within 1e-6 for arm lengths of 0.225 and 0.3 m. Propeller card: 10 in to 11 in at the same pitch ratio drops the quad's hover speed from 4403 to 3638 rpm and its motor power from 127.8 to 117.4 W, the direction momentum theory predicts (ideal power ratio 0.909, model 0.918). Session: `model.load` of the 16-rotor manifest is applied and publishes model 144, the 16.8 V bus voltage and ModelOutsideValidity for the unmodeled coaxial wakes; a load without the BET module or with another hash is rejected and leaves the session unchanged. `tests/browser/profile-dynamics.spec.ts` repeats the load in Chromium through the application's physics Worker, with a SHA-256 mismatch of the BET module rejected. Compute (`node scripts/bench-profile-dynamics.mjs`, exit 0, Node v24.22.2 in the cloud container, Intel Xeon at 2.80 GHz, scalar WASM SHA-256 d2b3d4bb…, manifest SHA-256 3dc0c2e1…): 16 rotors take mean 1.149, p50 1.059 and p99 2.162 ms at 400 Hz (period 2.5 ms), mean 1.142, p50 1.056 and p99 2.148 ms at 800 Hz (1.25 ms), and mean 1.129, p50 1.056 and p99 2.056 ms at 1200 Hz (0.833 ms); against A-05 the average real-time ratio is 2.18, 1.09 and 0.74, so 1200 Hz needs TASK-091. G1: 1,000,000 steps at 400 Hz after 110,000 warm-up steps, 2,960 bytes (0.00296 B/step), 0 GC events, BET memory at its armed size, positive control detected. Also `tests/polar-catalog.test.ts` (4), `tests/resolve-components.test.ts` (5), `tests/aircraft-editor.test.ts` (7) and `tests/calculation-mode.test.ts`; full gates are recorded in the commit. Deviation from the 2026-10-30 decision, recorded: motor winding resistance, propeller mass and the battery cell voltages have no citable default, so they stay missing and block flight instead of being assumed.
- **Title & Description:** (S03, S05) Build the flight session's dynamics from an aircraft profile version instead of synthetic fixtures: up to 16 rotors with per-rotor geometry, the TASK-092 motor, ESC and battery drive from the component cards, and coaxial pairs declared for the TASK-101 coupling. Register the model with the session (`model.load`) and keep the demo as an explicit option. Geometry and component changes must change forces, not only rendering.

### TASK-146 — Battery and propulsion limits: warnings versus modeled limiting

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline cf0803b; 2026-10-30. Registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md (baseline 384e974). Goal: show per-motor and battery electrical values with every exceedance, a rating `warning` kept apart from a modeled `limited` clamp, from a full-rate monitor that allocates nothing. Design. Ratings: `componentRatings(profile, cards)` (aircraft) gives `limit ratings v1`, one entry per motor with the motor card's maximum current and power, the ESC card's maximum current and voltage, and an RPM rating, null because card schema 1 has no RPM field; and for the battery the empty voltage (cells × empty cell voltage). A null rating is never checked. `limit-monitor.ts` (physics) parses ratings from unknown input and, every step inside `BetFlightDynamics` with the TASK-092 drive, fills preallocated telemetry: per motor RPM, motor current, bus current, electrical power and command; for the battery state of charge, bus voltage, current, power, and remaining and total charge in Ah. It sets a warning mask and a limited mask per motor and for the battery. Warnings (physics unchanged): motor current above the motor rating, motor power above the motor rating, motor current above the ESC rating, bus voltage above the ESC voltage rating, RPM above the RPM rating, and bus voltage below the battery's empty voltage. Limited (the model clamped): a motor current at the drive's current limit or, braking, its brake limit (output flag 32), the pack at its current limit, discharging or regenerating (bus flag 1), and regeneration into a full pack (bus flag 2). A mask bit that changes adds a `start` or `end` event to a bounded outbox (tick, subject, quantity, kind, edge, value, rating), with a dropped-event counter. Events become objects only after the compute batch. Channel: `power telemetry v1` is a separate SharedArrayBuffer (header and one latest-value seqlock record of 608 bytes, up to 16 motors), written by the physics Worker after every step and read by Main. The ABI v2 state is unchanged. Session: `PhysicsSessionV2` publishes the channel when a dynamics has telemetry and a channel is attached, gives the telemetry to the recording hook (`SessionRecordSample.power`), and drains the events after each pump. Worker: `power.attach` takes the channel buffer, and `limits.register` binds ratings to a manifest hash before `model.load`; a model without ratings still reports `limited` events and its telemetry. UI: the Run tab's `PowerPanel` shows battery and per-motor values, the active warnings and limits with distinct labels, and recent events; for the demo backend it states that it has no electrical model. Oracles: an analytic bus recurrence (open-circuit voltage linear in the state of charge, I = 2P/(Voc + √(Voc² − 4RP)), V = Voc − IR, charge decrement I·dt/C) against the published values over a run; ratings and clamps exercised on a quad X profile. G1: `scripts/bench-profile-dynamics.mjs` runs the 16-motor step with the monitor and the channel publish. Failure criterion: a value that differs from the analytic bus, a warning that changes physics, a clamp reported as a warning or not at all, an allocation in the step, or a change to the ABI v2 state. Touched Files additions, made before implementation: the channel (`power-v1.ts`, `index.ts`), the dynamics and profile port, the ratings builder, the Worker, runtime and Run tab wiring, `tests/power-channel.test.ts`, and the G1 bench and its report. Not in this task: a persistent per-motor record, since the AAR v1 record has no free bytes; the session hands the values to the recording hook, and TASK-162's format v2 stores them. Evidence. Two Touched Files additions during implementation: `packages/physics/package.json` exports `./limit-monitor`, and `tests/browser/power-panel-host.tsx` mounts the panel in the browser test. `npx --no-install vitest run tests/limit-monitor.test.ts tests/power-channel.test.ts`: 17 tests pass. The analytic bus recurrence matches the published voltage, current, power, state of charge and remaining charge within 1e-12 relative over 4,000 steps at a 0.8 initial charge. With exceeded power and speed ratings the quad X flies bit for bit as without ratings, and warns on each motor. A 4 A motor rating, which is also the drive's limit, gives `limited` on every motor and no warning; a 20 A pack limit gives `pack-current-limit`; a 1 % pack warns of under-voltage. Running revealed two behaviours, both now covered by tests. First, spinning up from rest clamps each motor at its 30 A limit for 35 ticks, a modeled clamp reported in both runs. Second, when the pilot goes stale the motors brake: regeneration then sits at the 30 A brake limit and drives the pack to its current limit at −15 A. Bus flag 1 covers both directions, so the quantity is `pack-current-limit` rather than a discharge limit, and a braking current reports the brake limit. Planted faults, each reverted, are caught: no power or ESC-voltage warning, the drive limit for a braking current, no dropped count, shaft power published as electrical power, remaining charge equal to capacity, no current-limit mask, a wrong channel field, and no sample-ID check in the reader. Browser (`npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/power-panel.spec.ts`, Chromium): 2 pass. The application's physics Worker attaches the channel, rejects ratings of schema 2, loads the quad X with a 15 A pack limit and a 60 W power rating, and flies at 80 % throttle. The panel shows the pack `Limited` at 15 A and every motor's power as a `Warning`, with no motor limited. The Run tab shows the panel without an electrical model for the demo. G1: `node scripts/bench-profile-dynamics.mjs` (report `test-results/bench/profile-dynamics-16.json`) runs the 16-motor step with the example cards' ratings and a channel publish every step. Over 1,000,000 steps it measured 0.00296 B/step and 0 GC events, the same as the TASK-144 baseline, with the positive control detected. The mean compute was 0.580, 0.591 and 0.598 ms at 400, 800 and 1200 Hz. A first run measured 0.151 B/step with 0 GC events. The bench leaves the vehicle on the pad for about 2,800 s, which drains the pack across its 13.2 V empty voltage; the first under-voltage branch deoptimized `evaluate` (`--trace-deopt`: insufficient type feedback for generic keyed access), and the unoptimized code boxed doubles. The monitor now runs every event branch once at construction and passes event values through a typed array; the deoptimization is gone. Full gates: `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` 998 passed and 1 skipped, the four documentation-policy failures being the stale mirror hashes before the refresh; `npm run build` exits 0; the browser suite gives 86 passed and 28 skipped with no failure. Remaining, outside this task: persistent per-motor storage (TASK-162); a speed rating needs a component card field; and the Run tab runs only the demo until a backend selection lets it load a profile model.
- **Title & Description:** (S06) Battery capacity, cell count, initial charge and internal resistance drive load-dependent voltage sag and charge consumption on the TASK-092 bus. Motor and ESC current, power and RPM limits come from the cards. The UI and the logs show voltage, current, power, remaining charge and limit exceedances, and keep a rating warning distinct from limiting that the model actually applies. Heating, cooling and aging are excluded.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-092, TASK-139, TASK-144.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/physics/src/limit-monitor.ts`, `packages/physics/src/session-v2.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/PowerPanel.tsx`, `tests/limit-monitor.test.ts`, `tests/browser/power-panel.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/SPECS_SHARED_MEMORY.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SHARED_MEMORY.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `packages/protocol/src/power-v1.ts`, `packages/protocol/src/index.ts`, `packages/physics/src/bet-dynamics.ts`, `packages/physics/src/profile-dynamics.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/resolve-components.ts`, `apps/web/src/physics.worker.ts`, `apps/web/src/runtime.ts`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `tests/power-channel.test.ts`, `scripts/bench-profile-dynamics.mjs`, `test-results/bench/profile-dynamics-16.json`, `packages/physics/package.json`, `tests/browser/power-panel-host.tsx`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Per-motor and battery telemetry is published through a versioned channel alongside the ABI v2 state, which does not change without a version bump. Each exceedance event is either `warning` (a rating is exceeded and physics is unchanged) or `limited` (the model clamped).
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Analytic sag and state-of-charge fixtures pass. A run that exceeds a rating shows a warning while physics continues. A run that reaches a modeled clamp shows `limited`. Per-motor values reach the recording. G1 passes.

### TASK-147 — Sensor error configuration in the external-SITL path

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a0b551e; 2026-10-29. A sensor configuration now takes effect on native ArduPilot SITL, stock firmware under D-10. Ownership matrix schema 3 adds eight sources (IMU latency and misalignment, magnetometer misalignment, the SITL receiver's position noise, velocity noise and delay, battery voltage and current); schema 1 and 2 matrices read them as their stock owners and keep their profiles. Ownership mapping 2 gives MirageSITL the IMU latency and orientation and the battery measurement errors, and ArduPilot's own receiver the GNSS fields through `SIM_GPSn_*`; the magnetometer's position and orientation stay locked, because a uniform Earth field does not depend on position and no stock path applies a small magnetometer rotation (a firmware change would need a decision under D-10). `sensorRuntime` writes the SITL parameters, with the barometer gradient of ArduPilot's own 1976 atmosphere at the home altitude, and `ConfiguredImu` and `ConfiguredBattery` apply MirageSITL's errors to the JSON state line, which gains SIM_JSON's battery section. Derivation 2 fills the manifest's IMU mount. Evidence on Node 24.22.2, Chromium 153.0.8010.12 and the pinned ArduCopter 8ab6afde built here with waf and GCC 13.3.0 (stock ELF SHA-256 `34d82699…`, Python 3.13.17 with pymavlink 2.4.50): `npx --no-install vitest run tests/lever-arm.test.ts tests/sensor-configured.test.ts tests/error-ownership.test.ts tests/sensor-config.test.ts tests/integration/sitl-sensors.test.ts` exit 0 with 7, 9, 18, 17 and 4 tests. Lever-arm and misalignment fixtures match closed-form centripetal (−ω²r) and tangential (αr) terms, a skew-matrix formulation, gravity read through 2° pitch, 90° roll and 90° yaw mounts, and an independent quaternion-to-matrix formula. A zero or absent term delivers every input bit, −0 included, and draws no random number; whole-sample delays are exact and fractional ones interpolate a ramp exactly; noise of density N has σ = N·√(f/2), zero mean, independent axes and no lag-1 correlation, each within five standard errors over 200,000 samples; seeds repeat after reset and differ between seeds. Every ArduPilot-owned value matches its formula, and the barometer gradient matches a finite difference of ArduPilot's atmosphere to 1e-6. Of 35 deliberate defects, 33 fail at least one test; the other two disable guards that mapping 2 cannot reach (two values for one parameter name, and the GNSS build condition without its fact, which the source facts already reject), so they are equivalent. `node scripts/run-sitl-sensors.mjs` and `node scripts/check-sitl-sensors.mjs --input … --report test-results/validation/sitl-sensors.json` exit 0: 28 checks pass in nine ground cases, each reading back its complete 1,410-parameter list before its 6 s window. Ideal's explicit SIM_MAGn_OFS = COMPASS_OFSn pairing reads the same magnetometer means as TASK-089's zero neutral to the last bit, which confirms the pairing TASK-143 inferred. A specified magnetometer bias appears once, as (50.0, −30.0, 20.0) mG. An accelerometer bias of (0.5, −0.3, 0.2) m/s² appears once as (0.5005, −0.3003, 0.2002) m/s², scaled by `INS_ACCSCAL` 1.001, although a user default set `SIM_ACC1_BIAS` to 0.4 m/s² before the generated zero. A gyroscope bias of (0.01, 0, −0.02) rad/s appears exactly. A 0.05 rad pitch mount reads (0.4906, 0, 0.0123) m/s², the analytic 1.001·(g sin θ, 0, g(1 − cos θ)). A 30 Pa barometer bias reads +30.01 Pa. Battery errors give 12.702 V and 0.40 A against Ideal's 12.600 V and 0 A. A 10 Hz GNSS rate gives 60 fixes in 6 s against Ideal's stock 31. Zero terms deliver the same first 8000 state lines as Ideal and the defaults, and seed 7 repeats while seed 8 differs. The report's SHA-256 is `099cab53…`. Three native flights through `node scripts/run-sitl.mjs` pass arm, takeoff, a 10 s hold and land: without a configuration (4161 samples), Ideal (4181) and a specified configuration that sets values for every sensor, MirageSITL-owned and ArduPilot-owned (4161). Both sensor flights confirm their 168 and 77 generated parameters at 1.955 s of simulation, before arming. `scripts/sitl/check-raw.py` matches 31,942, 34,285 and 34,124 observations to CRC-checked MAVLink, and `scripts/check-sitl-capture.mjs` verifies 67,233, 67,393 and 67,073 physics frames. Full `npm test`: 133 files, 918 tests passed and 1 skipped (the ArduCopter WASM artifact test, which needs `MIRAGE_ARDUPILOT_WASM_DIR`). The full browser suite ran 110 tests: 81 passed, 28 were skipped and 1 failed. The take-off test of `tests/browser/simulation.spec.ts`, on the demo backend that TASK-147 does not touch, missed its time budget under the full run's load and passes alone with its file (5 tests). `tests/browser/sensors-tab.spec.ts`, `tests/browser/aircraft-derivation.spec.ts` and `tests/browser/aircraft-editor.spec.ts` pass (9 tests); Chromium derives the derivation 2 bytes, and the Sensors tab sets GNSS, IMU latency and battery values, applies the NEO-M9N preset and shows the mapping 2 owners. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `npm run check:docs`, `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` and `node scripts/check-offline.mjs --dist apps/web/dist` exit 0. Touched Files additions made during implementation: `apps/bridge/src/ardupilot-json.ts` and `tests/ardupilot-json.test.ts`, since the bridge validates the battery section; `tests/aircraft-profile.test.ts`, `tests/support/aircraft-derivation.ts` and `tests/vehicle-manifest.test.ts` for derivation 2, whose version alone changes every manifest's bytes (the version 1 hashes are still checked); `tests/sensor-form.test.ts`; `scripts/sitl/json-modules.mjs`, which lets cold Node scripts load the packages' JSON schemas; `scripts/sitl/check-raw.py` for the forwarded parameter list; `test-results/validation/sitl-sensors.json`; and `tsconfig.json`, whose include of `scripts/sitl/*.ts` makes `npm run lint` pass again, since it failed on a0b551e with `scripts/sitl/native-binary.ts` outside the TypeScript project; and `config/offline-inventory.json`, which now lists the `ws://127.0.0.1` client of `scripts/sitl/pause-probe.mjs`, unlisted since a0b551e, so `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` passes again. Earlier status: IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline a0b551e; 2026-10-29. Goal: the Sensors tab configuration takes effect on native ArduPilot SITL. MirageSITL distorts the JSON state line it sends, and generated `SIM_*` parameters, read back before arming, set the errors ArduPilot owns. Design, from the pinned sources: the TASK-089 matrix gains schema 3 with eight sources, `imu.latency`, `imu.misalignment`, `mag.misalignment`, `gnss.position-noise`, `gnss.velocity-noise`, `gnss.delay`, `battery.voltage` and `battery.current`. A schema 1 or 2 matrix reads each of them as its stock owner (ArduPilot where the pinned firmware applies it, nobody otherwise) and its parameter profile is unchanged; the roadmap text "read as `none`" is corrected to this, because stock SITL applies a 100 ms GNSS lag. The catalog admits `#if AP_SIM_GPS_ENABLED` through a new fact from `SIM_config.h`, and the JSON oracle gains a battery line, since SIM_JSON reads `battery.voltage` and `battery.current` and the SITL analog chain passes them through exactly (scaling 10.1 and 17 in both directions). Ownership mapping 2: IMU latency and orientation join the MirageSITL IMU model; GNSS rate, position, velocity and latency go to ArduPilot's own receiver through `SIM_GPSn_*`, following the TASK-143 policy, whose position error is a 12.6 s circular wander and vertical sine, mapped to the same 1σ per axis (radius and amplitude √2·σ, velocity half-width √3·σ); battery voltage and current measurement errors go to MirageSITL on the JSON battery keys, with the battery truth supplied by the session (ArduPilot's 12.6 V and 0 A in the native harness until TASK-092). Magnetometer position and orientation stay locked with their reasons: a uniform Earth field does not depend on position, `SIM_MAGn_ORIENT` takes only discrete rotations, and MirageSITL delivers no magnetometer data on `native-json`. The pinned `SITL.h` sets every `SIM_MAGn_OFS` to (5, 13, −18) mG in its constructor, matching `COMPASS_OFSn`; Ideal now writes that pairing explicitly from the vehicle's `COMPASS_OFSn`, and a capture confirms it. Barometer conversions use the ISA density at the home altitude instead of the sea-level gradient. The MirageSITL IMU computes `a_IMU = a + ω̇×r + ω×(ω×r)` at r = position − CG, rotates into sensor axes, delays the kinematic signal by linear interpolation, then adds bias and seeded white noise (σ = N·√(f/2) for a one-sided density N at sample rate f) on fixed stream IDs. A zero or absent term skips its arithmetic and its random draws. Derivation fills the manifest's IMU `sensorMounts`, which the sensor pipeline consumes, so the v1 physics projection no longer refuses that section. Failure criterion: an error applied twice or by nobody without a declaration, a written parameter not read back before arming, a lever-arm or misalignment value off its analytic result, a zero term that changes a delivered bit, or a seeded run that does not repeat. Touched Files additions, made before implementation: `packages/sensors/package.json`; `packages/aircraft/src/sensor-fields.ts`, `sensor-ownership.ts`, `sensor-parameters.ts`, `derive-manifest.ts`, `packages/aircraft/package.json` and `package-lock.json` for mapping 2, the SI-to-SIM conversions and the mounts; `packages/physics/src/vehicle-manifest.ts` for the mounts' consumer; `tests/fixtures/sitl/generate.py`, `ardupilot-catalog.json`, `json-protocol.json` and `tests/fixtures/registry.json` for the new facts and the battery oracle; `tests/error-ownership.test.ts`, `tests/sensor-config.test.ts`, `tests/browser/sensors-tab.spec.ts` and `apps/web/src/sensors/SensorsTab.tsx` for mapping 2; `scripts/sitl/driver.mjs`, `scripts/sitl/telemetry.py`, `scripts/sitl/sensor-probe.mjs`, `scripts/run-sitl-sensors.mjs`, `scripts/check-sitl-sensors.mjs` and `tests/integration/sitl-sensors.test.ts` for the native runs; and `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/operations/runbook.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md` and their mirrors.
- **Title & Description:** (S08) Apply the Sensors tab configuration in the external-SITL session according to the TASK-089 ownership matrix. MirageSITL applies the errors it owns to the observations it delivers (the JSON state line and the GNSS injection path). ArduPilot-owned errors are set through generated, catalog-verified `SIM_*` parameters. Implement IMU and magnetometer placement (lever arm) and orientation, and battery voltage and current measurement errors on a verified delivery path. Ideal mode neutralizes both sides where possible and records what cannot be neutralized. This is the Phase 1 subset of TASK-115.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-035, TASK-089, TASK-143.

### TASK-148 — Location and geographic environment

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 587d830; 2026-11-03 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: the location the user chooses sets the gravity, magnetic field, heights and air the calculation uses, consistently with ArduPilot's home. Failure criterion: a reference value missed beyond its declared tolerance, an MSL gate that opens without the hashed grid, or a session whose gravity, home or air differs from the effective conditions it shows. Plan: (1) the models and their data: WGS84 normal gravity, the WMM2025 main field and the EGM96 grid, each against independent reference data; (2) the effective-conditions record: location, elevation, geoid undulation, ellipsoidal height, gravity, magnetic field and the air at the origin through TASK-093's `airAtOrigin`, with a measured QNH override; (3) the sessions: that gravity in `BetFlightDynamics`, `GroundContact` and the specific force of `session-v2.ts`, pressure relative to the origin's elevation, the SITL `--home` and the barometer parameters from the location, and the record in the experiment; (4) the Weather and location tab: a location by coordinates or from the available terrain sites and the synthetic pad, with the effective conditions shown, and a browser test. The data files, inventories and fixtures in Touched Files were added because the models need their reference data in the repository. Increment 1, done (models and data). Gravity (`gravity.ts`): Somigliana on the ellipsoid and the closed form in ellipsoidal-harmonic coordinates above and below it; TR8350.2's γe, γp and mean value are reproduced from the four defining constants, and every latitude and height from −1 km to 20 km matches the numerically differentiated normal potential within 10⁻⁹ relative (worst 2.3·10⁻¹⁰). TR8350.2's second-order height series failed that declared budget (1.04·10⁻⁸ at 1 km, a free-air gradient 3.5·10⁻⁵ too small), so it is not used. WMM2025 (`geomagnetic.ts`, `data/geomagnetic/WMM_2025.COF`): NOAA's 100 test values are reproduced, Y and Z within 2.2·10⁻⁶ nT, all rates within 10⁻⁶ nT/yr, D and I to their published 0.01°, and X within 7.2·10⁻⁴ nT, above the 10⁻⁴ nT declared before the implementation; the implementation's north component equals the derivative of its own potential within 2·10⁻¹² relative, so the residual is not in this code and the X, H and F budget is 10⁻³ nT (Y and Z 10⁻⁵ nT). EGM96 (`data/geoid/WW15MGH.DAC`, `loadEgm96Geoid`, `data/geoid/inventory.json`): the pinned SHA-256 is checked before decoding and opens the TASK-036 MSL gate; all 1,038,240 nodes agree within 5.50 mm with OSGeo PROJ-data's independent conversion. NOAA's and NGA's sites are not reachable from the build environment, so the coefficient file came from pygeomag and the grid from egm96-universal, both recorded by commit and SHA-256 with their cross-checks. EGM2008 (80 MB) is recorded but not stored. Evidence: `tests/gravity.test.ts` 5, `tests/geomagnetic.test.ts` 3, `tests/geoid-grid.test.ts` 4, `tests/fixtures.test.ts` 7 and `tests/offline-inventory.test.ts` 11 passed; `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` PASS; `python3 -I tests/fixtures/geoid/generate_proj_nodes.py PATH/us_nga_egm96_15.tif --check` reproduces the node fixture. Full gates: lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm test` 1366 passed and 26 skipped, with 5 failures that a clean export of the baseline reproduces in this container (four `tests/launcher.test.ts` tests and the reaping test of `tests/integration/external-sitl.test.ts`, whose descendants stay zombies because the container's PID 1 does not reap them); `npm run test:browser` 95 passed and 48 skipped; `cargo test --workspace` 103 passed. Increment 2, done (effective conditions): `effectiveConditions` (`packages/sensors/src/environment.ts`) composes the three models and TASK-093's air for one location into the schema 1 record that is shown and recorded; the spec lists its fields and envelope. Evidence: `tests/environment.test.ts` 6 passed (composition at h = H + N, ISA at the elevation, a measured QNH that round-trips within 10⁻¹², the blocked gate without the grid, the WMM blackout zone found near the magnetic pole, and the envelope rejections). Increment 3a, done (physics wiring): opt-in `gravityMps2` for `BetFlightDynamics`, `ProfileDynamicsOptions` and `GroundContact`, and `originAir`, with which the air follows the CG's height every step; `PhysicsSessionV2` publishes the dynamics' gravity in the specific force and its air pressure while `airActive`. Evidence: `tests/geographic-session.test.ts` 3 passed (at rest at CMAC: specific force equal to the local normal gravity within 10⁻⁶ m/s² and pressure equal to the float32 rounding of the oracle, 6.6 kPa below the sea-level value; the former values exactly without the options; the contact falls at its configured gravity; mismatches refused); `node scripts/bench-flight-contact.mjs --location` 0.00296 B/step, 0 GC events, 0 native allocator calls, 358 touchdowns in 357 cycles (`test-results/bench/flight-contact-location.json`); `node scripts/bench-physics.mjs` zero allocation. Full gates for 3a: lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm test` 1375 passed and 26 skipped with the same 5 container failures as increment 1; `npm run test:browser` 95 passed and 48 skipped; `cargo test --workspace` 103 passed. Increment 3b, done (application wiring): the Run tab starts a launcher run and connects an external SITL at the project's location: ArduPilot's `--home` is its latitude, longitude, elevation and heading (`homeOf`), `prepareVehicle` generates the TASK-147 barometer parameters at that elevation, and `RuntimePhysics.load` sends `environment.set` (the conditions' gravity and origin air) to the physics Worker before `model.load`; the Worker refuses a gravity outside 9.7 to 9.9 m/s² or an invalid air, and `profileModelPort` builds the dynamics with them. A planned repeat or variant keeps its parent's home and environment. Experiment payload schema 5 (schema 7 after the merge below) adds `environment` (the location with its effective conditions, or null for a run without one): `validateExperiment` requires the session's home to be the location, the conditions to be evaluated there with h = H + N, the WMM zone to match the horizontal field and the air to come from the location's sea-level pressure; schemas 1 to 4 read as 5 with `environment: null`; `conditions` (the measured QNH and the temperature offset) is a lineage input after `home`, and the date-dependent magnetic field is not. The Experiments tab names each record's conditions. ArduPilot SITL keeps computing its own magnetic field at the home; the WMM2025 field is shown and recorded for comparison, not written to the autopilot. Evidence: `tests/experiment-record.test.ts` 20 passed (an environment recorded and read back with the real EGM96 and WMM2025 models, a measured QNH, the blocked-geoid case, 13 refusals, the schema 4 migration, the lineage inputs and the Experiments tab's text); `tests/run-controller.test.ts` and `tests/app-shell.test.ts` pass with the environment in every request (118 tests); `tests/browser/profile-dynamics.spec.ts` 1 passed (the Worker refuses a gravity of 5 m/s²; after `environment.set` the 16-rotor vehicle on its gear publishes a specific force of 9.7965 m/s² within 0.005 and the air of CMAC's elevation within 100 Pa, 6 kPa below sea level). Increment 4, done (the Weather and location tab): `apps/web/src/weather/` keeps one location per project in the local setting `weather.location` (a built-in site or coordinates, the elevation, the heading, the standard or a measured sea-level pressure and the ISA offset; the default is CMAC, the former fixed home), shows the effective conditions from the models loaded in the browser from the repository's data files as the form changes, names every problem, and saves only a location inside the envelope. Phase 1 has no offline map and the TASK-131 inventory holds no elevation data, so the elevation is typed, the synthetic pad stands at any coordinates, and the plan's terrain sites are the one built-in site. Evidence: `tests/weather-location.test.ts` 5 passed (the default home, the envelope, the per-project setting and a damaged one, the form and the one-line description); `tests/browser/location.spec.ts` 1 passed (the default CMAC conditions, then coordinates in Israel at 250 m with a measured QNH of 1020 hPa and ISA +5 K, each shown value equal to `effectiveConditions` evaluated in Node at the same date; a blocked save with its problem named; axe without violations; the choice after a reload and the Run tab's location line). The first run of that test found the heading field sharing its element id with the section heading, which left the field without an accessible name; fixed. Merge with main (2026-11-05, main 940143a): TASK-172 and TASK-169 had taken experiment schemas 5 and 6, so the environment is schema 7, schema 6 records read as schema 7 without one, and `conditions` stays after `home` in the inputs (no stored `changes` list holds an input added after it); a batch run (TASK-176) and a connection start at the project's location, a run from a log (TASK-183) at the log's home in the conditions there with the standard sea-level pressure (`environmentAtHome`), and the TASK-172 COMPASS_DEC follows that home; a weather that keeps the air (TASK-166) and the end of a weather return to the location's origin air (`tests/geographic-session.test.ts` 4 passed; `tests/experiment-record.test.ts` with the schema 6 migration). Start check for launcher runs (2026-11-05): ArduPilot was given the session's home as --home, and the Run tab now checks that its first 3D fix (GPS_RAW_INT) lies within the connection limits of 10 m and 5 m of it before READY, as a connection already did; a fix elsewhere stops the run with the distance (failure `home`). Evidence: `tests/run-controller.test.ts` 126 passed, two of them new (READY waits for the fix and ignores a 2D one; a fix 334 m below the location's home stops the run). Prepared for the Mac, not required by the DoD: `tests/browser/location-flight.spec.ts` (skips without the TASK-152 installation) chooses 31.5°, 34.9°, 250 m, heading 90°, QNH 1020 hPa and ISA +5 K in the Weather and location tab, starts native ArduCopter 8ab6afde through the Run tab, takes off to 5 m and lands, and checks with tolerances declared in the file the start check, the record's home and conditions against `effectiveConditions` in Node, COMPASS_DEC and SIM_BARO_RND at 250 m, the resting specific force against the location's gravity (0.005 m/s²), the resting air pressure against the origin air (50 Pa) and ArduPilot's GPS altitude (5 m); its evidence goes to `test-results/browser/task-148-location-flight.json`. To run on the M5: `MIRAGE_BROWSER_GPU=hardware CHROMIUM_PATH="/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome" npx playwright test tests/browser/location-flight.spec.ts`. Full gates: lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm test` 1378 passed and 26 skipped, with the 5 container failures of increment 1, the documentation-policy tests before this record's hash refresh (they pass after it), and `tests/worldcover-geotiff.test.ts`, which timed out hashing its tiles under the full suite's load and passes alone (17 tests); `npm run test:browser` 96 passed and 48 skipped; `cargo test --workspace` 103 passed.
