### TASK-149 — Accident detection and automatic pause

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 0669655; 2026-11-02 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: decision F-09 in profile flight sessions: an accident on the tick a hull shape or propeller disc meets its criteria, the run paused there with the autopilot, the event and its contact load recorded, and a labelled continuation. Failure criterion: a hard landing on the supports that raises an accident, a strike raised on another tick or not at all, a session that steps past the accident tick, a continuing contact raised again, or an allocation in the step. Defaults (lead, open to change), since F-09 leaves them to Claude: "while its rotor turns" is at least 1 rad/s; an accident is raised when a shape starts to meet the criteria, and every new accident pauses the run again; the post-accident flag is the Run tab's `afterAccident` and the recording's `events.accident` rows, because an experiment record's inputs are fixed at version 1. The Touched Files list ends with the files added in implementation, from `packages/physics/src/bet-dynamics.ts` on: the dynamics owns the monitor, the clock and control processor give the pause its tick and its admission, and the Worker, bridge client, controller and Run panel carry it to the user. The Run tab does not record yet (TASK-163 wires recorder v2 into runs), so the recording is checked with the recorder's own consumer. Evidence (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1): `tests/accident.test.ts` 7 passed (each threshold below and at its value, a continuing contact raised once and a new one again, outbox overflow, the record rows and names); `tests/accident-session.test.ts` 5 passed on the quad X with the example cards: tipped 60 degrees and pitched 10 degrees nose down 5 mm above its lowest shape with stopped rotors, the session pauses at tick 13 on motor 1's propeller, as an independent plain run of the same dynamics predicts from its raw reports, publishes that tick marked paused and steps no further, and continuing it raises exactly the plain run's later strikes in one second, each pausing on its tick (motor 1's propeller again at ticks 21 and 24 as it bounces, motor 4's at tick 68); upside down, the body strikes at tick 13; in lockstep with the rotors at duty 0.15 the turning propeller of motor 1 (113.6 rad/s) strikes at tick 13 and the next autopilot frame waits until `sim.start`; dropped level 0.2 m onto its supports with the motors off, it touches down at 1.98 m/s and is stopped at 10.1 g with no accident; and the events and the pause read back from a recording. `tests/run-controller.test.ts` with the three new accident cases, `tests/session-v2.test.ts`, `tests/profile-dynamics.test.ts`, `tests/clock.test.ts`, `tests/clock-policy.test.ts`, `tests/control-v2.test.ts`, `tests/protocol.test.ts`, `tests/protocol-golden.test.ts`, `tests/bet-runtime.test.ts` and `tests/contact-binding.test.ts` pass (267 tests in those files). `node scripts/check-sitl-link-allocation.mjs`: 10⁶ lockstep steps at 400 Hz with the accident check on the quad's nine shapes at 0.003824 B/step and no GC event; a first version read each rotor speed as a returned double and ran at 0.0516 B/step with 61 GC events, so the monitor now reads all speeds into a typed array. Full gates (same machine): lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm run check:docs` passes; `npm test` 1298 passed and 1 skipped, its failures `tests/drive-chain.test.ts`, which fails on main on this Mac too, and the PWM test of `tests/external-sitl-session.test.ts`: its full-throttle climb without an autopilot reaches 7.94 m, flips and comes down on the turning propellers of motors 1 and 3 (991 rad/s) at tick 1508, where the session now pauses, so that test now expects the pause and the accidents and passes alone (4 of 4); `npm run test:browser` for the Run tab and its SITL paths (`external-sitl`, `run-panel`, `propeller-clearance`, `manual-connect`, `motor-test`) 12 passed with native ArduCopter (7.8 min); the whole browser suite runs again after TASK-163.
- **Title & Description:** (S18) Record contact of the body or a propeller as an accident: TASK-095 propeller-strike events, or body contact outside the support points. The accident pauses the run, preserving state and logs, and contact loads and events are recorded. A run may continue after an accident, but the continuation is labeled as not validated for a physically damaged aircraft. User-commanded motor failures remain independent.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-095, TASK-144.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/physics/src/accident.ts`, `packages/physics/src/session-v2.ts`, `tests/accident.test.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `packages/physics/src/bet-dynamics.ts`, `packages/physics/src/profile-dynamics.ts`, `packages/physics/src/clock.ts`, `packages/physics/src/clock-policy.ts`, `packages/physics/package.json`, `packages/protocol/src/control-v2.ts`, `apps/web/src/physics.worker.ts`, `apps/web/src/sitl/bridge-client.ts`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/session-controller.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `tests/accident-session.test.ts`, `tests/run-controller.test.ts`, `tests/external-sitl-session.test.ts`, `docs/decisions/phase1-fidelity.md`, `docs/ru/phase1-fidelity.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Accident event: tick, contact location, body or propeller, impulse and contact load. The session pauses on that tick through the same path as a user pause, which TASK-156 connects to ArduPilot. A post-accident flag is set on the experiment. Implemented as: `AccidentEvent` (tick, simulated time, part as a hull shape ID or a rotor ID, deepest point body FRD from the CG, penetration, normal impulse and force, rotor speed) from `AccidentMonitor` of `packages/physics/src/accident.ts`; `PhysicsSessionV2.drainAccidents`, `accidentsDropped` and `accidentTotal`; the Worker message `accident.events`; `ControlProcessorV2.paused()`; the Run tab's `paused` state with reason `accident` and `ControllerView.afterAccident`, the post-accident flag of the run; and the `events.accident` rows, whose count in the recording's catalog entry marks the experiment's recording, since an experiment record's inputs are fixed at version 1.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** A hard landing on the support points raises no accident. An arm or propeller strike raises an accident on the correct tick. The run pauses on that tick. The event and the contact loads appear in the recording. Continuation is visibly labeled.

### TASK-195 — Propeller clearance check with a previewed fix

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 0669655; 2026-11-02 (registered 2026-11-01 by the user's decision on TASK-095 increment 4: block a run on a propeller clash and offer a fix that shows its change; baseline 0e260ac). Goal: the check, the previewed fix and the run block of the user's decision. Failure criterion: a clash that is not reported, a reported clash between parts that do not touch, a fix that leaves a fixable clash or is not the shortest whole millimetre, a fix saved without confirmation, or a run that starts from a clashing profile. Defaults (lead, open to change), since the decision fixed the behaviour but not these values: the fix leaves tip gaps of 5 % of the larger propeller diameter (the closest gaps of large production multirotors), grows every arm by one common factor so the aircraft keeps its shape, rounds up to whole millimetres and leaves the mass properties unchanged with a note. Changes to the registered contract: `propellerClearance` takes the profile and each motor's propeller radius instead of the manifest, because the Aircraft tab checks a profile before its motor, ESC and battery cards are chosen and a manifest does not say whether its supports came from a stated clearance; and derivation 5 (standard gear rules 3) counts the propeller discs in the supports of a profile without a clearance, because the lower rotor of a coaxial pair can hang below the hull and F-07 lets no disc carry support. The Run tab already names a `vehicle` failure's reason, so `RunPanel.tsx` is unchanged and the refusal lives in the run preparation. The Touched Files list ends with the files added in implementation, from `packages/aircraft/src/derive-manifest.ts` on; `tests/browser/propeller-clearance.spec.ts` replaces `tests/browser/aircraft-editor.spec.ts`. Implemented (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Playwright 1.56.1 Chromium): `packages/aircraft/src/clearance.ts` with a GJK distance on the shapes' support functions, the clash kinds, `describeClash`, `clearanceShapes` and `clearanceFix`; `propellerDisc` and `restingClearanceM` shared with the derivation; the disc thickness moved to `vehicle-manifest.ts` and re-exported from `profile-dynamics.ts`; the refusal in `prepareVehicle`; the Aircraft tab's `ClearancePanel` with the clash list, to-scale top views now and after, the before-and-after table and "Save the fixed version"; the octa-x fixture version 2 on 0.385 m arms (fixture registry and UI recipe), with the derivation 5 hashes re-recorded and its derivation 1 hash re-recorded for the new arms, while the other four fixtures and the engine-sensitive variant reproduce their derivation 1 recordings unchanged. Evidence: `tests/aircraft-clearance.test.ts` 13 passed: distances against closed forms (discs side by side and stacked, capsules parallel and crossing, a capsule above a disc, within 10⁻⁹ m) and four tilted disc pairs against an independent sampled distance (never above it, below it by at most the sample spacing); the five S19 configurations clear with the synthetic 0.28 m and the example 0.254 m propellers; octa X on its former 0.35 m arms has the eight neighbouring pairs at 2R − 2L·sin(π/8) = 12.1 mm and the fix gives exactly the version 2 fixture, 0.385 m arms, with 0.384 m nearer than the 5 % gap; the 44 in octocopter on 0.25 m arms names all 28 pairs and eight body clashes (neighbours 928.7 mm, body 372.5 mm) and the fix gives 1.537 m arms with nothing else changed and no clash left; a coaxial pair whose discs touch is no clash, alternate hexa arms 6 cm apart in height clear and 5 mm apart clash; coaxial X8 at a 0.03 m clearance clashes with the body and four lower discs (18.5 mm) and the fix gives 0.054 m; without a clearance derivation 5 rests it at 0.0535 m; stacked propellers stay in the fixed report; and `prepareVehicle` refuses the quad on 0.17 m arms naming its four pairs (13.6 mm) and admits its fixed version. `tests/aircraft-profile.test.ts` 39, `tests/vehicle-manifest.test.ts` 24, `tests/profile-dynamics.test.ts` 16 (all five configurations, octa-x on its new arms, rest with no shape touching), `tests/fixtures.test.ts`, `tests/parameter-readback.test.ts`, `tests/output-mapping.test.ts`, `tests/aircraft-editor.test.ts` and `tests/experiment-record.test.ts` pass (142 tests in those files). `tests/browser/propeller-clearance.spec.ts`: the quad's arms shortened to 0.17 m in the Aircraft tab save as version 2 with the four clashes listed, Cancel leaves the profile, the fix shows 0.170 m → 0.189 m for each arm and both top views (four clashing discs now, none after), axe finds no violation, the tab fits 390 px, nothing saves before the confirmation, and "Save the fixed version" stores version 3 with only the arm lengths changed (1 passed); with the TASK-152 installation of ArduCopter 8ab6afde the Run tab refuses version 2 as a `vehicle` failure naming the four clashes, then flies version 3: READY, take-off to 5 m, a 10 s hold within 0.5 m, LAND, disarmed and stopped (1 passed, 1.2 min). Full gates (same machine): lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm run check:docs` passes; `npm test` 1284 passed and 1 skipped, its only failure `tests/drive-chain.test.ts`, which fails on main on this Mac too (a first full run, before the mirror hashes were refreshed, also failed the documentation policy, as expected, and one more file that its truncated log did not name; the rerun failed only `drive-chain`); `npm run test:browser` 117 passed and 16 skipped (15.7 min).
- **Phase 1 disposition:** Required (S03, S18): an aircraft whose propellers overlap each other or reach its body cannot fly, so a result from it would mean nothing.
- **Title & Description:** Check the geometry a derived manifest gives: two propeller discs that overlap, unless they form a coaxial pair of one arm; a propeller disc that reaches the standard hull's body; and, with a ground clearance, a hull shape or a propeller disc below the supports at rest. The Aircraft tab lists every clash with its motors or shapes and its size in millimetres. A profile with a clash still saves, but no run starts from it, and the Run tab names the reason. A fix button proposes the smallest change that clears every clash (longer arms, a larger ground clearance), shows each changed value before and after, and on confirmation saves a new profile version; it never edits a profile silently.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-095, TASK-141, TASK-154.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/aircraft/src/clearance.ts`, `packages/aircraft/package.json`, `apps/web/src/aircraft/AircraftEditor.tsx`, `apps/web/src/aircraft/ClearancePanel.tsx`, `tests/fixtures/aircraft/octa-x.json`, `tests/support/aircraft-derivation.ts`, `tests/aircraft-clearance.test.ts`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `packages/aircraft/src/derive-manifest.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/standard-gear.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/standard-hull.ts`, `packages/physics/src/vehicle-manifest.ts`, `packages/physics/src/profile-dynamics.ts`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `apps/web/src/style.css`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `tests/support/aircraft-recipes.ts`, `tests/aircraft-profile.test.ts`, `tests/browser/propeller-clearance.spec.ts`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/decisions/phase1-fidelity.md`, `docs/ru/phase1-fidelity.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** `propellerClearance(profile, propellerRadiusM)` returns the ground clearance at rest, whether the profile states it, and every clash (`propellers` with the two motors and their overlap seen from above, `propeller-body` with the motor and its overlap, `ground` with the part and its depth, in metres) of the propeller discs and the standard hull at the derived geometry; `clearanceFix(profile, propellerRadiusM)` returns null without a clash, or the fixed profile, each changed value before and after, the common arm factor and the fixed profile's report. Coaxial pairs (two motors of one arm) are exempt from the overlap check. Run preparation (TASK-154) refuses a profile with a clash as a `vehicle` failure that names each clash. Derivation 5 (standard gear rules 3) rests a profile without a clearance with the lowest of its hull and propeller discs one static deflection above the ground.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** An octocopter with 1.12 m (44 in) propellers on 0.25 m arms is blocked with each overlapping pair named; the fix proposes longer arms, shows before and after, and the saved version passes and flies; the five S19 configurations pass (the octa-x fixture, whose neighbouring 0.28 m discs overlap by 12 mm on 0.35 m arms, is corrected); a coaxial pair is not a clash; a browser test applies the fix through the UI alone.

## Stage P1.5: Integrated External-SITL Session

Scope step 5. Launch, connect, fly and pause native ArduPilot on macOS through the application (S01–S03). Sequential batches wait until TASK-158 passes.

### TASK-150 — MAVLink message set for Phase 1 ground-station functions

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 5d44ca8; 2026-10-30. Registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md (baseline 384e974). Goal: the MAVLink2 messages that TASK-153, TASK-159, TASK-170 and TASK-172 need, byte-exact against pymavlink 2.4.50. Design. `scripts/generate-mavlink-fixtures.py` runs the pinned pymavlink 2.4.50 generator on the pinned `ardupilotmega.xml`, which includes `common.xml` and so also covers EKF_STATUS_REPORT, the one ArduPilot-dialect message the codec already supports; the lock records the hashes of every definition file it reads. The script writes `packages/mavlink/src/message-table.ts`: for each supported message its ID, name, CRC extra, MAVLink2 payload length and fields (name, wire type, offset, array length, units, extension). `definition()` reads that table, so a supported message cannot drift from the generator. The supported set is the 12 existing messages (0, 20, 22, 23, 24, 27, 70, 93, 105, 107, 113, 193) plus SYS_STATUS (1), PARAM_REQUEST_LIST (21), ATTITUDE (30), LOCAL_POSITION_NED (32), GLOBAL_POSITION_INT (33), SERVO_OUTPUT_RAW (36, with servo outputs 9 to 16 as extensions), the mission protocol (MISSION_REQUEST 40, MISSION_SET_CURRENT 41, MISSION_CURRENT 42, MISSION_REQUEST_LIST 43, MISSION_COUNT 44, MISSION_CLEAR_ALL 45, MISSION_ITEM_REACHED 46, MISSION_ACK 47, MISSION_REQUEST_INT 51, MISSION_ITEM_INT 73), VFR_HUD (74), COMMAND_INT (75), COMMAND_LONG (76), COMMAND_ACK (77), SET_POSITION_TARGET_LOCAL_NED (84), POSITION_TARGET_LOCAL_NED (85), SET_POSITION_TARGET_GLOBAL_INT (86), POSITION_TARGET_GLOBAL_INT (87), BATTERY_STATUS (147) and STATUSTEXT (253). Builders: a cold `packInto(id, values, payload)` that rejects unknown fields, out-of-range integers, non-finite floats and over-long text, leaving the payload unchanged. Decoders: cold field and text readers, and preallocated typed targets for the high-rate telemetry subset (ATTITUDE, LOCAL_POSITION_NED, GLOBAL_POSITION_INT, VFR_HUD, SERVO_OUTPUT_RAW, SYS_STATUS, BATTERY_STATUS) that read 64-bit fields as two 32-bit words and never allocate. Oracle: golden frames that pymavlink packs from recorded field values, at least one per message with negative, extension and truncated payloads; the codec must reproduce every byte and the decoders every value. G1: the `mavlink` component of `scripts/bench-links-ew.mjs` also parses and decodes the high-rate subset. Failure criterion: a byte or value that differs from pymavlink, a field the table misplaces, an allocation in a high-rate decoder, or a lock that does not match the codec. Touched Files additions, made before implementation: `packages/mavlink/src/message-table.ts` (generated table), `scripts/bench-links-ew.mjs` (G1) and its three tracked reports. Evidence. `scripts/generate-mavlink-fixtures.py` ran with pymavlink 2.4.50 on a clean checkout of commit 2e2b6af99084e4fde9bb304c84bb9253a1a98b6f (environment variables `MIRAGE_MAVLINK_SOURCE` and `MIRAGE_MAVGEN_OUTPUT`, added so the script runs outside `/workspace`); the five frames before TASK-150 come out byte-identical, and the generated table reproduces the CRC extras and lengths of all 12 earlier definitions. The goldens hold 76 new frames, two per supported message (a distinct value in every field, and a frame truncated to its first field), and the lock (schema 2) records the hashes of all nine definition files. `npx --no-install vitest run tests/mavlink.test.ts`: 11 tests pass. They cover byte-exact packing of all 76 frames from their recorded values; decoding of every value from 7-byte chunks, with float fields compared after float32 rounding; rejection of unknown fields, out-of-range and non-integer values, non-finite floats, wrong array lengths and non-ASCII or over-long text, with the payload unchanged; the seven high-rate decoders against the pattern frames, each refusing every other message; a corrupted payload byte in every new frame rejected as a bad CRC; and agreement of lock, table and codec, with no overlapping or missing payload byte. `tests/fixtures.test.ts` passes with the golden's new registry hash. G1: `node scripts/bench-links-ew.mjs all 400`, `800` and `1200` (reports `test-results/bench/links-ew-all-400.json`, `-800.json` and `-1200.json`) each decode 22,000,000 high-rate telemetry frames with 0 GC events and 0 B/step, the allocating positive control detected. A first version decoded through the shared `readNumber` and allocated (0.005 B/step, GC in every window); the decoders now use direct DataView getters. Full gates: `npm test` 955 passed and 1 skipped, the four documentation-policy failures being the stale mirror hashes before the refresh; `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build` exit 0; the browser suite gives 81 passed and 28 skipped, with the two `tests/browser/simulation.spec.ts` takeoff tests failing on the known input-failsafe stall that also occurred before this change (alone, one passed and the other passed two of three runs). `scripts/sitl/pause-probe.mjs` hashes the lock into its source identity, so its TASK-129 report is now a historical snapshot.
- **Title & Description:** (S02, S09, S10, S11) Extend the MAVLink2 codec, with golden pymavlink 2.4.50 fixtures, to the messages Phase 1 needs: the parameter list request and parameter payload builders; COMMAND_LONG, COMMAND_INT and COMMAND_ACK; STATUSTEXT and SYS_STATUS; ATTITUDE, LOCAL_POSITION_NED, GLOBAL_POSITION_INT and VFR_HUD; SERVO_OUTPUT_RAW and BATTERY_STATUS; the mission protocol; and the position-target messages. Update `dialect.lock.json`, which lists only 4 of the 12 message IDs the codec supports today.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-037.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/mavlink/src/codec.ts`, `packages/mavlink/src/messages.ts`, `packages/mavlink/dialect.lock.json`, `scripts/generate-mavlink-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures/mavlink/golden.json`, `tests/mavlink.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `packages/mavlink/src/message-table.ts`, `scripts/bench-links-ew.mjs`, `test-results/bench/links-ew-all-400.json`, `test-results/bench/links-ew-all-800.json`, `test-results/bench/links-ew-all-1200.json`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Unsigned MAVLink2 only, as today; byte-exact packing with CRC_EXTRA; preallocated decode targets for the high-rate telemetry subset; unsupported frames consumed whole, preserving the TASK-047 repair.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Every added message matches pymavlink bytes. Chunking, truncation and bad-CRC tests pass. G1 holds for the high-rate subset. The dialect lock matches the codec.

### TASK-151 — Local launcher service for macOS

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a5f26c3; 2026-10-31. Implemented and checked on the reference Mac, including G4 offline with the network switched off. `apps/launcher` binds 127.0.0.1 only (default port 47800). API v1 (status, prerequisites, catalog, start, stop, restart, logs, DataFlash download) requires the locally generated token (`<home>/token`, 64 hex, mode 0600, refused if others can read it, never printed) as `Authorization: Bearer`, the exact configured origin and a loopback Host, and refuses any query string. `catalog.ts` admits a build only when its source commit is in `scripts/sitl.lock.json`, the executable and `copter.parm` keep their recorded SHA-256 and the executable passes the TASK-127 Mach-O64/ELF64 admission. `prerequisites.ts` reports platform, Node.js 24, a writable home with 2 GiB free, pymavlink 2.4.50 and a usable build, each with a fix. `processes.ts` starts SITL in its own process group with a per-experiment folder, parameter file, optional `scripts/frame.lua`, run logs and an owner record; stop sends SIGTERM and SIGKILL after 3 s, a crash keeps its exit code or signal, and restart starts a new run. Each run hosts its own TASK-040 bridge in ArduPilot JSON mode, and `relay.ts` carries SITL's `udpclient:` MAVLink into it, because SITL sends from an ephemeral port that the bridge's connected socket cannot receive. One experiment runs at a time. Touched Files also list `apps/launcher/src/relay.ts` and `config/offline-inventory.json`, which now names the launcher's default origin and the loopback WebSocket origin; the latter was already missing for the TASK-129 pause probe and failed `tests/offline-inventory.test.ts` on a5f26c3. Evidence on the reference MacBook Air (Apple M5, 32 GB), macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1: `npx --no-install vitest run tests/launcher.test.ts` exit 0 with 7 tests. Six use fake executables: a private token file; wrong, missing or URL tokens, wrong or missing origins and a foreign Host refused; start with its folder and parameter file, logs, DataFlash list and download, a refused second start, and a stop that ends the owned group including a forked grandchild; a crash with exit code 3, a restart as run 2, and SIGKILL escalation after 3 s for a TERM-resistant process; an outside `sleep` left running; seven invalid requests and a changed executable refused; prerequisites with fixes. The seventh runs the real pinned ArduCopter (`d47c1f64…`) through the launcher: more than 800 JSON lockstep frames through the hosted bridge, a HEARTBEAT back through the relay, then stop with no process left in its group. `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` exit 0; `npx --no-install vitest run tests/offline-inventory.test.ts` exit 0 with 11 tests; ESLint on `apps/launcher` and the test and `tsc --noEmit` exit 0. Network-off run (G4) on 2026-10-31 with `.cache/task-152/offline-check.sh` at HEAD 9eca6d4, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Apple M5: with Wi-Fi off, `scutil --nwi` reports no IPv4 or IPv6 state, the only addresses are loopback and link-local, and github.com, 1.1.1.1 and 8.8.8.8 do not answer before or after the checks; `npx --no-install vitest run tests/launcher.test.ts tests/install-macos.test.ts` exit 0 with 13 tests, none skipped, among them both real-SITL cases (the TASK-127 build to a HEARTBEAT in 1.11 s, and the TASK-152 installation in 1.15 s); `node scripts/check-offline.mjs` exit 0. The working tree also held the uncommitted launcher test case added for TASK-152 and another session's uncommitted TASK-179 changes, which touch none of the launcher files. Report in `.cache/task-152/offline-run-20261031T132207/`. Correction of 2026-11-04: on macOS `kill(-group)` answers EPERM, not ESRCH, while a group holds only exited processes not yet reaped: 20 of 20 when forced, and 2 of 400 with no repository code involved when a leader and its child die together under load. `OwnedProcess.signalGroup` tolerated only ESRCH, and it runs in the exit listener and the SIGKILL timer, where a throw is uncaught and would end the launcher. It showed as `tests/run-controller.test.ts` timing out in its Stop test with `kill EPERM`, about 2 runs in 5 when several test files ran together, on a clean b07dc24 too. EPERM now counts as nothing left to signal, in `signalGroup` and in the same check of `runOwned` in `scripts/run-sitl.mjs`; any other answer is still an error. `tests/launcher-processes.test.ts` fails on the old code, one case with no mocks, and passes now; that test load, repeated 8 times, gave 8 clean runs and no EPERM.
- **Title & Description:** (S01, S02) Provide a local loopback service that the user starts. It checks prerequisites and keeps a catalog of the installed compatible ArduPilot versions. It starts and stops owned SITL processes with per-experiment working directories and parameter files, collects diagnostics and DataFlash logs, and hosts the WebSocket bridge. A normal web page cannot start programs; the launcher is the only component that does.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-040, TASK-127.
- **Touched Files:** `apps/launcher/package.json`, `apps/launcher/src/server.ts`, `apps/launcher/src/processes.ts`, `apps/launcher/src/prerequisites.ts`, `apps/launcher/src/catalog.ts`, `apps/launcher/src/logs.ts`, `tests/launcher.test.ts`, `tests/launcher-processes.test.ts`, `scripts/run-sitl.mjs`, `package-lock.json`, `docs/operations/runbook.md`, `docs/ru/runbook.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/ru/architecture.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `apps/launcher/src/relay.ts`, `config/offline-inventory.json`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** A versioned loopback API (status, prerequisites, start, stop, logs, catalog) authenticated with a locally generated token and an exact Origin match; it binds to loopback only and records process ownership. Tokens are never printed or put in URLs.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Start, stop, crash and restart cases pass with fake and real binaries. Processes the launcher does not own are never touched. Unauthorized origins and tokens are rejected. The service works offline on a reference Mac (G4).

### TASK-152 — macOS installation and first-run prerequisite check

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a5f26c3; 2026-10-31. Implemented; the clean installation, and restart and launch with the network switched off, are recorded on the reference Mac. `scripts/install-macos.mjs` checks the host (macOS on Apple Silicon, Node.js 24, Xcode command line tools, git, Python 3.9 or later, each with its fix), installs the Python packages of `scripts/install/requirements-macos.txt` with `pip --require-hashes` (the eight packages TASK-127 used, every PyPI file hash of each pinned version), fetches ArduPilot at the `scripts/sitl.lock.json` commit with the ten submodules the SITL build needs, builds native ArduCopter SITL with waf, admits it as a Mach-O64 executable and records it in the launcher `catalog.json`, fetches UAVLogViewer at the commit, submodule and LICENSE hash of its source lock (the offline bundle is TASK-179), and writes `install-manifest.json` with every item's version, SHA-256, size and inventory license. A rerun checks each step and skips what is done. The launcher gains an installation check that recomputes the SHA-256 of every file the manifest recorded in the launcher folder; its fixes name the installer. The Project tab's "Setup: local launcher and prerequisites" section (`FirstRun.tsx`) takes the launcher port and token, keeps the token in the page only (Authorization header, never a URL or storage), distinguishes an unreachable launcher from a refused token, and lists each prerequisite as OK, Missing or Needs attention with its fix. Touched Files also list `scripts/install/install-rules.ts` (the tested rules), `scripts/install/requirements-macos.txt`, `apps/web/src/shell/AppShell.tsx` (placing the section) and `apps/web/src/style.css`. The launcher started twice on the installed folder reports all six prerequisites OK with the same token. `npx --no-install vitest run tests/install-macos.test.ts` exit 0 with 5 tests (host checks, pinned requirements, manifest rules, `--plan`, and every recorded hash of the installation with every prerequisite OK); `npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/first-run.spec.ts` exit 0 (a real launcher with a fake build: unreachable port, refused token, each prerequisite with its fix, the token absent from the address, storage and page text, 390 px, zero axe violations). ESLint and `tsc --noEmit` exit 0. Network-off run on 2026-10-31 (the TASK-151 run and report): `tests/launcher.test.ts`, also touched by this task, gains a case on the installation itself that starts the launcher twice on `.cache/task-152/home` with the same token and all six prerequisites OK, then launches the installed build (`02066ec9…`) through JSON lockstep to a HEARTBEAT and stops its group; it passes with the network off in 1.15 s, and so do the installation hash check of `tests/install-macos.test.ts` and `npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/first-run.spec.ts` (1 test, exit 0).

### TASK-153 — Browser physics as the JSON lockstep backend for native ArduPilot

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 89b35e8; 2026-10-31 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: the profile dynamics in PhysicsSessionV2 answer native ArduPilot's JSON lockstep through the launcher's bridge, and the application flies the vehicle. Failure criterion: a lost, resent or reordered servo frame steps the session twice or not at all, READY from anything but this run's live EKF diagnostics, or an allocation in the lockstep step. Increment 1 (headless core, MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1): `SitlJsonLink` (`packages/autopilot/src/sitl-link.ts`) turns each new servo frame of a bridge record into one ActuatorFrameV2 for the session's next tick and, once the session has published that tick, its state into the JSON state record; resent frames get the stored reply, lost frames are counted and never replayed, frames that arrive before the reply are held, and a restart at frame 0 is reported. `GroundStation` (`packages/autopilot/src/ground-station.ts`) packs the heartbeat and COMMAND_LONG requests with the TASK-150 codec. `tests/external-sitl-session.test.ts` (4 tests) drives a real PhysicsSessionV2 with the S19 quad X on the example cards: every case of lost, resent, stale, held, foreign-epoch and damaged records steps exactly as specified, the replies pass the bridge's own state-line validator, PWM drives the rotors, and pymavlink 2.4.50 decodes the ground station's frames identically. `tests/external-sitl-flight.test.ts` (G4) flies that profile (manifest `18d03856…`) with the installed native ArduCopter `8ab6afde` of the TASK-152 home through the launcher, at 800 Hz and speedup 4: READY from LiveEkfWarmup at 41.1 s simulated time, GUIDED and armed at 44.0 s, NAV_TAKEOFF to 5 m, a 10 s hold between 4.92 and 4.98 m, LAND and disarmed on the pad at 75.0 s, in 18.7 s wall time; 60,032 lockstep steps with no duplicate, gap, restart or held frame, and every command acknowledged with result 0 (report `test-results/validation/task-153-flight.json`, ignored). 6,224 frames carried PWM outside 1000–2000 µs and were clamped; their cause is not investigated yet. At 400 Hz ArduCopter refused to arm ("PreArm: Gyro 0 rate 400Hz < loop rate*1.8 720Hz"), so integrated sessions run at 800 Hz or 1200 Hz. G1: `node scripts/check-sitl-link-allocation.mjs` measures receive, pump and poll together: 1,000,000 warmed steps at 400 Hz, 0.0035 B/step, 0 GC events, positive control 30 GC events, exit 0 (report `test-results/bench/sitl-link-allocation.json`, ignored). It found three allocations, now removed: a DataView per servo datagram, a subarray per reply, and boxed double arguments in the state-line encoder (48 B per line). Increment 2 (baseline 4e29ec6, same Mac): the physics Worker runs the link (`SitlBridgeClient`, `apps/web/src/sitl/bridge-client.ts`) on a WebSocket it owns to the run's bridge, posts MAVLink to Main and takes Main's MAVLink back; Main (`apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`) prepares the vehicle from a stored profile version and its cards, gives the Worker the BET module (served to loopback at `/__bet/` by `apps/web/vite.config.ts`), the manifest and its ratings, loads the model in sitl-lockstep at 800 Hz, starts the run through the launcher and is the ground station; the Run tab's ArduPilot SITL backend (`apps/web/src/run/SitlFlight.tsx`) shows the stage, READY with its reason, the autopilot's mode and arming, altitude, simulated time and speed, and stepped, lost, resent and held frames. `tests/sitl-bridge-client.test.ts` (3 tests) admits READY only after 30 s of acknowledged IMU samples and fresh healthy diagnostics, revokes it when the EKF reports and heartbeats stop while GPS continues, and when GPS also stops, and ends it on a SITL restart. `tests/browser/external-sitl.spec.ts` (Playwright Chromium; Google Chrome is not installed on the M5): the S19 quad X profile on the example cards, seeded through the application's store, flies with the installed ArduCopter from the Run tab alone at speedup 4: READY, Take off to 5 m, once the climb has settled and one more simulated second has passed a hold of at least 10 s of simulated time with every reading within 0.5 m, Land, Landed below 0.1 m with 0 lost and 0 resent frames, and Stop; the token never enters the URL and the panel has no axe violations (1 test, 48.7 s and 49.6 s in two runs, exit 0). In Google Chrome 156.0.8078.80 (Playwright 1.56.1 with `CHROMIUM_PATH` set to Chrome, headless, software GL as configured) on the stakeholder's M5, the acceptance machine of A-01, on 2026-11-01 at commit ec0c6bc: `tests/browser/external-sitl.spec.ts` passed (1 test, 51.7 s).
- **Title & Description:** (S01, S02, S03) Run the profile dynamics in the physics Worker in SITL lockstep with native ArduPilot over the launcher's bridge: servo packets come in for up to 16 outputs and JSON state lines go out. MAVLink travels over the same bridge to the application's ground-station functions. READY is admitted only from this session's live EKF diagnostics (LiveEkfWarmup), never from offline captures. Overload and dropped steps are visible.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-043, TASK-129, TASK-144, TASK-150, TASK-151.
- **Touched Files:** `apps/web/src/physics.worker.ts`, `apps/web/src/runtime.ts`, `apps/web/src/sitl/bridge-client.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/external-sitl.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/live-warmup.ts`, `packages/physics/src/session-v2.ts`, `packages/physics/src/clock-policy.ts`, `apps/bridge/src/server.ts`, `apps/bridge/src/session.ts`, `tests/external-sitl-session.test.ts`, `tests/browser/external-sitl.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/ru/architecture.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `packages/autopilot/src/sitl-link.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/ground-station.ts`, `packages/autopilot/package.json`, `tests/external-sitl-flight.test.ts`, `tests/support/quad-profile.ts`, `scripts/check-sitl-link-allocation.mjs`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/SitlFlight.tsx`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/shell/AppShell.tsx`, `apps/web/vite.config.ts`, `tests/sitl-bridge-client.test.ts`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** The `sitl-lockstep` clock policy; servo-to-rotor mapping from the profile; the existing bridge framing, epochs and limits; the autopilot state-machine lifecycle `disconnected → initializing → warming → ready → running → fault`; zero allocation in the lockstep step.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** On macOS, a profile-defined quad arms, takes off, holds and lands through the browser application. Lost, duplicated and reordered packets never double-step. Stale diagnostics revoke READY. G1 holds for the step. No offline report admits READY.

### TASK-154 — Experiment start, stop, readiness and failure handling

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline e7d09a9; 2026-11-01 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: the Run tab's integrated session (TASK-153) gets the contract's run states, an immutable experiment record and a reason, a diagnostic log and a restart path for every failure. Failure criterion: a failure without its reason, a stop that leaves an owned process, or a run whose record misses a version or parameter used. Decisions of 2026-11-01 (user): the ArduPilot build identity (catalog ID, firmware version, commit, binary and default-parameter SHA-256) is experiment payload data in record envelope v1, without a dependency-model change; a restart after a failure is a new launcher experiment with a new record; a profile that configures MirageSITL-owned sensor error terms, which the browser path does not apply yet, is refused at start with the terms named, while ArduPilot-owned SIM_* sensor parameters are applied; busy port is injected with a fake SITL that cannot bind a held port and with a program that is not the launcher on the launcher's port, and bridge loss with an in-process launcher handle that no HTTP route reaches. Defaults (lead, open to change): the payload module is `packages/aircraft/src/experiment.ts`; `paused` is modeled without a control until TASK-156; parameter read-back must complete within 30 s of simulated time; a READY loss in flight is shown as a warning; a refused start writes no record. Implemented (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Playwright 1.56.1 Chromium; Google Chrome is not installed on the M5): the run states as a pure `SessionController` on launcher, physics, record and clock ports, rendered by `RunPanel`; experiment payload schema 1 with its validator registered for the `experiment` kind; tagged initial parameters with the ArduPilot-owned sensor parameters and PARAM_VALUE read-back; the launcher's clean refusals, build identity in the view, exit reasons and the `closeBridge` seam. `tests/run-controller.test.ts` 67 tests (scripted ports and a real launcher with the shared fake SITL), `tests/experiment-record.test.ts` 9 (worst case 26,226 bytes), `tests/parameter-readback.test.ts` (pymavlink 2.4.50 oracle) and `tests/launcher.test.ts` pass. Eleven controller tests first failed on real defects, all fixed: a Stop during the start did not cancel it and could leave a launched SITL running, a record failure after a Stop stopped twice, a successful Stop again still showed the stop failure, the ground-station beat could replace a bridge-loss or crash reason or fail a start before the bridge opened, and a heartbeat without autopilot 3 started the parameter download; the browser run also found that the dynamic JSON import of the ArduPilot catalog failed under Vite, so every start failed at preparation. `tests/browser/run-panel.spec.ts` with the installed ArduCopter `8ab6afde` (binary `02066ec9…`) behind a wrapper, each case through the Run tab alone, each failure showing its reason and diagnostic log, leaving no process of the run's group, and then reaching READY on Start again with a new experiment and record: MirageSITL-owned sensor terms refused (2.8 s); missing binary, with the record read back from IndexedDB equal to the POSTed parameters, the catalog hashes and the snapshot (22.1 s); busy port, a fake SITL that cannot bind a held port and a program that is not the launcher on the launcher's port (22.1 s); SITL killed by SIGKILL during EKF warmup (22.3 s); bridge loss through `closeBridge` (26.2 s). The TASK-153 flight passes on the new panel (35.4 s). Full suites: browser 108 passed, 15 skipped (8.2 min); unit 1155 passed, 5 skipped, with the known `drive-chain` golden-hash failure that main has on this Mac and one file lost to two concurrent `scripts/build-wasm.mjs` runs (`tests/sitl-frames.test.ts`, 4 tests, passes alone); lint, typecheck and check:docs pass. In Google Chrome 156.0.8078.80 (Playwright 1.56.1 with `CHROMIUM_PATH` set to Chrome, headless, software GL as configured) on the stakeholder's M5, the acceptance machine of A-01, on 2026-11-01 at commit ec0c6bc: `tests/browser/run-panel.spec.ts` passed, the sensor-term refusal (3.8 s) and the four DoD faults, missing binary (27.4 s), busy port (27.1 s), SIGKILL during warmup (28.1 s) and bridge loss (30.6 s).
- **Title & Description:** (S02) The user selects a profile version and a compatible ArduPilot version and starts an experiment. The launcher spawns SITL with the generated frame, output, sensor and initial parameters, and the UI shows connection, parameter loading and EKF readiness. Stop terminates only the experiment's own processes. Every failure shows its reason, a diagnostic log and a restart path. Changing model characteristics requires a new run.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-136, TASK-142, TASK-143, TASK-153.
- **Touched Files:** `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/session-controller.ts`, `apps/launcher/src/processes.ts`, `tests/run-controller.test.ts`, `tests/browser/run-panel.spec.ts`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `apps/launcher/src/server.ts`, `apps/launcher/src/logs.ts`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/SitlFlight.tsx`, `apps/web/src/App.tsx`, `apps/web/src/shell/AppShell.tsx`, `packages/aircraft/src/experiment.ts`, `packages/aircraft/package.json`, `packages/autopilot/src/ground-station.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/parameter-readback.ts`, `packages/autopilot/package.json`, `scripts/check-sitl-sensors.mjs`, `tests/experiment-record.test.ts`, `tests/parameter-readback.test.ts`, `tests/launcher.test.ts`, `tests/support/fake-sitl.mjs`, `tests/support/fake-firmware.ts`, `tests/browser/external-sitl.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/operations/runbook.md`, `docs/ru/runbook.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Run states: idle, starting, loading parameters, warming, ready, running, paused, stopping, and failed with a reason. The experiment record stores the profile version, the ArduPilot version and binary hash, and the initial parameters. It embeds `sensorSnapshot` (TASK-143) of its exact aircraft-profile dependency verbatim.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Fault-injection tests (missing binary, busy port, crash during warmup, bridge loss) show the reason and a working restart. Stop leaves no owned process behind. The experiment record contains every version and parameter used.

### TASK-155 — Manual connection to a running compatible SITL

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline bee4567; 2026-11-01 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: the Run tab connects to an ArduCopter SITL the user started, judges its compatibility with reasons, and disconnects without ever signalling it. Failure criterion: a connected SITL that does not fly the profile with MirageSITL physics, a refusal without its reasons, or a disconnect or failure that ends the user's process. Decisions of 2026-11-01 (user): a SITL with built-in physics is refused with the reason; only verified source commits connect; the SITL's parameters are checked and never written, every difference being a reason to refuse; a connection is recorded as experiment payload schema 2 with an external SITL variant, schema 1 staying readable. Implemented (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Playwright 1.56.1 Chromium; Google Chrome is not installed on the M5): launcher `POST /v1/connections` with ownership `external` (JSON physics port, MAVLink over SITL's TCP server or a UDP port), its view with the servo packets seen, and disconnect; `SessionController.connect` with the frame-script, JSON-physics, armed, version (AUTOPILOT_VERSION, which the codec now supports), parameter (every difference listed) and home checks, the TCP-SITL clock classification and a schema 2 record once all pass; the Run tab's Connect form and its list of passed checks. Three defects found against the real build, all fixed: the first servo packets announce SIM_RATE_HZ's default 1200 Hz, not the rate SITL steps at (SIM_JSON adopts the physics peer's step), so physics now steps at the session's 800 Hz and the rate is shown, not checked; stream requests stopped after their acknowledgements, but a link's first send pass resets intervals set before it, which a TCP SITL whose scheduler starts at the connection receives, so the connection had no GPS and no READY, and streams are now requested until their messages arrive (owned runs included); and a SITL connected again keeps its clock, so its GPS led the new session's clock and was judged incoherent, and LiveEkfWarmup now compares GPS in the session clock shifted by the autopilot's lead, bounded from below by GLOBAL_POSITION_INT's time_boot_ms. Evidence: `tests/launcher-connections.test.ts` 5 passed (three connect and disconnect cycles against the pinned ArduCopter leave its process running); `tests/run-controller.test.ts` 86 passed (20 for connections); `tests/experiment-record.test.ts` 11; `tests/autopilot-version.test.ts` 4 against the pymavlink goldens of message 148; `tests/parameter-readback.test.ts` and `tests/sitl-bridge-client.test.ts` (lead of 120 s admitted only with GLOBAL_POSITION_INT) pass; `tests/browser/manual-connect.spec.ts` 3 passed in 1.3 min with the installed ArduCopter `8ab6afde` (reports 4.8.0-dev) started as a user would: two connect and disconnect cycles to READY with schema 2 records and the process still running, a SITL with built-in physics refused with the reason that its clock cannot be paused, and one whose FRAME_TYPE differs refused naming it and then a fitting one connected. Full gates: `npm run test:browser` 111 passed and 15 skipped (9.3 min); `npm test` 1186 passed and 1 skipped with 6 failures: the 3 documentation-policy tests on the mirror hashes, which this review then refreshed; the `drive-chain` golden hash, which fails on main on this Mac too; the fixture registry hash of the regenerated MAVLink goldens, which TASK-155 updated (then 17 of 17 in `tests/fixtures.test.ts` and `tests/mavlink.test.ts`); and one `flight-contact` case that timed out under the full suite's load and passes alone (5 of 5). Lint and typecheck 0; check:docs passes. In Google Chrome 156.0.8078.80 (Playwright 1.56.1 with `CHROMIUM_PATH` set to Chrome, headless, software GL as configured) on the stakeholder's M5, the acceptance machine of A-01, on 2026-11-01 at commit ec0c6bc: `tests/browser/manual-connect.spec.ts` passed, the two connect and disconnect cycles to READY (44.7 s), the built-in-physics refusal (18.4 s) and the parameter refusal followed by a fitting connection (23.9 s).
- **Title & Description:** (S02) Connect to an ArduPilot SITL that the user started; disconnecting never terminates it. Check compatibility: version, frame, and JSON lockstep with MirageSITL physics. Enable pause only for configurations that TASK-129 classified as clock-compatible.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-129, TASK-153.
- **Touched Files:** `apps/web/src/run/ManualConnect.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/session-controller.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `apps/launcher/src/server.ts`, `apps/launcher/src/connections.ts`, `apps/launcher/src/relay.ts`, `apps/bridge/src/ardupilot-json.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/experiment.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/autopilot-version.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/ground-station.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/parameter-readback.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/live-warmup.ts`, `packages/autopilot/package.json`, `packages/mavlink/dialect.lock.json`, `packages/mavlink/src/message-table.ts`, `scripts/generate-mavlink-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures/mavlink/golden.json`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `tests/mavlink.test.ts`, `tests/autopilot-version.test.ts`, `tests/launcher-connections.test.ts`, `tests/run-controller.test.ts`, `tests/experiment-record.test.ts`, `tests/parameter-readback.test.ts`, `tests/sitl-bridge-client.test.ts`, `tests/support/run-tab.ts`, `tests/browser/manual-connect.spec.ts`, `tests/browser/run-panel.spec.ts`, `tests/browser/external-sitl.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/operations/runbook.md`, `docs/ru/runbook.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Connection descriptor: JSON physics and MAVLink endpoints, plus a compatibility verdict with its reasons. Ownership is `external`, so stop and disconnect only release the connection. Implemented as launcher API v1 `POST /v1/connections`, `GET /v1/connections/{id}` and `POST /v1/connections/{id}/disconnect`, experiment payload schema 2 (`sitl.ownership` `launcher` or `external`) and MAVLink AUTOPILOT_VERSION (148) in the codec; see "Manual connection to a running SITL (TASK-155)" in the engines spec.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Connect and disconnect cycles leave the external process running. Incompatible configurations are refused with reasons. Pause is disabled, with an explanation, where the clock is not compatible.

### TASK-156 — Synchronized pause and resume in integrated sessions

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 0669655; 2026-11-02 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: one pause path for the integrated session that freezes the physics and ArduPilot's simulated time together and resumes both, with the accident pause of TASK-149 on it. Failure criterion: a pause that lets simulated time or the retained state move, a resume outside the TASK-129 continuity tolerance or with a failsafe, a pause that is not logged and recorded, or a hidden tab that lets a run go on unwatched. Defaults (lead, open to change), since the task leaves them: the visibility policy pauses an active run whenever its tab is hidden and waits for Resume, because the ground station runs on the page's main thread, which a browser throttles in a hidden tab; a user or hidden-tab pause is possible while loading parameters, warming, READY or flying, and an accident also pauses a start once SITL runs; `terrain` is in the reason union for TASK-186. Two changes of TASK-149's behaviour, found by this task's browser test: an accident before the autopilot's first heartbeat (a nose-heavy quad tipping over on its supports) froze the autopilot and ended as a lost heartbeat, so a start now pauses on it and Resume restarts its heartbeat deadline; and the wreck's contact chatter raised a new accident every few ticks, so a shape now counts again only after 0.25 s clear of the ground, which F-09's record and the "Accidents" section state. The registered Touched Files named session, clock-policy, scenario-runner, runtime and bridge files that needed no change: the session's pause already exists (`sim.pause`, TASK-149's `paused()`), a scenario's progress is its ticks, which stop, and the bridge keeps a paused WebSocket alive with its 1 s ping. Evidence (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Playwright 1.56.1 Chromium): `tests/run-controller.test.ts` 98 passed, the four new cases a user pause in flight resumed to the same flight with the pause logged and 600 s of paused wall time counted, a hidden-tab pause at READY, an accident before the first heartbeat that pauses the start and restarts its deadline on Resume, and a refused `sim.pause` failing with its reason; `tests/accident.test.ts` 7 and `tests/accident-session.test.ts` 5 passed with the re-arm rule (the tipped quad's strikes in one second are motor 1's propeller at tick 13, whose bounces stay that strike, and motor 4's at tick 68). `tests/browser/sitl-pause.spec.ts` with the TASK-152 installation of ArduCopter 8ab6afde, through the Run tab alone: a user pause in a 5 m hover at 55.1 simulated seconds held 606.3 s of wall time with the simulated time, the 5.05 m altitude and the 44,000 stepped frames unchanged, then resumed at 55.1 s into a hover between 5.05 and 5.07 m for 10 s of simulated time (20 readings), armed in GUIDED with no failsafe or EKF fault message, the pause logged and recorded as rows 1 and 0 (1 passed, 13.7 min); and a quad with its CG 0.152 m forward tipped onto its nose at 0.90 s while settling at the start, motor 3's stopped propeller at 184.8 N, the run paused on it before the first heartbeat and continued labelled (the rocking wreck struck once more), loaded its parameters, then paused when its tab was hidden and waited for Resume after the tab was shown again, every pause recorded (1 passed, 35 s). Full gates (same machine): lint, typecheck and build 0; `npm run check:docs` passes; `npm test` 1314 passed and 1 skipped, its only failure `tests/drive-chain.test.ts`, which fails on main on this Mac too; `npm run test:browser` 121 passed and 16 skipped (27.7 min), with both pause tests and the TASK-195, TASK-149 and TASK-163 browser tests among them.
- **Title & Description:** (S02) Pause freezes physics and ArduPilot simulated time together (per TASK-129), retaining position, velocity, aircraft state and scenario progress, and must not itself cause sensor or link-loss failsafes. The bridge keeps paused sessions alive, and charts and logs stay viewable. Parameter changes are allowed where TASK-129 showed support, with restart and disarm requirements visible. Pause and resume events are recorded, and paused wall time does not extend the simulated duration. Accident pauses (TASK-149) and missing-terrain pauses use the same path. Define a tab-visibility policy that never silently continues on stale manual input.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-129, TASK-149, TASK-153.
- **Touched Files:** `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/session-controller.ts`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `apps/web/src/sitl/bridge-client.ts`, `apps/web/src/physics.worker.ts`, `tests/run-controller.test.ts`, `tests/browser/sitl-pause.spec.ts`, `tests/support/run-tab.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`, `packages/physics/src/accident.ts`, `tests/accident.test.ts`, `tests/accident-session.test.ts`, `docs/decisions/phase1-fidelity.md`, `docs/ru/phase1-fidelity.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** One pause path with a reason (`user`, `accident`, `terrain`, `visibility`); a keep-alive for paused bridge sessions; pause and resume events with tick and wall time.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** A pause of at least 10 min in hover resumes within the TASK-129 continuity tolerance with no failsafe. Events appear in the record. Simulated duration excludes paused wall time. Browser tests cover a user pause, an accident pause and a hidden-tab pause.

### TASK-157 — Virtual one-motor-at-a-time check

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline f65d80b; 2026-11-01 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: before flight, ArduPilot's own motor test spins each motor alone and the result per motor compares ArduPilot's frame with what the physics turned. Failure criterion: a configuration whose correct wiring fails, a swapped mapping or reversed spin that passes, or a check that runs while armed or airborne. Implemented (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Google Chrome 156.0.8078.80): `MotorCheck` in `packages/autopilot/src/motor-test.ts` with the expectation from the TASK-142 frame configuration (test order, output, yaw-factor spin) and the observation from power telemetry and the running manifest; `GroundStation.motorTestInto` (MAV_CMD_DO_MOTOR_TEST, PWM 1150 us, 1.5 s); `SessionController.checkMotors` only while warming or READY, disarmed and below 0.3 m; the Run tab's motor-check panel and a ring on the turning rotor in the flight view. The planned `apps/web/src/aircraft/MotorTest.tsx` became `apps/web/src/run/MotorCheck.tsx`, because the check needs a running session. A first fault test swapped two channels inside the JSON link and saw no fault: the link reads a channel and writes the same actuator slot, so the swap cancels; the faults are now injected in the model the physics runs. Evidence against the installed native ArduCopter `8ab6afde` in Node (`tests/motor-check-sitl.test.ts`, 7 passed): every motor of the five S19 configurations passes (quad X tests motors 1, 4, 2, 3, about 1,700 rev/min each; hexa X, octa X, X8 and the 16-motor octa with its scripting frame); motors 1 and 2 on each other's outputs fail on exactly those two ("the motor mapping is swapped"), motor 3 with its spin reversed fails on exactly that one ("the spin is reversed"). `tests/motor-test.test.ts` 5 passed; `tests/run-controller.test.ts` 3 new tests passed (sequence, refusals before parameters, armed, airborne and while running, an ArduPilot refusal with its reason); in Google Chrome `tests/browser/motor-test.spec.ts` passed (57.6 s): through the Run tab the ring followed motors 1, 4, 2, 3, all four passed, and the check is disabled in flight. Full gates: lint and typecheck 0; `npm run test:browser` 114 passed and 16 skipped (12.8 min); `npm test` 1193 passed and 1 skipped with 19 failures: 4 documentation-policy tests on the mirror hashes, refreshed after this review; the `drive-chain` golden hash that fails on main on this Mac; and 14 cases of `tests/launcher.test.ts` and `tests/run-controller.test.ts` whose hooks timed out under the full suite's load (now with this task's real-SITL file beside them) and cascaded into 409 "Another experiment is running", which pass alone (105 of 105); check:docs passes.
- **Title & Description:** (S03) Before flight, spin each motor individually through ArduPilot's motor-test command in SITL. The 3D scheme highlights the motor that physics actually drives, with its spin direction. A mismatch between the expected and the observed output or spin is reported.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-141, TASK-142, TASK-154.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/autopilot/src/motor-test.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/ground-station.ts`, `packages/autopilot/package.json`, `apps/web/src/run/MotorCheck.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/session-controller.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/FlightView.tsx`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `tests/motor-test.test.ts`, `tests/motor-check-sitl.test.ts`, `tests/run-controller.test.ts`, `tests/support/quad-profile.ts`, `tests/browser/motor-test.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** A motor-test sequence per output, disarmed and on the ground only. The result per motor is the expected output, the observed output, the observed spin, and pass or fail.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** All five configurations pass. A deliberately swapped mapping and a reversed spin are detected. The test refuses to run while armed or airborne.

### TASK-158 — Nominal flight acceptance on macOS

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 587d830; 2026-11-04 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). This completes milestone M3. Goal: on the reference Mac (A-01) in Google Chrome, each of the five S19 configurations goes through the application's UI alone from an empty project to a stopped run: component cards and the aircraft profile typed in, the outputs mapped and checked by ArduPilot's own motor test, a start to READY, take-off to 5 m and a hover, a pause and a resume, the landing and the stop. The verdicts of its workflows go into the TASK-132 matrix with the SHA-256 of their evidence. Failure criterion: a verdict not computed from the run's own facts against criteria declared before the run, a cell without its evidence hash, a step done outside the UI, a failed step or a stop that leaves an owned process reported as PASS, or evidence from another host or an earlier run admitted. User decision of 2026-11-03: decision A-04 is amended, and runs with ArduPilot SITL use 800 Hz, because stock ArduCopter refuses to arm while its gyro runs below 1.8 times its 400 Hz loop ([phase1-acceptance.md](decisions/phase1-acceptance.md)). Design. `tests/browser/phase1-nominal.spec.ts` is opt-in (`MIRAGE_PHASE1_NOMINAL=1`) and has one test per configuration. Each creates a project, types the four example cards into the Components tab (the values and synthetic source of the TASK-139 example cards) and builds the TASK-141 recipe in the Aircraft tab with those cards on every motor, the battery card and the NACA 6409 polar. It reads the generated frame and outputs, then, on the Run tab, starts the installed ArduCopter 8ab6afde at real time, runs the motor check, takes off, holds, pauses for 30 s of wall time, resumes, holds again, lands and stops, recording each step's facts or its error. `scripts/phase1/nominal-rules.ts` holds the configurations, the criteria and the verdicts. `scripts/phase1/nominal-flight.mjs` runs the spec in Chrome and writes each configuration's evidence report to `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/`: host, macOS, Chrome, the application's commit and sources, the firmware, the experiment record, and the run's DataFlash files and recording, each with its SHA-256. It writes the verdicts into `docs/validation/phase1-matrix.json` only on the acceptance machine. Criteria, declared before the first run: every reading of a 10 s simulated hold within 0.5 m of 5 m, as in TASK-153 and TASK-156; a pause of at least 30 s of wall time with simulated time, altitude and stepped frames unchanged, and less than 1 s of simulated time between Pause and Resume; the landed CG within 6 mm of the standard gear's rest height; every motor of the motor check passing on the output the Aircraft tab maps it to; READY from the run's own EKF diagnostics, at 800 Hz, with the record naming the build whose binary and default parameters hash as the catalog says; and Stop leaving no process of the run. Workflows written: profile-creation, output-mapping, start, flight-task and pause-resume; the other four per-configuration workflows belong to other tasks. Correction after the first quad run, before any evidence: the landing's servo-frame criterion, taken from TASK-153's run without a pause, required none resent, and the run showed 0 lost, 1 resent and 25 held. While paused, SITL resends the frame it waits on every second (decision D-03), and one resend still on its way when Resume answers that frame is answered again from the stored reply, so the criterion is now no lost frame and at most one resent frame per pause (`resentPerPause`). Exploration before implementation (the headless harness of `tests/external-sitl-flight.test.ts` with each fixture at speedup 4; not evidence): hexa-x, octa-x, coaxial-x8 and octa-coaxial-16 on the example cards reached READY at 41.1 s, held between 4.93 and 5.19 m and landed at their predicted rest heights. Touched Files additions, from the design: the spec and the rules module, `tests/support/aircraft-ui.ts` (a hook before the profile is saved), the evidence reports, the decision record of the A-04 amendment and the engines spec's rate note in both languages, and CLAUDE.md in both languages. Added before the evidence runs: the flight task also needs its capture, a DataFlash file whose VER record names the build, damaged nowhere but possibly cut short at its end by Stop, and a closed recording with nothing dropped; the landing checks for failsafe messages as the pause does; run speed and the power panel are reported and not judged. The runner renders on the Mac's GPU (`MIRAGE_BROWSER_GPU=hardware`), since Playwright's default is a software renderer. First evidence run, 2026-11-04 00:29: all 25 cells passed the criteria declared so far, while the body rates in the flights' own logs showed the quad at 61.40 deg/s of roll rate, standard deviation, against at most 0.15 deg/s on the other four. The altitude criterion cannot see that sustained 10 Hz attitude oscillation (`docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, section "Attitude oscillation of the quad X at stock gains"), which also corrects TASK-096. User decisions of 2026-11-04, offered with that evidence: a steady hover is required, decision A-13: in each hold, the standard deviation of the roll, pitch and yaw rate in the flight's own log is at most 1 deg/s on each axis ([phase1-acceptance.md](decisions/phase1-acceptance.md)). The quad flies its acceptance with `INS_GYRO_FILTER` at 40 Hz, and the other four fly stock settings. Design of the change: a `tuning` step after READY types the setting into the Parameters tab, where the write must be confirmed without needing a restart and the table must read it back; each report's `flownSettings` comes from the flight's own PARM records, which must show the tuning and stock values for the rest; a hold without RATE records fails; and `--stock-settings` flies without the tuning as the negative control, writing `quad-stock-settings.json` and never the matrix. Evidence (MacBook Air Mac17,4, Apple M5, 32 GB, macOS 27.2 build 26B123, built-in display 1710 x 1107 at 60 Hz, Low Power Mode on; Google Chrome 156.0.8078.80 headless, ANGLE Metal on the M5; Node 24.22.1; native ArduCopter V4.8.0-dev 8ab6afde; checkout 587d830 with this change, every uncommitted path and source hashed in the reports): `node scripts/phase1/nominal-flight.mjs` exit 0, 5 passed in 13.3 min, and all 25 TASK-158 cells of the matrix PASS, each citing its report and SHA-256. Every configuration: cards and profile through the UI equal to the example cards and the S19 fixture; its generated frame (FRAME_CLASS 1, 2, 4, and 15 for the scripting matrix of octo and octo-paired-16); the motor check passed for 4, 6, 8, 8 and 16 motors on the mapped outputs; READY from the run's EKF diagnostics at 41.0 to 41.9 s simulated, at 800 Hz; a pause of 31.3 to 31.4 s of wall time with simulated time, altitude and stepped frames unchanged and at most 0.2 s simulated between Pause and Resume; no failsafe message; 0 lost and 0 resent frames; Stop left no process; one DataFlash file (2.5 to 3.4 MB, cut short by Stop only, VER names the build) and a closed recording (6.3 to 8.6 MB, nothing dropped); no report holds the launcher token. The quad's write was confirmed and read back as 40, and its log shows 40 Hz with stock rate gains; the other four logs show stock values. Holds, m, with landed CG against the rest height: quad 5.01 to 5.06, after Resume 5.06 to 5.07, landed 0.10 (0.1000); hexa 5.03 to 5.08, 5.09 to 5.11, 0.11 (0.1149); octo 4.91 to 4.98, 4.98 to 4.99, 0.01 (0.0085); x8 5.09 to 5.15, 5.15 to 5.16, 0.17 (0.1750); octo-paired-16 5.09 to 5.17, 5.17 to 5.18, 0.20 (0.1971). Steadiness, standard deviation of the body rates over the first hold, roll, pitch and yaw, agreeing with pymavlink's reading of the same files to within 0.001 deg/s: quad 0.08, 0.09, 0.03 deg/s; hexa 0.10, 0.22, 0.07; octo 0.12, 0.06, 0.02; x8 0.09, 0.10, 0.01; octo-paired-16 0.06, 0.08, 0.02. After Resume, at most 0.09 deg/s. The worst reading leaves the limit about four times above it. Negative control: `node scripts/phase1/nominal-flight.mjs --configuration quad --stock-settings` exit 1, flight-task FAIL (roll rate 60.44 deg/s, pitch rate 1.15 deg/s), pause-resume FAIL (58.88 deg/s), the other three workflows PASS, the height inside its band (4.99 to 5.07 m), and the matrix untouched. Reported, not judged. Hover draw at the moment sampled: quad 7.7 A and 128 W (14.5 to 24.7 A and 238 to 403 W in its oscillating flights), hexa 15.0 A and 248 W, octo 23.8 A and 389 W, x8 19.6 A and 321 W, octo-paired-16 50.6 A and 796 W, at 15.7 to 16.6 V. Run speed at the Real time setting, on the quiet machine (load average 2 to 3) with Low Power Mode on: 1.001, 1.001, 1.001, 1.001 and 0.998 times real time. A-05's verdict stays with TASK-175, TASK-189 and TASK-191, but this matters for them: during the two hours after the macOS 27.2 update of 2026-11-03 21:07 (load average 40 to 87) the same flights measured 0.30 to 0.84, and commit beda7a5, which measured 1.000 in the morning on macOS 26.6, measured 0.406, so that slow period came from the machine and not from later code. Gates (same machine): `tests/integration/phase1-nominal.test.ts` 16 passed, against a run written out by hand with one copy per missed criterion, the steadiness limit at and just above 1 deg/s on each axis, the flown settings, and the tracked matrix bound to its reports; typecheck, lint of the changed files and build 0; `npm run check:docs` passes. `npm test` 1393 passed and 1 skipped, with 7 failures: the 5 of the documentation policy test on mirror hashes not yet refreshed, which pass after this review; the golden hash of `tests/drive-chain.test.ts`, which fails on main on this Mac too; and `tests/external-sitl-flight.test.ts`, which conflicts with native ArduCopter runs at the same time, as recorded in TASK-095, and passes alone. In Chrome, `tests/browser/aircraft-editor.spec.ts` and `output-mapping.spec.ts`, the users of the changed helper, 8 passed. The full browser suite was not rerun, since no application source changed; the new spec's 5 tests skip there as opt-in.
- **Title & Description:** (S01–S03) Through the UI on a reference Mac: create the profile, map outputs, run the motor test, start, arm, take off, hover, land, pause and resume, and stop. Do this first for a quad with one motor per arm, then for the remaining S19 configurations, including the 16-motor one. Sequential batches (TASK-176) wait for this task.
