### TASK-172 — Flight task library with routes and initial conditions

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline b07dc24; 2026-11-04 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: repeatable takeoff, hover, landing, straight flight, climb, descent, acceleration, braking, turning and route tasks defined in the Flight tasks tab, flown by ArduPilot through guided commands and uploaded missions, with requested and actual values side by side and recorded. Failure criterion: a task that completes without ArduPilot having flown it, a mission reported uploaded without its acknowledgement, a requested trace that is not what was commanded, an initial condition not found in the truth state, or a task definition that does not round-trip or is missing from its experiment. User decision of 2026-11-04 on the airborne start: ground values (position on the pad, heading, battery charge) apply at the start tick before arming, as registered. ArduPilot needs about 41 s of simulated time to become READY, so an aircraft placed in the air at the start tick falls before anything can fly it. An airborne start is therefore a lead-in: ArduPilot takes off and flies to the requested height, heading and speed, and the task's clock starts on arrival, with requested and actual shown there. Roll and pitch cannot be set on their own in the air; TASK-173 commands them. The Interfaces and DoD below are worded accordingly. Design. `packages/scenarios/src/flight-tasks.ts` holds task definition v1 and `FlightTaskRunner`, a pure stage machine: it takes the session's truth and the autopilot's mode, arming and mission reports, and returns the commands to send and the requested values of the moment. Guided stages send SET_POSITION_TARGET_LOCAL_NED with the position, velocity and acceleration of a path smoothed within the limits the autopilot reports, at every observation (every 80 physics steps on the Run tab, 0.1 s at 800 Hz) and so well inside `GUID_TIMEOUT` (`packages/autopilot/src/guided.ts`, `packages/scenarios/src/smooth-motion.ts`). A route is a mission: MISSION_COUNT, each MISSION_REQUEST_INT answered with its MISSION_ITEM_INT, and MISSION_ACK (`packages/autopilot/src/mission.ts`), flown in AUTO after a guided take-off, with progress from MISSION_ITEM_REACHED. Requested altitudes are above the pad, as the Run tab shows them; the commands subtract the CG's rest height measured at arming. The Run tab runs a stored task on the READY session and shows requested next to actual; the recording gains the requested series on the physics tick; experiment payload schema 5 stores the task definition with its project item, and `flight task` joins the inputs a repeat keeps and a variant may change. Plan, in increments: (1) definition v1 and the runner against a simulated vehicle; (2) guided and mission packing and the upload protocol; (3) every task type against native ArduCopter in `tests/integration/flight-tasks-sitl.test.ts`; (4) ground initial conditions in the physics session, verified in the truth state at the start tick; (5) the controller, the recording, the experiment record and the project item; (6) the Flight tasks tab with its route editor, requested against actual on the Run tab, and the route drawn in the flight view. Completion tolerances are operational, declared here before the first flight, and do not judge fidelity (SA-01): a climb or take-off stage ends within 0.5 m of its altitude with vertical speed below 0.3 m/s; a heading stage within 5°; a speed stage within 0.5 m/s; a route leg on ArduPilot's own MISSION_ITEM_REACHED. The end state of a take-off, vertical, speed or turn stage must hold for 2 s of simulated time, on the task's clock too, so a measured trace shows any overshoot (added by the correction below; the tolerances are unchanged). Flights in these tests set the quad's 40 Hz gyro filter (A-13). Evidence on the M5 reference Mac, macOS 27.2, stock ArduCopter 8ab6afde, speed 4, 800 Hz, quad X with the 40 Hz gyro filter: `npx vitest run tests/integration/flight-tasks-sitl.test.ts` 15 passed alone, no lockstep duplicates or gaps, observed every 80 physics steps as the Run tab observes. Simulated seconds per flight: takeoff 64.1, hover 74.1, landing 64.1, straight 100.1, climb 88.1, descent 81.1, acceleration 95.1, braking 91.1, turn 94.1, route 89.1, climb at 5 m/s 109.1, descent at 5 m/s 92.1, airborne hover 87.1, straight from another place 96.1, route from another place 88.1. Correction of 2026-11-04: the user asked why the first flights' gaps were so large, and to fix them. ArduPilot's own log (PSCD, PSCN, PSCE, GUIP, ATT, XKF1, with LOG_DISARMED for the warmup) separated two causes, neither of them the physics or the control loops. First, the targets had sharp corners. In the first climb the aircraft stayed within 5 cm and 0.05 m/s of ArduPilot's plan throughout, while that plan accelerated at exactly WP_ACC_Z, 1 m/s², and the target's 2 m/s ran ahead of it: 2 s and about 2 m behind, the correction adds to the target's velocity, so the plan flew about 4.2 m/s, and with 1.9 m/s left at the end it needed about 1.8 m to stop. Targets now carry position, velocity and acceleration on a path smoothed within WP_ACC_Z, PSC_D_JERK, PSC_NE_JERK and ATC_ACC_Y_MAX, read from the autopilot, so what is requested is what can be flown: the climb reaches 2 m/s after 2.2 s and takes 7.2 s instead of 5 s. Second, EKF3 learned a false gyro error at boot. The first suspect, SIM_DRIFT_SPEED, was refuted by flying with it at 0: the gap barely changed. A run boots with an empty memory, so at 5.4 s ArduPilot set its first heading without COMPASS_DEC and was 12.8° off, the local declination. GPS swung the heading back, and EKF3 took part of that swing for a gyro error of -1.76 °/s, unlearned only to -0.18 °/s at arming; ArduPilot's own heading stayed within 0.02° of the command while the true heading crept away. A run the launcher starts now gets COMPASS_DEC at boot from ArduPilot's own table for its home, and the learned error stays near +0.02 °/s, the simulated gyro drift. Largest difference between requested and actual during the measured part, now with the 2 s hold after each change, shown and not judged (SA-01), first flights then now: climb 5 to 15 m at 2 m/s altitude 2.38 then 0.10 m, climb rate 2.25 then 0.14 m/s, highest 17.28 then 14.96 m; descent 15 to 5 m at 1 m/s altitude 0.74 then 0.09 m, lowest 4.26 then 5.01 m; hover altitude 0.08 then 0.07 m, heading 0.88 then 0.44°; straight speed 0.08 then 0.03 m/s, heading 1.99 then 0.54°, altitude 0.04 m both; acceleration speed 0.78 then 0.38 m/s; braking speed 1.24 then 0.52 m/s; turn speed 0.22 then 0.09 m/s, heading 2.06 then 1.34°; the stop after an airborne start 3.04 then 0.69 m/s. Values no choice of request can change, over the whole flight: highest speed asking 5 m/s 6.67 then 5.52, asking 8 m/s 9.10 then 8.48; largest distance from the requested position 3.96 then 0.49 m (acceleration), 1.67 then 0.48 m (braking). What remains is the aircraft: it accelerates by tilting, and the lean follows the demand about 0.2 s late, so a speed change at 2 m/s² differs by about 0.5 m/s at its corners. Softer corners barely help: PSC_NE_JERK at 2.5 and at 1 instead of 5 gives braking 0.46 and 0.40 m/s, with the ramp at 4.9 and 6.1 s instead of 4.4 s and the position gap at 0.57 and 0.95 m, so ArduPilot's default stays. The turn's 1.34° comes at its end: yaw is the quad's weakest axis, and it runs on about 1° before it stops. At the top of what a task may ask: 5 m/s up and down differs by 0.17 m; 12 to 0 m/s at 5 m/s² by 1.81 m/s, and 0 to 12 m/s at 5 m/s² by 1.83 m/s, which need a 27° lean; 30 °/s at 8 m/s reaches ArduPilot's 30° lean limit and differs by 2.68 m/s. Route points were passed within 1.77 m and the last within 0.08 m, from the pad and from 10 m north, 5 m west: ArduPilot rounds a corner between waypoints and stops only at the last. An airborne start asking 8 m, 3 m/s, 45° arrived at 7.97 m, 2.96 m/s, 45.4°. COMPASS_DEC from the table matches the value ArduCopter itself reports at the default home and at Zurich, within 1e-6 rad. From the Run tab, through the Worker and the bridge, measured from the recording: over two runs the climb differs by 0.10 to 0.11 m and peaks at 14.95 to 14.96 m, and the braking differs by 0.53 m/s, as the Node flights do (`tests/browser/flight-tasks.spec.ts`, 3 passed in Google Chrome, headless, on the M5 GPU). That spec also shows the ground start 10 m north, 5 m west, heading 90°, 80 % charge in the first recorded truth and battery rows, where the Run tab flies a route typed in the Flight tasks tab, the recording holds its requests, and Repeat keeps the task. The traces go to the ignored `test-results/validation/task-172-flights.json` and `task-172-browser-flights.json`. Not covered: the Flight tasks tab offers no tuning maneuvers (TASK-173).
- **Title & Description:** (S10) Provide repeatable takeoff, hover, landing, straight flight, climb, descent, acceleration, braking and turning tasks. Route points, altitudes and speeds are entered through the UI. ArduPilot receives the commands (mission upload or guided commands), and physics calculates the resulting motion. Initial position, attitude, speed and battery charge are configurable; mass and payload stay in the aircraft profile. Requested and actual values are shown side by side.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-150, TASK-153, TASK-165.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/scenarios/src/flight-tasks.ts`, `packages/scenarios/src/smooth-motion.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/site-parameters.ts`, `data/ardupilot/declination.json`, `scripts/generate-declination.mjs`, `apps/web/src/params/ParameterExport.tsx`, `config/offline-inventory.json`, `docs/operations/offline-inventory.md`, `docs/ru/offline-inventory.md`, `tests/smooth-motion.test.ts`, `tests/site-parameters.test.ts`, `tests/offline-inventory.test.ts`, `tests/browser/run-panel.spec.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/mission.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/guided.ts`, `apps/web/src/tasks/FlightTasksTab.tsx`, `apps/web/src/tasks/RouteEditor.tsx`, `apps/web/src/tasks/flight-task-form.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/FlightTaskPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/session-controller.ts`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `apps/web/src/physics.worker.ts`, `apps/web/src/logging/run-recorder.ts`, `apps/web/src/experiments/ExperimentsTab.tsx`, `apps/web/src/shell/AppShell.tsx`, `packages/autopilot/src/ground-station.ts`, `packages/physics/src/profile-dynamics.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/experiment.ts`, `packages/project/src/schema.ts`, `packages/aar/src/signal-catalog.ts`, `packages/aar/src/recorder-v2.ts`, `tests/flight-tasks.test.ts`, `tests/integration/flight-tasks-sitl.test.ts`, `tests/browser/flight-tasks.spec.ts`, `tests/run-controller.test.ts`, `tests/experiment-record.test.ts`, `tests/profile-dynamics.test.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Task definition v1 (type, parameters, route, initial conditions) stored with the experiment. Missions use the MAVLink mission protocol with acknowledgements. Ground initial conditions (position on the pad, heading, battery charge) are applied at the start tick before arming; an airborne start is a lead-in that ArduPilot flies to the requested height, heading and speed before the task's clock starts.

### TASK-173 — Tuning maneuvers with requested-versus-actual response

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline b0c791e; 2026-11-04 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: commanded steps and doublets in roll, pitch, heading and altitude, with requested against actual shown, and rise time, overshoot and settling computed from the recorded series on one time base. Failure criterion: a metric that disagrees with the analytic second-order fixture beyond the tolerances declared before the implementation, a metric computed from anything but the recording, a missing value shown as a number, lines drawn on different time scales, a maneuver reported as flown without ArduPilot having flown it, or a recording stored before this task that no longer opens. Design. A maneuver is a flight task of one of eight new types, so TASK-172's definition, runner, recording, experiment record and Repeat apply unchanged. Definition schema 2 adds those types; a task of an earlier type stays schema 1, byte for byte. Roll and pitch go as SET_ATTITUDE_TARGET, added to the generated message table (message 82, CRC_EXTRA 49), with the thrust field at 0.5, which holds the height; heading and altitude go as position targets. A request of roll or pitch is a new signal, `events.flightTaskAttitude` (70), because a row must hold exactly its signal's channels and more channels on `events.flightTask` would make stored recordings unreadable. The metrics describe and no limit applies (SA-01): see "Tuning maneuvers" in the engine specification for their definitions. The Touched Files grew beyond the registered list by the wiring above and three files of `apps/web/src/analysis/`. Numerical tolerances, declared in `tests/maneuvers.test.ts` before the implementation from the error of linear interpolation: at 1000 Hz delay, rise and settling within 0.1 ms and overshoot within 0.01 points; at 50 Hz delay and rise within 3 ms, settling within 5 ms and overshoot within 0.2 points. Evidence on the M5 reference Mac, macOS 27.2, stock ArduCopter 8ab6afde, speed 4, 800 Hz, quad X with the 40 Hz gyro filter, truth at the Logging tab's default 50 Hz. Metrics: `npx vitest run tests/maneuvers.test.ts tests/response-series.test.ts`, all within those tolerances across ζ 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1 and 1.5, 1000 and 50 Hz, bands of 5 and 2 %. Flights: `npx vitest run tests/integration/flight-tasks-sitl.test.ts -t maneuver`, 8 passed, no lockstep duplicates or gaps, each change on its timeline's moment within 1 ms, shown and not judged; the whole file, these and TASK-172's 15 flights, passes alone. ArduPilot's noise is unseeded, so repeated flights differ in the last digit given here. Roll step of 10°, hold 2 s: rise 0.34 s then 0.29 s back, overshoot 0 then 2.5 %, settling at 5 % 0.54 then 0.44 s, end error -0.23°. Roll doublet: rise 0.34, 0.24, 0.33 s, overshoot 0, 1.9, 0 %, settling 0.54, 0.40, 0.53 s. Pitch step of -10°: rise 0.30 then 0.32 s, overshoot 1.9 then 0 %, settling 0.45 then 0.50 s. Heading step of 45°, hold 6 s: delay 0.25 s, rise 0.57 s, overshoot 7.2 %, settling 1.44 s. Heading doublet of 30°, hold 5 s: rise 0.40, 0.76, 0.41 s, overshoot 8.9, 5.3, 7.4 %, settling 1.35, 1.46, 1.24 s. Altitude step of 3 m, hold 8 s: delay 0.90 s, rise 1.87 s, overshoot 0.6 %, settling 2.98 s. Altitude doublet of 2 m, hold 6 s: rise 1.52, 2.36, 1.47 s, overshoot 1.2, 0, 2.3 %, settling 2.45, 3.64, 2.39 s. What the numbers come from, as far as checked: the height's delay and rise follow WP_ACC_Z (1 m/s²), since 10 % of 3 m takes about 0.8 s at that acceleration, and yaw is the quad's weakest axis, as TASK-172 found. The roll's rise was not separated into ArduPilot's shaping of the request (ATC_INPUT_TC, 0.10 s for Copter) and the aircraft's own response. From the Run tab, through the Worker and the bridge, measured from the recording, `tests/browser/maneuvers.spec.ts` in Google Chrome, headless, on the M5 GPU, 2 passed: roll doublet rise 0.33, 0.24, 0.33 s and settling 0.53, 0.40, 0.53 s, altitude step delay 0.90 s, rise 1.87 s, settling 2.98 s, as the Node flights give, so the path through the page adds no measurable delay at speed 4. That spec also finds the numbers on the page equal to those it computes from the recording's own rows, and the request's drawn corners and the actual line's halfway crossing at their recorded moments. A correction found by flying: a true roll of 9.76° for a request of 10°, with ArduPilot's estimate at 9.97°. ArduPilot's log showed the error present before take-off and its learned gyro bias climbing on all three axes, which pointed to its simulated gyro drift. Flown with SIM_DRIFT_SPEED at 0 the end error falls from -0.23° to 0.00°, the rise is 0.31 s both ways and the overshoot 0.4 %. ArduPilot's default stays, by the decision of TASK-089, and the Experiments tab explains this beside a roll or pitch. Traces: the ignored `test-results/validation/task-173-flights.json` and `task-173-browser-flights.json`. Not covered: oscillation frequency and damping ratio, which `docs/decisions/phase1-fidelity.md` lists for TASK-184, and frequency sweeps.
- **Title & Description:** (S10) Command changes in roll, pitch, heading and altitude (steps and doublets with configurable amplitude and duration), and display requested versus actual response with rise-time, overshoot and settling metrics.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-162, TASK-172.

### TASK-174 — Manual flight with a controller in integrated sessions

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 48b4579; 2026-11-03 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: fly an integrated ArduPilot run with the calibrated USB controller through RC override, with stale input, unplugging and reconnection under the input-safety rules. Failure criterion: thrust held on stale input, a disconnected controller that leaves the aircraft flying on its last sticks, or another transmitter taking over silently. User decision of 2026-11-03: losing the controller in flight pauses the run (the TASK-156 path, reason `controller`). Design: the session controller sends RC_CHANNELS_OVERRIDE at 25 Hz from the `GamepadAdapter` through `PilotRcMapper` and `GroundStation.rcOverrideInto`, commanding the mode itself (altitude hold, loiter or stabilize; in flight only the first two), and arms on the ground only with the throttle at most 5 % (the TASK-038 rearm rule); an unplugged, invalid or different controller pauses the run; a watchdog in the physics Worker pauses the session before the next SITL frame when Main sent no override for `INPUT_TIMEOUT_MS` (250 ms, the existing input timeout), because a stalled main thread cannot notice its own silence and ArduPilot would apply the last override for its RC_OVERRIDE_TIME; Resume waits for a connected controller, goes on in manual control with a device that never left, and otherwise releases control to GUIDED, where ArduPilot holds position, since a replugged or swapped controller is a new device (TASK-038). Found by this task's browser test and fixed in TASK-160's Parameters tab: its export preview was recomputed and redrawn at every Worker status even while hidden, which blocked the main thread for about 300 ms and tripped the watchdog; it now keys on the change list and the list revision, and a hidden tab draws nothing. The registered `apps/web/src/runtime.ts` and `packages/mavlink/src/rc-override.ts` needed no change; the files below were changed. Evidence (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Playwright 1.56.1 Chromium): `tests/run-controller.test.ts` 108 passed, five new cases (refusals, mode and arm with the throttle down and overrides at 25 Hz with their PWM, an unplug that pauses with Resume waiting for the controller, a swap whose Resume releases to GUIDED with the release sentinels, the Worker's stale-input pause and a landing that ends manual control, an in-flight takeover); `tests/sitl-bridge-client.test.ts` passed with the watchdog on a real session (overrides every 40 ms keep stepping; 250 ms of silence is fresh; 1 ms more pauses before the frame, unanswered; the running state after a pump never refreshes it). `tests/browser/manual-flight.spec.ts` with the TASK-152 installation of ArduCopter 8ab6afde and a mocked controller through the Run tab alone (1 passed, 1.7 min): with the throttle up the takeover was refused, with it down ArduPilot went to ALTHOLD and armed; raising the throttle climbed above 4 m and centring it held 6.5 m within 1 m for 4 s; a 1.5 s stall of the page paused the run through the Worker's watchdog (252 ms of silence) and Resume went on in manual control; unplugging paused the run with simulated time standing and Resume refused until the controller was back; the replugged controller did not take over, Resume set GUIDED, which held 6.56 m within 1 m; manual control was taken again in flight and released; the run landed and stopped. Full gates (same machine): lint of the changed files, typecheck and build 0; `npm run check:docs` passes; `npm test` 1343 passed and 1 skipped, its failures `tests/drive-chain.test.ts`, which fails on main on this Mac too, and seven launcher-based tests (`tests/run-controller.test.ts` with the real launcher and the fake SITL, `tests/dataflash-capture.test.ts`) whose 10 s setup hook timed out under the full suite's load; run alone, those two files pass 115 of 115; `npm run test:browser` 125 passed and 16 skipped (35.6 min), none failed.
- **Title & Description:** (S10) Fly ArduPilot with the calibrated USB controller through RC override over the bridge. Stale input, unplugging and reconnection follow the existing input-safety rules, and the pilot-link-loss effect (TASK-168) acts on this path.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-038, TASK-153.
- **Touched Files:** `apps/web/src/run/session-controller.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/ManualControl.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `apps/web/src/sitl/bridge-client.ts`, `apps/web/src/physics.worker.ts`, `apps/web/src/params/ParametersTab.tsx`, `packages/physics/src/session-v2.ts`, `packages/autopilot/src/ground-station.ts`, `tests/run-controller.test.ts`, `tests/sitl-bridge-client.test.ts`, `tests/browser/manual-flight.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** RC override at a declared rate, with the channel-group sentinels of TASK-038. Disconnecting never silently selects another transmitter.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** A browser test with a mocked gamepad arms and flies SITL. Unplugging triggers the defined policy. Thrust is never held on stale input.

### TASK-175 — Real-time and accelerated runs with visible actual speed

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline f5d1e0d; 2026-11-03 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: real-time and accelerated runs with the same physics and step sequence, the actual speed and overload visible, and a run that falls behind never presented as real time. Failure criterion: a mode that changes the step sequence or skips steps, a displayed speed that differs from the measured ratio, a real-time run below A-05's 0.99 average called real time, or a dropped interval left out. Design: in JSON lockstep each physics step answers one SITL frame, so the modes differ only in ArduPilot's `--speedup` (1 real time; 2 or 4 a cap; 100, the launcher's largest, as fast as possible) and the registered `clock-policy.ts` and `physics.worker.ts` needed no change; `RunSpeedMeter` in the session controller samples each Worker status, leaves paused time out, and gives the window and average speeds, the 1 s intervals of a real-time run that advanced less than 0.99 s, and the A-05 verdict. Lead default, open to change: the run is judged from the autopilot's first heartbeat, because counting SITL's boot made every real-time run read as behind (the first browser run measured 0.958 with 10 s of wall time for 2 s of simulated time before the autopilot was up); the start-up is shown on its own, not hidden. Evidence (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Playwright 1.56.1 Chromium): `tests/run-speed.test.ts` 8 passed, the meter against hand-computed ratios and a real `PhysicsSessionV2` of the quad profile in sitl-lockstep giving byte-identical state lines for 1106 servo frames delivered at real time and at 25 times that pace, then the same accident report (propellers 2 and 4, tick 1106) at both; `tests/run-controller.test.ts` 103 passed. `tests/browser/run-speed.spec.ts` with the TASK-152 installation of ArduCopter 8ab6afde through the Run tab alone (1 passed, 2.2 min): the real-time run averaged 1.000 and was shown as Real time with its dropped intervals listed (in the first rerun 8, the first 5 s after the first heartbeat running at 0.23 to 0.34 before ArduPilot caught up), its speed over the test's 20 s reported 1.001 against 0.996 measured by the test; the as-fast-as-possible run averaged 1.837 on this machine, reported 1.745 against 1.753 measured, never judged against real time; start-up took about 2 s of wall time for 1.00 s of simulated time; and the two runs' recorded truth.position rows over their first 20 s of simulated time (1000 rows) were identical. The first full browser suite failed this test because it compared the test's 12 s measurement with the panel's 5 s window, which differed by 7.2 % in the as-fast-as-possible run, whose speed varies; the test now compares the meter's own ratio over the same 20 s, with the declared 5 % unchanged. Full gates (same machine): lint of the changed files, typecheck and build 0; `npm run check:docs` passes; `npm test` 1344 passed and 1 skipped, its only failure `tests/drive-chain.test.ts`, which fails on main on this Mac too; `npm run test:browser` 123 passed and 16 skipped (32.8 min) with that one failure, and the corrected test passes alone (1 passed, 2.2 min).
- **Title & Description:** (S10) Run the same physics with the same step sequence in real-time and accelerated modes. Acceleration is limited only by the host and is never achieved by simplifying the model or skipping steps. Actual simulation speed and overload are visible, and a run that falls behind is never presented as real-time. No fixed acceleration factor is promised.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-132, TASK-153, TASK-156.
- **Touched Files:** `apps/web/src/run/run-speed.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/SpeedIndicator.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/session-controller.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `tests/run-speed.test.ts`, `tests/browser/run-speed.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Run-speed modes `real-time` and `as-fast-as-possible`. The displayed speed is the measured simulated/wall ratio over a window. Dropped intervals are counted and shown.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** Seeded trajectories in both modes agree within a tolerance declared before the test. The displayed speed equals the measured ratio. A real-time run with drops is labeled as such.

### TASK-176 — Sequential user-defined parameter batches

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline f0c4911; 2026-11-04 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: several parameter sets the user supplies run one after another on one scenario chosen on the Run tab, with every other input identical, and the results collect in one record. Failure criterion: two runs of a batch that differ in anything but the set, a failed run that stops or corrupts another, a retry, or any path that chooses values. Design: an `experiment-batch` item (`packages/project/src/batch.ts`), a new version after each run, with a second run of one set invalid; a set's values are the run's initial parameters, tagged `set`; the contract is in the engine specification, "Parameter batches". Browser test on the reference Mac (M5, Chrome, native ArduCopter 8ab6afde at 4×), with nobody touching the Run tab: three sets on a roll doublet of ±10° with 2 s holds. Set 1 has ATC_ANG_RLL_P 3, set 2 names NO_SUCH_PARAM and set 3 has ATC_ANG_RLL_P 6. Set 2 failed with "ArduPilot did not confirm the initial parameters: NO_SUCH_PARAM does not exist in this ArduPilot build", and set 3 still flew. ArduPilot's own parameter report in the recordings holds 3 and 6, `experimentChanges` between the two gives only `initial parameters`, a Repeat of set 3 has the same set and ArduPilot reports 6 again, and after a reload the stored batch opens with its three runs and the reason. The same flow passed on a 5 m hover before the test moved to the doublet. Response at the 5 % band, ATC_ANG_RLL_P 3 against 6, read from the recordings and shown in the panel: 0 to 10°, rise 0.341 against 0.331 s and settling 0.541 against 0.534 s; 10 to −10°, rise 0.241 against 0.230 s, overshoot 2.3 against 1.7 % and settling 0.409 against 0.395 s; −10 to 0°, rise 0.341 against 0.334 s and settling 0.540 against 0.544 s. Shown and not judged (SA-01). Two numbers that looked wrong were traced. A 5 m task reads 4.90 m highest because truth reached 5.00 m above the ground and that column counts from where the aircraft stood, 0.10 m up; its header now says so. A 20° change rises faster than a 10° one, which four more native flights separated: near rest, 5° takes 0.37 and 0.36 s, 10° takes 0.336 and 0.332 s and 20° takes 0.252 s; at a sideways speed of 1.3 to 5.7 m/s, 10° takes 0.29 s and 20° takes 0.24 s, with overshoot of 1.9 to 2.8 % against none from rest. So size is the large part and speed the small part. ArduPilot's own DesRoll (ATT at 10 Hz) shows the size effect, about 0.36 s at 10° and 0.30 s at 20°, with its roll within about 0.5° of that target, so most of it is ArduPilot's command and not the flight model. The remaining 0.02 to 0.04 s and the line of ArduPilot's shaper responsible were not traced. A batch cut short by a closed browser is not resumed: its record says how far it got. Regression: `tsc`, lint and `npm run build` exit 0; `npm test` gives 1700 passed, 1 skipped and 3 failed, the 3 being the known Mac difference in `tests/drive-chain.test.ts` and two native flights that overlapped others ("Arm: RC not found"), and those flight files pass alone (`tests/external-sitl-flight.test.ts` and all of `tests/integration/flight-tasks-sitl.test.ts`); the 23 browser suites that touch the Run, Experiments and Logging tabs give 41 passed and 6 skipped.
- **Title & Description:** (S12) The user supplies several parameter sets. The same scenario runs automatically and sequentially for each set with all other inputs identical, and the collected results can be inspected. The system does not search for new values or declare a set fit for real flight.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-158, TASK-161, TASK-165, TASK-172.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/project/src/batch.ts`, `packages/project/src/schema.ts`, `packages/project/src/project.schema.json`, `packages/project/src/store.ts`, `packages/project/package.json`, `packages/aircraft/src/experiment.ts`, `apps/web/src/experiments/batch-runner.ts`, `apps/web/src/experiments/batch-form.ts`, `apps/web/src/experiments/BatchPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/experiments/ExperimentsTab.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/session-link.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/shell/AppShell.tsx`, `apps/web/src/style.css`, `tests/batch.test.ts`, `tests/experiment-record.test.ts`, `tests/browser/batch.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Batch record: scenario, parameter sets and the resulting experiment IDs. Failed runs are recorded and never retried silently.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** N sets produce N experiments with identical non-parameter inputs. One failed run does not corrupt the others. There is no automatic optimization path.

### TASK-177 — Experiment comparison and result metrics

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline f0c4911; 2026-11-04 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: compare two flown experiments, different conditions for one aircraft or equal conditions for different configurations, with the inputs held equal and those that differ listed, the components' own mass accounted for, and takeoff capability, thrust margin, RPM, current, power, energy, estimated flight duration, position and maneuver response and limit exceedances side by side, the calculation mode and confidence label in view. Failure criterion: a metric that disagrees with a hand-computed fixture, a held input that differs without a flag, a missing value shown as a number, a recording that cannot say whether limits were exceeded reported as having none, or a duration estimate without its scenario and reserve. Design: see "Experiment comparison" in the engine specification, which declares the metrics with units and formulas. `packages/analysis` is pure and reads a stored recording. The comparison is a panel of the Experiments tab, because the twelve tabs of TASK-137 are fixed, so `CompareTab.tsx` became `ComparePanel.tsx`. Two things this task needed that did not exist. The limit monitor's events reached only the page's memory, so a new signal, `events.limit` (71), records them, with a first row that lets an older recording say it does not know. And nothing computed the thrust at full command, so `thrustCapacity` does, in a Worker, on the rotor engine and battery of flight. Evidence on the M5 reference Mac, macOS 27.2, stock ArduCopter 8ab6afde, speed 4, 800 Hz, quad X with the 40 Hz gyro filter, truth at 50 Hz. Metrics and comparison: `npx vitest run tests/experiment-metrics.test.ts tests/experiment-compare.test.ts`, passing, each expected figure's arithmetic beside it. Thrust: `npx vitest run tests/thrust-capacity.test.ts`, against the flight dynamics on a body 100 000 times heavier on a stiff pack, within the 0.5 % set beforehand: 93.38 against 93.37 N (quad X), 139.88 against 139.87 N (hexa X), 186.62 against 186.61 N (octa X), 186.73 against 186.72 N (coaxial X8), 373.30 against 373.29 N (16 motors). On its own pack the quad gives 78.45 N at 15.29 V and 83.65 A, with 1279 W on both sides. From the Run tab, `tests/browser/compare-experiments.spec.ts` in Google Chrome, headless, on the M5 GPU, a hover at 5 m for 10 s on two configurations, the second on motors 20 g heavier rated at 10 W, shown and not judged: thrust over weight 5.333 and 5.063, mean current 7.68 and 8.19 A, mean rotor speed 4403 and 4501 rev/min, energy 0.355 and 0.375 Wh, 31.2 and 29.3 minutes to a 20 % reserve, the 10 W rating exceeded on all four motors for about 26.8 s, and mass 0.420 and 0.500 kg of motors with the stated mass up by the same 0.080 kg. The page's numbers equal what the test works out from the recording's rows and the cards by its own arithmetic, nothing is flagged, and asked as one aircraft under different conditions the same two flag their motors and mass. A finding, left open and not fixed here because it changes the flight model's hot path: in flight the battery bus flips every tick between two voltages at high demand, from duty 1.00 on the quad to duty 0.60 on 16 motors, whose hover is at 0.50, and a pack at its current limit is flagged while the motors go on drawing what they ask, up to 2.45 times what it delivers on 16 motors, so thrust rises with the limit. See "Thrust at full command". Hover and gentle flight are stable and unaffected. `thrustCapacity` solves the true steady state instead, and the tab says when the pack limits an aircraft: 16 motors on one example pack give a ratio of 1.84, where a flight reaches 3.43. Until that is fixed, recorded current, power and energy at very high demand are one phase of that swing, and thrust in a flight at the limit is too high. Also seen, not changed: `experimentChanges` of TASK-165 reports sensors as changed for any change of profile, because a snapshot names its profile version, and stored lineages hold that. Traces: the ignored `test-results/validation/task-177-browser-flights.json`. Not covered: comparing more than two experiments at once, which TASK-176 collects, and accounting for a CG or inertia change beyond flagging that they differ. Correction of 2026-11-04: TASK-197 closed the finding above; a flight now settles on the thrust at full command, and a pack at its limit holds the motors back.
- **Title & Description:** (S12) Copy a profile, change motors, propellers or other settings, and compare experiments: different conditions for one aircraft, or equal conditions for different configurations. Controlled comparisons preserve payload, battery, weather and task as selected, and account explicitly for changes in the components' own mass. Results include takeoff capability and thrust margin; RPM, current, power and energy; estimated flight duration for a stated scenario; position and maneuver response; and component limit exceedances. The calculation mode and input confidence stay visible.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-140, TASK-146, TASK-165, TASK-173.

## Stage P1.8: Log Analysis with UAVLogViewer

Scope step 8. Integrate the existing UAVLogViewer for DataFlash and experiment logs (S14), building on TASK-130.

### TASK-178 — DataFlash reader and writer

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a0c6e99; 2026-10-30. Registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md (baseline 384e974). Goal: a DataFlash reader whose field values and times equal pymavlink 2.4.50's `mavlogdump.py --format json`, and a writer for MirageSITL experiment records that pymavlink and the reader read back unchanged. Design. Reference log: the first 100,011 bytes, which end on a record boundary, of the ArduCopter V4.8.0-dev (8ab6afde) SITL log captured by the TASK-147 run without a sensor configuration; it has FMT, FMTU, UNIT, MULT, PARM, VER, FILE and GPS records and the flight's main telemetry. `scripts/generate-dataflash-fixtures.py` runs `mavlogdump.py --format json` on it and writes `tests/fixtures/dataflash/reference.json` with every record's type, timestamp and field values. Floats that JSON cannot hold are written as the strings `NaN`, `Infinity` and `-Infinity`, and FILE.Data, which pymavlink returns as raw bytes, as hex. The units come from a separate resolution in the script over the log's UNIT, MULT and FMTU records. The reader (`reader.ts`) is incremental: `push(chunk)` and `finish()`. Like pymavlink, it searches for the header 0xA3 0x95 followed by a defined type, starting from the built-in FMT definition (type 128, 89 bytes). Damaged ranges are reported with their byte offsets: skipped bytes up to the next valid header; a final record that the file cuts short (`truncated`); and an FMT record that the reader rejects (`bad-format`) because a format character is unknown or its Length differs from the size of its format. The output is a plain object that a Worker can transfer. It has one group per message definition, with the type, name, format, count, the byte offset of each record and the global record index. The columns are typed. The integer and float types keep their wire type: int8, uint8, int16, uint16, int32, uint32, float32, float64, and int64 and uint64 as BigInt64Array and BigUint64Array. The scaled formats c, C, e, E and L are float64 divided by 100 or 10^7, as pymavlink divides; g is a half float widened to float32; a is int16[32]; and n, N and Z are fixed-width bytes with pymavlink's text decoding (UTF-8, else Latin-1, cut at the first NUL). Each column has its unit and multiplier from FMTU, resolved against the log's final UNIT and MULT tables, and a value scale, which is 1 where the format already scales the value. pymavlink instead resolves each FMTU against the tables at the point it reads the FMTU, so in ArduPilot logs, where FMTU records precede UNIT, it loses units; this reader does not copy that. Times reproduce pymavlink's microsecond clock. The timebase comes from the first GPS record with GWk > 0: (GPS epoch + week + GMS) − 18 s − TimeUS. A record whose first column is TimeUS gets timebase + TimeUS·10^-6, and any other record repeats the previous time. Logs whose GPS records have no GWk, GMS or TimeUS give `clock: 'unsupported'`, and their timestamps are NaN. The writer (`writer.ts`) writes, in order: the FMT records, the UNIT and MULT tables, and FMT and FMTU for four MirageSITL messages, whose names start with `MS`. In the pinned ArduPilot source no four-character log name starts with `MS`. The messages are MSTR (truth: latitude, longitude, altitude, NED position and velocity, attitude in degrees and body rates in degrees per second), MSMO (per-motor command, rpm, thrust, torque, current and voltage, with instance `#`), MSEV (events with a kind and a 64-character text) and MSPC (parameter changes: name, requested, previous and confirmed values, status requested, confirmed or rejected, and request sequence). Values are validated and quantized to their wire types. The generator writes a writer sample with the TS writer from fixed input values, exactly representable in their wire types, and records pymavlink's reading of it. `dataflash.worker.ts` parses a Blob or ArrayBuffer in 1 MiB chunks, posts progress and transfers the result. Damaged-file cases are built from the reference slice by recorded edits (inserted garbage, an unknown type byte, a zeroed block, a cut tail), and pymavlink's reading of each is also recorded. Oracle: pymavlink 2.4.50. Every value is compared exactly: float32 fields as the same double, 64-bit fields as BigInt, timestamps as the same double. Failure criterion: any value, time, count or record order that differs from mavlogdump; a writer sample that pymavlink reads differently from its input; or a damaged range whose offsets differ from the edit. Touched Files additions, made before implementation: `tests/fixtures/dataflash/sitl-copter.bin` (the reference slice) and `tests/browser/dataflash-worker.spec.ts` (the Worker in Chromium). Evidence. `scripts/generate-dataflash-fixtures.py` ran with pymavlink 2.4.50 on the source log (SHA-256 e6961ff9…adbef97, 2,076,672 bytes): slice of 2,259 records and 57 message types; the script's own checks pass: for each of the four damaged copies (37 garbage bytes inserted, an undefined type byte, 300 zeroed bytes, a cut final record), pymavlink loses exactly the recorded records and reports the recorded skipped ranges, and pymavlink reads the 2,682-byte writer sample's eleven records back unchanged. Writing the sample also found a writer defect before any test ran: the FMT for FMT had Length 0 because the size of `n` was missing, which pymavlink rejected; it is fixed and the size lookup now throws on an unknown character. `npx --no-install vitest run tests/dataflash.test.ts`: 26 tests pass; with `tests/fixtures.test.ts` (the two new registered fixtures) 32 pass. Planted faults, each reverted, are caught: multiplying by 10^-7 instead of dividing, a leap-second change, no repeated time, the first-GPS-fix rule, the Latin-1 fallback, half floats, the unit and value-scale rules, the skip start, FMT redefinition and length checks, and the TimeMS rules. Beyond the slice, the reader matches `mavlogdump --format json` on five complete SITL logs (208,868 records and about 2.2 million field values, FILE.Data aside, with no difference); each of those logs ends in a record cut short when SITL stopped, which the reader reports as `truncated` and pymavlink also drops. `npx --no-install playwright test tests/browser/dataflash-worker.spec.ts`: the module Worker parses the slice in 16 KiB chunks in cross-origin-isolated Chromium, with the per-type counts of the reference, BigInt TimeUS and Float64 times, and rejects an unknown message. Full gates: `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` 981 passed and 1 skipped, the four documentation-policy failures being the stale mirror hashes before the refresh; `npm run build` exits 0; the browser suite gives 83 passed and 28 skipped, with the `tests/browser/simulation.spec.ts` stall test at line 81 failing on the known input-failsafe stall that also occurred before this change; alone it passed twice. Remaining: opening a writer file in the pinned UAVLogViewer needs the viewer build of TASK-179, which this container does not have; TASK-180 covers it. Correction of 2026-11-04: a file cut one or two bytes into a record's header read as `skipped`, so `assessDataflash` called an intact log damaged. Of the cuts at each of the last 6,000 byte positions of the reference log, 3.8 % read as damaged, and none after the fix; a real stopped run hit this once in the TASK-176 regression. pymavlink reports nothing for such a tail and no damaged fixture has one, so the comparison with it is unchanged.
- **Title & Description:** (S14, S15) Parse self-describing DataFlash logs (FMT records first) in a Worker: chunked and validated, with damaged ranges reported. Write MirageSITL experiment records (truth, motor telemetry, events, and parameter changes with requested, confirmed and rejected status) as DataFlash files that the viewer can open.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-004.

### TASK-179 — Bundle the pinned UAVLogViewer for offline use

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline a5f26c3; 2026-10-31, finished 2026-11-05 on baseline 6d0c3c8. Implemented and checked on the reference M5. Three patches on the pinned commit (`third_party/uavlogviewer/patches/`): 0000 compatibility (`CMD` without `Frame`; the parser submodule's own worker listener, which parsed every log twice and threw `TypeError` on every `loadType`, registers only when that module is the worker; `_COMMIT_` and `_BUILDDATE_` from `UAVLOGVIEWER_COMMIT` and `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`), 0001 offline (no analytics, web font, online probe or MapTiler key; Font Awesome 5.2.0 bundled from `@fortawesome/fontawesome-free` with its registry integrity hash; IP geolocation and online imagery only with `?online=1`), 0002 message interface (origin-checked protocol 1: load a log; ready, accepted, rejected with a reason, parsed with the number of message types; `?file=` same-origin only). `scripts/build-uavlogviewer.mjs` checks the source against the lock, applies the patches in a fresh work copy, installs the locked packages, builds with `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` and writes the viewer with `licenses/` (GPL-3.0 texts, patches, corresponding-source README, the notices of 958 production npm packages) and `build-manifest.json`; `scripts/install-macos.mjs` step 5 now builds it into `<launcher home>/uavlogviewer` and skips it when the manifest still matches. Vite serves it at `/uavlogviewer/` (loopback, GET/HEAD, COOP/COEP/CORP, traversal refused) from `MIRAGE_UAVLOGVIEWER_DIR` or the launcher home, and the Analysis tab (`LogViewerHost.tsx`) loads it on first opening, refuses files without the DataFlash header and explains how to install a missing viewer. `scripts/check-uavlogviewer.mjs` now also checks a build's manifest against the lock, the patch series and the files on disk, and a patched build passes only with no request outside loopback. In Google Chrome 156.0.8078.80 on the M5 on 2026-11-05: `node scripts/check-uavlogviewer.mjs` exit 0 for ten native SITL logs (four TASK-127 captures, the TASK-152 quad flight and the five TASK-158 flights of 2026-11-04; 168 or 169 message types, no exception, no request outside loopback, icon font loaded, build identity PASS and equal to the locked digest) and for the lock's older-firmware log, 65,536 bytes with its recorded SHA-256, ArduPlane V3.8.2-dev, 148 message types, which failed on 2026-10-28 before patch 0; `tests/browser/log-viewer.spec.ts` passes both tests with a real ArduCopter flight log as the reference, which needs `MIRAGE_UAVLOGVIEWER_DIR` when the launcher home is elsewhere, or that test skips. ### TASK-181 — Two-flight and two-interval comparison in the viewer

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline feb91c9 with TASK-170; 2026-11-05 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: two flights, or two intervals of one, compared in the integrated viewer. ## Stage P1.9: Experiments from Logs and Fidelity

Scope step 9. Real logs are optional; when present they seed experiments and fidelity evidence (S15).

### TASK-182 — Create an experiment from a real log

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline feb91c9 with TASK-180; 2026-11-05 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: a log and an interval say field by field what they give, the user supplies what is missing, and the run that plans starts. Failure criterion: a value the log does not hold, a value without its source message, a missing item not listed, or a run presented as a replay. The Experiments tab's **Create experiment from log** reads the log with `extractFromLog` and reports 16 fields as `extracted` with the source message, `user-supplied` or `missing`, says which values were read but will not be used, and lists the parameters that stay out of the run with the reason. The aircraft is always missing and must be chosen. The run starts on the ground at the log's home with its settings, on stock simulated sensors, and its `log-extraction` record, which depends on the experiment, says which settings the run really took. Evidence (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 27.2, Node 24.22.1, Google Chrome 156): `tests/log-to-experiment.test.ts` 11 passed against pymavlink 2.4.50's reading of the reference log (`tests/fixtures/dataflash/reference.json`); `tests/browser/from-log.spec.ts` 1 passed on native ArduCopter `8ab6afde`: a refused file, a refused interval, the report, the aircraft supplied, READY with the home -35.362938°, 149.165085° and `set` parameters in the experiment. Remaining: comparing the new run with the log is TASK-183, and fidelity metrics are TASK-184. The reference log gives one setting that differs from its default, so a log with many settings has only been run through the unit rules, not flown.
- **Title & Description:** (S15) **Create experiment from log** accepts a log and an interval and extracts the available initial conditions, parameters, and task and control information. It asks the user to supply missing inputs through the UI and discloses missing commands, weather or aircraft information. Exact replay of an arbitrary log is not promised, and the lack of a log never disables simulation.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-165, TASK-172, TASK-178.
- **Touched Files:** `packages/analysis/src/log-to-experiment.ts`, `packages/analysis/package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `packages/aircraft/src/experiment.ts`, `packages/project/src/schema.ts`, `packages/project/src/project.schema.json`, `apps/web/src/experiments/FromLogWizard.tsx`, `apps/web/src/experiments/log-plan.ts`, `apps/web/src/experiments/ExperimentsTab.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/session-link.ts`, `apps/web/src/shell/AppShell.tsx`, `tests/log-to-experiment.test.ts`, `tests/browser/from-log.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Extraction report: each field is `extracted`, `user-supplied` or `missing`, with its source message.

### TASK-183 — Re-simulation with changed parameters and comparison with the original flight

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline feb91c9 with TASK-181; 2026-11-05 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: a run from a log with its own or changed parameters, its path computed afresh, compared with the original flight in the viewer and by metrics. Failure criterion: a recorded position reaching the physics, a comparison against another log, or a judgement of either flight. `resimulationInputs` is all the Run tab reads of a log: its home and its settings, with the user's changes on top. The wizard takes those changes, and the `log-extraction` record keeps them. On the Experiments tab `FromLogCompare.tsx` takes the original log again, checks its hash and reads both logs the same way, from XKF1 or from MirageSITL's truth in a log written from a recording, into duration, greatest height, farthest from the start, path length and how far apart the two paths are once the interval's start is lined up with the start of the run's log. **Compare both in Analysis** opens them in the TASK-181 viewer on that alignment. Evidence (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 27.2, Node 24.22.1, Google Chrome 156, native ArduCopter `8ab6afde`, the quad X at 4×): `tests/log-to-experiment.test.ts` 16 passed: the inputs hold no path and do not change with the flight, hand-computed metrics and differences on tracks that go elsewhere, and a real log's track equal to pymavlink's XKF1 rows; `tests/browser/from-log-compare.spec.ts` 1 passed: `PSC_NE_POS_P` changed to 0.9 and read back in the experiment, a wrong log refused, the recording's greatest height under 0.5 m against this run's over 4 m after a 5 m take-off, and two curves on one value axis in the viewer starting together. Remaining: the repository's one reference log holds 0.4 s on the ground, so a real long flight has been compared only by unit fixtures, and fidelity metrics belong to TASK-184. A run does not replay the log's commands: it flies what the Run tab asks, as TASK-182 says.
- **Title & Description:** (S15) Run the created experiment with the original or changed ArduPilot parameters. The trajectory is recalculated, not forced to follow the recorded path. Compare the result with the original log in the viewer and with metrics.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-177, TASK-181, TASK-182.

### TASK-184 — Fidelity assessment per profile version and regime

- **Status:** TODO — registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md; baseline 384e974.
- **Title & Description:** (S15) Where matching data exists, compare attitude and rate response, overshoot and oscillation, altitude, climb and descent, trajectory, and measured propulsion and energy quantities, using the TASK-090 metrics, and show them with charts. MirageSITL sets no fidelity limits and issues no fidelity pass or fail: the user judges how far to trust the results (scope amendment of 2026-10-28). A comparison belongs to one profile version and its tested regimes; geometry, mass, motor or propeller changes require a new comparison. Missing data is shown as missing. ## Stage P1.10: Visualization and Existing Terrain

Scope step 10. Geometry-derived views and the existing terrain, without new providers or deferred effects (S17, S18).

### TASK-185 — Geometry-driven aircraft model, views and telemetry panels

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline 5312613; 2026-11-01 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: the Run tab's ArduPilot SITL backend shows the chosen profile's aircraft in 3D and flies it at the session's pose, with external and onboard views, the trajectory, the route when one exists, and flight and per-motor panels. Failure criterion: a drawn aircraft that differs from its profile, a view switch that leaks renderer resources, panels that do not show the session's values or update per physics step, or frame times outside A-06. Implemented (MacBook Air Apple M5, macOS 26.6, Node 24.22.1, Google Chrome 156.0.8078.80): `FlightView` draws the aircraft from the TASK-138 layout with the Aircraft tab's `Rotor` and `Strut` (now exported, sized by the shared `schemeDimensions` and `armMounts`), a blade per rotor turning with that motor's speed from power telemetry v1, the external chase camera and the onboard camera (TASK-067's `FpvCamera` with a plain 90° lens), a 120 s trajectory ring and a route overlay that is empty until TASK-172; `FlightTelemetry` and the TASK-146 `PowerPanel` refresh every 250 ms. The planned files `apps/web/src/scene/*` and `TelemetryPanels.tsx` became `apps/web/src/run/FlightView.tsx`, `flight-view-geometry.ts` and `FlightTelemetry.tsx`, and `FlightScene.tsx` stays the demo's scene, since the SITL backend has its own runtime. Defects found by the tests and fixed: the onboard camera rejected the canvas before layout (size 0) and in fractional CSS pixels; and the trail dropped about a third of its samples, because 0.3 − 0.2 is just below 0.1 in floating point. Evidence: `tests/flight-view-geometry.test.ts` 5 passed; in Google Chrome `tests/browser/flight-view.spec.ts` 2 passed (all five S19 configurations drawn as their layouts within 1e-6 m, 19.4 s; 20 external and onboard round trips and a tab switch with geometries, textures and programs unchanged, 1.0 min) and `tests/browser/external-sitl.spec.ts` passed with the view checks in the hover (1.1 min): the drawn aircraft within 0.5 m of 5 m, more than 100 trail samples, every motor above 1,000 rev/min and the pack current positive. Frame times (`tests/browser/flight-view-frames.spec.ts`, opt-in) in visible full-screen Chrome on the M5's built-in display (1710 x 1107 points, 2x, 60 Hz, Apple M5 10-core GPU) during a SITL hover: external view 3,601 intervals, median 16.665 ms, p99 17.670 ms, maximum 17.680 ms; onboard view 1,801 intervals, median 16.665 ms, p99 17.670 ms, maximum 17.675 ms. The p99 exceeded 17.665 ms by 0.005 ms with no late frame, as TASK-073's M5 run did on 2026-10-22; by the stakeholder's decision of 2026-11-01, A-06 on macOS (no displayed-frame timing source) reports the p99 and requires the median within 16.680 ms and no interval above 25 ms, which this run meets. Full gates: `npm run test:browser` 113 passed and 16 skipped (14.2 min); `npm test` 1184 passed and 1 skipped with 13 failures: 5 documentation-policy tests on the mirror hashes, refreshed after this review; the `drive-chain` golden hash that fails on main on this Mac; and 7 cases of `tests/run-controller.test.ts` and `tests/parameter-readback.test.ts` that timed out under the full suite's load and cascaded, which pass alone (101 of 101). Lint and typecheck 0; check:docs passes. Confirmation run of the amended test, 2026-11-01: passed in 2.6 min, external view 3,601 intervals (median 16.665 ms, p99 17.670 ms, maximum 17.680 ms), onboard view 1,800 intervals (median 16.665 ms, p99 17.670 ms, maximum 17.675 ms), window 1710 x 1107 filling the screen at 2x.
- **Title & Description:** (S17) Build the 3D aircraft from the profile: arms, motors, spin indicators and thrust axes. Provide external and onboard views; the onboard view reuses the TASK-067 camera pose without artistic or analog effects. Show the trajectory, the route and its points, and the flight and per-motor telemetry panels.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-067, TASK-141, TASK-153.
- **Touched Files:** `apps/web/src/run/FlightView.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/flight-view-geometry.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/FlightTelemetry.tsx`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/aircraft/AircraftScheme3D.tsx`, `apps/web/src/aircraft/scheme-geometry.ts`, `apps/web/src/style.css`, `tests/browser/flight-view.spec.ts`, `tests/browser/flight-view-frames.spec.ts`, `tests/browser/external-sitl.spec.ts`, `tests/flight-view-geometry.test.ts`, `tests/support/run-tab.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/decisions/phase1-acceptance.md`, `docs/ru/phase1-acceptance.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** The scene reads interpolated state only. Panels update at a decimated rate and never on each physics step.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** The rendered aircraft matches the profile in tests. Views switch without leaks. Panels show per-motor values from the session. Frame times meet the TASK-132 rendering criteria.

### TASK-186 — Terrain source selection, coverage and missing-terrain pause

- **Status:** IN_PROGRESS — Claude; baseline 36bd40b; 2026-11-05 (registered 2026-10-28 from PROJECT_SCOPE.md, baseline 384e974). Goal: a run's ground chosen at run time in the Weather and location tab, the synthetic pad or the local FABDEM terrain, with every inventory source's kind, layer, coverage, detail, license and readiness shown, and a real-terrain run paused where its terrain ends. Failure criterion: a decoded tile off the encoder's positions beyond one quantization step, a lattice post off the plane it was written from beyond the declared budget, a plane substituted for missing ground, a pause on another tick than the one that leaves the ground, an imagery source offered as ground, or a not-ready adapter shown as ready. Design, declared before implementation in SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES ("Terrain source selection: TASK-186"): source records from the TASK-131 inventory, a per-project terrain choice (the pad, or FABDEM with a radius of 256 m to 1500 m), a quantized-mesh-1.0 decoder, a lattice of 129 × 129 posts 4 m apart built on Main from the deepest tile level around the home, `terrain.chunks` to the physics Worker, a per-tick readiness check of the 8 m square around the vehicle that pauses the session on its tick, the synchronized pause with reason `terrain` (Resume refused: a run's terrain is loaded before it starts), the terrain's height as a ground elevation, and experiment payload schema 9 with `terrain`. Touched Files additions, made before implementation, because the decoder, the lattice, the pause and the record live there: `packages/terrain/src/quantized-mesh.ts`, `packages/terrain/src/run-terrain.ts`, `packages/terrain/package.json`, `data/terrain/inventory.json` (the `r2-region-cache` row), `packages/physics/src/session-v2.ts`, `packages/aircraft/src/experiment.ts`, `apps/web/src/physics.worker.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/session-controller.ts`, `apps/web/src/run/RunPanel.tsx`, `apps/web/src/sitl/sitl-run.ts`, `apps/web/src/weather/terrain-store.ts`, `apps/web/src/weather/local-terrain.ts`, `apps/web/src/weather/WeatherTab.tsx`, `apps/web/src/weather/LocationPicker.tsx`, `scripts/check-bet-runtime.mjs`, `tests/quantized-mesh.test.ts`, `tests/terrain-pause.test.ts`, `tests/run-controller.test.ts`, `tests/experiment-record.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/registry.json`, `tests/fixtures/terrain-qm-slope/generate.py`, `tests/fixtures/terrain-qm-slope/layer.json`, `tests/fixtures/terrain-qm-slope/terrain-manifest.json` (and its four level-14 tiles), `tests/browser/terrain-sitl.spec.ts`, `docs/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`, `docs/ru/SPECS_SIMULATION_ENGINES.md`. `apps/web/src/terrain-config.ts` stays the build-time configuration of the demo page's rendered tiles: a run's ground no longer depends on it, and the Run tab's 3D view still draws the pad. Implementation (2026-11-05): as designed; the spec lists the decoder's index handling, the lattice formula, the Worker messages and the record. Evidence: `tests/quantized-mesh.test.ts` 8 passed (the four fixture tiles against the independent quantized-mesh-encoder's positions and plane, brute-force triangles within 10⁻⁹ m, truncated tiles and a bad index refused, 32-bit indices after their alignment); `tests/terrain-coverage.test.ts` 8 passed (every inventory row's record, the pad ready anywhere offline, FABDEM ready only while served and covering the place, imagery never ground, Google and R2 needing keys or a deployment, a 256 m lattice on the plane within 0.0067 m with its seams accepted, a 1500 m lattice whose chunks outside the data are missing with the reason, missing tiles and homes, the WGS84 radii); `tests/terrain-pause.test.ts` 3 passed (a real session pauses on tick 58 when the ground goes missing after 57, at that step's position, again on the next tick after a resume, never without terrain, and runs on over a lattice that holds it); `tests/run-controller.test.ts` 130 passed, three new (the chunks to the physics and the terrain to the record; the pause with its place, Resume refused with the reason, a malformed notice ignored; a user pause takes the terrain reason, an accident keeps its own); `tests/experiment-record.test.ts` 32 passed, two new (schema 9 with its nine refusals and the schema 8 migration; the terrain as an input by its data and radius, not its lattice); `tests/fixtures.test.ts` 8 passed with the fixture registered; `tests/browser/terrain-source.spec.ts` 3 passed in Chromium (only the loopback server contacted, the pad the only ground, every other source's reason, axe without violations; with the fixture served, FABDEM ready inside it, a refused radius, the choice kept after a reload, the terrain height used as the ground elevation, and the browser's lattice equal in SHA-256 to Node's; a location outside it refused). Remaining on the Mac, required by the DoD: `tests/browser/terrain-sitl.spec.ts` (skips here) flies native ArduCopter on the fixture's terrain with a 256 m radius on a straight task 700 m north and declares the `terrain` pause between 504 and 504.02 m north, a refused Resume with ArduPilot's simulated time standing still, and the record's terrain; its second test hovers on the real FABDEM output when `MIRAGE_GEODATA_DIR` serves it (built with `scripts/geodata/` as the runbook says; set the variable before Playwright starts the dev server). Run it with `MIRAGE_BROWSER_GPU=hardware CHROMIUM_PATH="/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome" npx playwright test tests/browser/terrain-sitl.spec.ts`.
- **Title & Description:** (S17) Select at run time, not at build time, between the explicitly labeled synthetic level pad and the available real terrain. Imagery and elevation are distinguished, and coverage, source and detail are shown. An adapter that needs deployment, keys or data is shown as not ready. Missing terrain during a real-terrain run pauses it through the synchronized pause instead of substituting a plane. Automatic region downloads are excluded.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-065, TASK-131, TASK-148, TASK-156.
- **Touched Files:** `apps/web/src/terrain-config.ts`, `apps/web/src/weather/TerrainSource.tsx`, `packages/terrain/src/coverage.ts`, `tests/terrain-coverage.test.ts`, `tests/browser/terrain-source.spec.ts`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Terrain source record: kind, coverage, resolution, license and readiness from the TASK-131 inventory. A missing collision chunk pauses with reason `terrain`.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** The pad works offline. Real terrain loads from inventoried local data. A missing chunk causes a synchronized pause with a message. Unready adapters (Google tiles, R2) are marked not ready.

## Stage P1.11: Integrated Acceptance and Local Delivery

Scope step 11. Execute S19 on macOS and prepare local delivery, recording PASS, FAIL, BLOCKED and NOT_RUN separately.

### TASK-188 — Phase 1 acceptance matrix harness

- **Status:** DONE — Claude; baseline feb91c9 with TASK-166, TASK-106 and TASK-168; 2026-11-05. `node scripts/phase1/acceptance.mjs` checks all 51 cells of the matrix against their evidence by `scripts/phase1/acceptance-rules.ts`: the file must be there and hold the SHA-256 the cell cites, be a report of this configuration and workflow with this verdict from the A-01 machine with acceptance settings, come from this repository's history, and have been made by the sources it names as they stand now. Forged, missing and invalid evidence is refused and fails the matrix; a stale report leaves its cell to run; a failure stays a failure while its report stands, even if its sources change; missing evidence shows as NOT_RUN. Its exit codes follow the repository's other checks: 0 all PASS, 1 a failure or refused evidence, 2 cells left to run. `tests/phase1-acceptance.test.ts` holds those rules on hand-built matrices and reports. The runner is `node scripts/phase1/nominal-flight.mjs`, extended here with the parameter change, recording, reopening and refusal workflows of each configuration, so one flight per configuration gives nine workflows (run file schema 2). On first check the aggregator refused the 25 TASK-158 cells as stale, correctly, since their spec had changed. The rerun on the A-01 M5 in Chrome 156 with hardware GPU, Low Power Mode on, all five configurations at 800 Hz in real time (16 motors: 134.9 s simulated in 134.8 s of running wall time): all 45 cells PASS, with hold body-rate standard deviations at most 0.18 deg/s. The check now reports 45 PASS, 6 NOT_RUN, none refused. The 6 belong to other tasks: the one-hour recording (TASK-189), installation and offline (TASK-190), export and import (TASK-165's evidence), comparisons (TASK-181), parameter export (TASK-161's evidence). `scripts/validate-release.mjs` keeps the historical system matrix and is left alone. The reports cite the commit they ran on, with their uncommitted files hashed. No report enables a runtime mode: nothing in the application reads them.
- **Title & Description:** (S19) Build a hash-bound runner and aggregator over the configurations (quad, hexa and octo with one motor per arm; a paired or coaxial configuration; eight arms with two motors each) and the workflows: profile creation through the UI, output mapping, start, flight task, pause and resume, parameter changes, recording, reopening results, and explicit refusal of unsupported operations. Reuse the TASK-082 evidence conventions.
- **Dependencies:** TASK-132, TASK-134, TASK-158.
- **Touched Files:** `scripts/phase1/acceptance.mjs`, `scripts/phase1/acceptance-rules.ts`, `scripts/phase1/nominal-rules.ts`, `tests/browser/phase1-nominal.spec.ts`, `tests/integration/phase1-nominal.test.ts`, `docs/validation/phase1-matrix.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/quad.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/hexa.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/octo.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/x8.json`, `test-results/validation/phase1-nominal/octo-paired-16.json`, `tests/phase1-acceptance.test.ts`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/ru/CLAUDE.md`, `docs/decisions/phase1-acceptance.md`, `docs/ru/phase1-acceptance.md`, `docs/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ru/PROJECT_ROADMAP.md`.
- **Interfaces / Contracts:** Verdicts PASS, FAIL, BLOCKED_EXTERNAL, BLOCKED_DECISION and NOT_RUN, each with an evidence path and SHA-256. Missing evidence is NOT_RUN. Reports never enable a runtime mode.
- **DoD & Acceptance Criteria:** The matrix aggregates evidence with hashes. Missing evidence shows as NOT_RUN. Failures are preserved. A forged or stale report is rejected.
